\phi/\phi_{0}=p/q
, with
p
and
q
relatively prime integers,

\displaystyle\left[\hat{t}^{q}_{\mathbf{L}_{2}},\hat{t}_{\mathbf{L}_{1}}\right]=0

(15)

and

\displaystyle\left[\hat{t}_{\mathbf{L}_{1,2}},H^{\tau}_{BM}\left(p_{x},p_{y}-\frac{eB}{c}x\right)\right]=0.

(16)

The simultaneous eigenstates of the magnetic translation operators
\hat{t}_{\mathbf{L}_{1}}
and
\hat{t}^{q}_{\mathbf{L}_{2}}
can thus be used to produce a complete and orthonormal set of basis states for solving the
\mathbf{B}\neq 0
BM model. As we will see below, depending on whether they are conduction or heavy fermions there will be
p
states or
q
states per momentum point. It will be helpful to derive an identity for
\hat{t}^{s}_{\mathbf{L}_{1}}
which relates it to
\hat{T}^{s}_{\mathbf{L}_{1}}
.

Supplementary note 2.1
Landau gauge magnetic translation group identities

Using Eq.(
11
), we have

\displaystyle\hat{t}^{s}_{\mathbf{L}_{1}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(e^{2\pi i\frac{\phi}{\phi_{0}}\frac{y}{L_{2}}}\hat{T}_{\mathbf{L}_{1}}\right)^{s}=\left(e^{2\pi i\frac{\phi}{\phi_{0}}\frac{y}{L_{2}}}\hat{T}_{\mathbf{L}_{1}}\right)\ldots\left(e^{2\pi i\frac{\phi}{\phi_{0}}\frac{y}{L_{2}}}\hat{T}_{\mathbf{L}_{1}}\right)\left(e^{2\pi i\frac{\phi}{\phi_{0}}\frac{y}{L_{2}}}\hat{T}_{\mathbf{L}_{1}}\right)\left(e^{2\pi i\frac{\phi}{\phi_{0}}\frac{y}{L_{2}}}\hat{T}_{\mathbf{L}_{1}}\right)\left(e^{2\pi i\frac{\phi}{\phi_{0}}\frac{y}{L_{2}}}\hat{T}_{\mathbf{L}_{1}}\right)

(17)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(e^{2\pi i\frac{\phi}{\phi_{0}}\frac{y}{L_{2}}}\hat{T}_{\mathbf{L}_{1}}\right)\ldots\left(e^{2\pi i\frac{\phi}{\phi_{0}}\frac{y}{L_{2}}}\hat{T}_{\mathbf{L}_{1}}\right)\left(e^{2\pi i\frac{\phi}{\phi_{0}}\frac{y}{L_{2}}}\hat{T}_{\mathbf{L}_{1}}\right)\left(e^{2\pi i\frac{\phi}{\phi_{0}}\frac{2y-L_{1y}}{L_{2}}}\hat{T}^{2}_{\mathbf{L}_{1}}\right)

(18)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(e^{2\pi i\frac{\phi}{\phi_{0}}\frac{y}{L_{2}}}\hat{T}_{\mathbf{L}_{1}}\right)\ldots\left(e^{2\pi i\frac{\phi}{\phi_{0}}\frac{y}{L_{2}}}\hat{T}_{\mathbf{L}_{1}}\right)\left(e^{2\pi i\frac{\phi}{\phi_{0}}\frac{3y-2L_{1y}-L_{1y}}{L_{2}}}\hat{T}^{3}_{\mathbf{L}_{1}}\right)

(19)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(e^{2\pi i\frac{\phi}{\phi_{0}}\frac{y}{L_{2}}}\hat{T}_{\mathbf{L}_{1}}\right)\ldots\left(e^{2\pi i\frac{\phi}{\phi_{0}}\frac{y}{L_{2}}}\hat{T}_{\mathbf{L}_{1}}\right)\left(e^{2\pi i\frac{\phi}{\phi_{0}}\frac{4y-3L_{1y}-2L_{1y}-L_{1y}}{L_{2}}}\hat{T}^{4}_{\mathbf{L}_{1}}\right).

(20)

Now,
1+2+3+\ldots+(s-1)=\frac{1}{2}s(s-1)
. Therefore,

\displaystyle\hat{t}^{s}_{\mathbf{L}_{1}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle e^{-\pi is(s-1)\frac{p}{q}\frac{L_{1y}}{L_{2}}}e^{2\pi is\frac{p}{q}\frac{y}{L_{2}}}\hat{T}^{s}_{\mathbf{L}_{1}}

(21)

where
\phi/\phi_{0}=p/q
.
Although the above formula was derived for positive
s
, it also holds for negative
s
. This can be seen by noting that

\displaystyle\left(e^{-\pi is(s-1)\frac{\phi}{\phi_{0}}\frac{L_{1y}}{L_{2}}}e^{i\frac{eB}{\hbar c}sL_{1x}y}\hat{T}^{s}_{\mathbf{L}_{1}}\right)\left(e^{\pi is(-s-1)\frac{\phi}{\phi_{0}}\frac{L_{1y}}{L_{2}}}e^{-i\frac{eB}{\hbar c}sL_{1x}y}\hat{T}^{-s}_{\mathbf{L}_{1}}\right)=1.

(22)

So,
\hat{t}^{-1}_{\mathbf{L}_{1}}
denotes the inverse of
\hat{t}_{\mathbf{L}_{1}}
, and
\hat{t}^{-s}_{\mathbf{L}_{1}}=\left(\hat{t}^{-1}_{\mathbf{L}_{1}}\right)^{s}
.

Supplementary note 3
Finite
\mathbf{B}
basis for the
c
and
f
fermions

The
\mathbf{B}=0
basis for
f
fermions is constituted by two AA-stacking localised Wannier states per valley per spin
Song and Bernevig 2022
. We denote them as
W_{\mathbf{0},b\tau}(\mathbf{r})
, for
b\in\{1,2\}
and
\tau=\pm 1
. Using the recently introduced method
Wang and Vafek 2023
, we can construct the finite
\mathbf{B}
basis by first building hybrid Wannier states out of the zero field Wannier states followed by projection onto a representation of magnetic translation group(MTG). For given spin, the hybrid Wannier states read

\displaystyle w_{b\tau}(\mathbf{r},k_{2}\mathbf{g}_{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}e^{ik_{2}\mathbf{g}_{2}\cdot(n\mathbf{L}_{2})}\hat{t}^{n}_{\mathbf{L}_{2}}W_{\mathbf{0},b\tau}(\mathbf{r})=\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}e^{2\pi ik_{2}n}W_{0,b\tau}(\mathbf{r}-n\mathbf{L}_{2}).

(23)

where
k_{2}\in[0,1)
. These hybrid Wannier states although localized along the
x
direction are Bloch-like extended in the
y
direction and thus eigenstates of
\hat{t}_{\mathbf{L}_{2}}

\hat{t}_{\mathbf{L}_{2}}w_{b\tau}(\mathbf{r},k_{2}\mathbf{g}_{2})=w_{b\tau}(\mathbf{r}-\mathbf{L}_{2},k_{2}\mathbf{g}_{2})=e^{-2\pi ik_{2}}w_{b\tau}(\mathbf{r},k_{2}\mathbf{g}_{2}).

(24)

We next construct normalized eigenstates of MTG out of the hybrid Wannier states as

\displaystyle\eta_{b\tau k_{1}k_{2}}(\mathbf{r})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{\mathcal{N}}}\sum_{s\in\mathbb{Z}}e^{2\pi isk_{1}}\left(\hat{t}^{s}_{\mathbf{L}_{1}}w_{b\tau}(\mathbf{r},k_{2}\mathbf{g}_{2})\right),

(25)

where
k_{1}\in[0,1)
and normalization
\mathcal{N}=n_{tot}s_{tot}
. Here
s_{tot}
and
n_{tot}
denote the total count of
s
and
n
. Note that although these summations should be unbounded, we require these cutoffs for defining the normalization of our states and intermediate steps. They eventually cancel in the final formulas are are not physical. The normalisation is justified in Eqs.(
29
)-(
31
). The domains of
k_{1,2}
can be understood via noting that
\eta_{b\tau k_{1}k_{2}}
is periodic under
k_{1,2}\rightarrow k_{1,2}+1
. Under magnetic translations
\hat{t}_{\mathbf{L}_{1}}
and
\hat{t}^{q}_{\mathbf{L}_{2}}
, we then have

\displaystyle\hat{t}_{\mathbf{L}_{1}}\eta_{b\tau k_{1}k_{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle e^{-2\pi ik_{1}}\eta_{b\tau k_{1}k_{2}}

(26)

\displaystyle\hat{t}^{q}_{\mathbf{L}_{2}}\eta_{b\tau k_{1}k_{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle e^{-2\pi iqk_{2}}\eta_{b\tau k_{1}k_{2}}.

(27)

Thus states with
k_{2}
that differ by
1/q
have the same eigenvalue under
\hat{t}^{q}_{\mathbf{L}_{2}}
. This is because the
qL_{2}
translations break up the
k_{2}
domains into units of width
1/q
. To make it apparent from the quantum numbers, we relabel the states as

\displaystyle\eta_{b\tau kr^{\prime}}(\mathbf{r})=\eta_{b\tau k_{1}k_{2}+\frac{r^{\prime}}{q}}(\mathbf{r})\;\;\text{,}\;\;k=(k_{1},k_{2})\in[0,1)\otimes[0,1/q)\;\;\text{,}\;\;r^{\prime}=0,1\ldots q-1.

(28)

Thus
\eta_{b\tau kr^{\prime}}(\mathbf{r})
with different
k
quantum numbers are guaranteed to be orthogonal. Moreover we have
q
states for given
k
, corresponding to
r^{\prime}\in\{0\ldots q-1\}
for each
b\in\{1,2\}
, i.e. a total of
2q
heavy fermion states for given
k
. For same
k
and different
r^{\prime}
the overlaps for given valley are

\displaystyle\int d^{2}\mathbf{r}\eta^{*}_{b\tau kr^{\prime}_{1}}(\mathbf{r})\eta_{b^{\prime}\tau kr^{\prime}_{2}}(\mathbf{r})=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\mathcal{N}}\sum_{s_{1}s_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}}\sum_{n_{1}n_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}}e^{2\pi i(s_{2}-s_{1})k_{1}}e^{-2\pi i(k_{2}+\frac{r^{\prime}_{1}}{q})n_{1}}e^{2\pi i(k_{2}+\frac{r^{\prime}_{2}}{q})n_{2}}e^{\pi is_{1}(s_{1}-1)\frac{p}{q}\frac{L_{1y}}{L_{2}}}e^{-\pi is_{2}(s_{2}-1)\frac{p}{q}\frac{L_{1y}}{L_{2}}}

\displaystyle\times\int d^{2}\mathbf{r}e^{2\pi i(s_{2}-s_{1})\frac{p}{q}\frac{y}{L_{2}}}W^{*}_{\mathbf{0}b\tau}(\mathbf{r}-s_{1}\mathbf{L}_{1}-n_{1}\mathbf{L}_{2})W_{\mathbf{0}b^{\prime}\tau}(\mathbf{r}-s_{2}\mathbf{L}_{1}-n_{2}\mathbf{L}_{2}).

(29)

Now since the
\mathbf{B}=0
Wannier states are well localised and orthonormal, we have

\displaystyle\int d^{2}\mathbf{r}e^{2\pi i(s_{2}-s_{1})\frac{p}{q}\frac{y}{L_{2}}}W^{*}_{\mathbf{0}b\tau}(\mathbf{r}-s_{1}\mathbf{L}_{1}-n_{1}\mathbf{L}_{2})W_{\mathbf{0}b^{\prime}\tau}(\mathbf{r}-s_{2}\mathbf{L}_{1}-n_{2}\mathbf{L}_{2})\approx\delta_{bb^{\prime}}\delta_{s_{1}s_{2}}\delta_{n_{1}n_{2}}

(30)

and thus,

\displaystyle\int d^{2}\mathbf{r}\eta^{*}_{b\tau kr^{\prime}_{1}}(\mathbf{r})\eta_{b^{\prime}\tau kr^{\prime}_{2}}(\mathbf{r})\approx\delta_{bb^{\prime}}\frac{1}{\mathcal{N}}\sum_{s_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}}\sum_{n_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}}e^{2\pi i\frac{r^{\prime}_{2}-r^{\prime}_{1}}{q}n_{1}}=\delta_{r^{\prime}_{1}r^{\prime}_{2}}\delta_{bb^{\prime}}\frac{s_{tot}n_{tot}}{\mathcal{N}}=\delta_{r^{\prime}_{1}r^{\prime}_{2}}\delta_{bb^{\prime}}.

(31)

Henceforth we have a complete orthonormal basis for the heavy fermions in finite
\mathbf{B}
, constituted by
2q
states for given
k
, i.e. 2 states per moiré unit cell per valley per spin, which clearly implies that these are total Chern 0 states.

Now let us discuss the basis for
c
fermions. Remember that at
\mathbf{B}=0
, the basis for
c
fermions is constituted by four
\mathbf{k}\cdot\mathbf{p}
Bloch states at the
\Gamma
point in the moiré Brillouin Zone(mBZ), per valley per spin. We denote them by
\tilde{\Psi}_{\Gamma a\tau}
for
a\in\{1\ldots 4\}
and
\tau=\pm 1
.

We promote the
\mathbf{B}=0

c
fermion basis to finite
\mathbf{B}
using a result obtained when the
\mathbf{k}\cdot\mathbf{p}
method is extended to finite
\mathbf{B}
Luttinger and Kohn 1955
, which prescribes the ansatz for finite field
\mathbf{k}\cdot\mathbf{p}
states to be Landau level(LL) coefficients on top of the zero field
\mathbf{k}\cdot\mathbf{p}
states. Henceforth, we define the finite
\mathbf{B}
basis for
\mathbf{k}\cdot\mathbf{p}
Bloch states as an expansion over product of LLs and the

\mathbf{B}=0

\mathbf{k}\cdot\mathbf{p}
Bloch states at the
\Gamma
point in mBZ. However in order to use the same quantum numbers label as finite
\mathbf{B}

f
fermion basis we first project the Landau gauge LL onto the representation of MTG as

\displaystyle\chi_{k_{1}k_{2}m}(\mathbf{r})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{\ell L_{2}}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{\mathcal{N}}}\sum_{s\in\mathbb{Z}}e^{2\pi isk_{1}}\left(\hat{t}^{s}_{\mathbf{L}_{1}}e^{2\pi ik_{2}\frac{y}{L_{2}}}\varphi_{m}\left(x-k_{2}\frac{2\pi\ell^{2}}{L_{2}}\right)\right)

(32)

where
k_{1}\in[0,1)
and unlike before,
k_{2}\in[0,p/q)
as justified in Eqs.(
35
)-(
38
). The functions
\varphi_{m}
are
m
th 1D harmonic oscillator states that determine the Landau gauge Landau levels

\displaystyle\varphi_{m}(x)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\pi^{\frac{1}{4}}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{2^{m}m!}}e^{-x^{2}/2\ell^{2}}H_{m}(x/\ell)

(33)

where
H_{m}
is the Hermite polynomial and
\ell^{2}=\hbar c/(eB)
. The normalization of the LL MTG states is explained in Eqs.(
45
)-(
50
).
The domains for the quantum numbers
k_{1,2}
can be understood by noting that
\chi_{k_{1}k_{2}m}
is periodic under
k_{1}\rightarrow k_{1}+1
and up to a phase under
k_{2}\rightarrow k_{2}+p/q
as shown below

\displaystyle\chi_{k_{1}k_{2}+\frac{p}{q}m}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\ell L_{2}}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{\mathcal{N}}}\sum_{s\in\mathbb{Z}}e^{2\pi isk_{1}}\left(\hat{t}^{s}_{\mathbf{L}_{1}}e^{2\pi i(k_{2}+\frac{p}{q})\frac{y}{L_{2}}}\varphi_{m}\left(x-(k_{2}+\frac{p}{q})\frac{2\pi\ell^{2}}{L_{2}}\right)\right),

(34)

now using the fact,
\frac{2\pi\ell^{2}}{L_{2}}\frac{p}{q}=L_{1x}
we have

\displaystyle\chi_{k_{1}k_{2}+\frac{p}{q}m}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{\ell L_{2}}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{\mathcal{N}}}\sum_{s\in\mathbb{Z}}e^{2\pi isk_{1}}\left(\hat{t}^{s}_{\mathbf{L}_{1}}e^{2\pi i(k_{2}+\frac{p}{q})\frac{y}{L_{2}}}\varphi_{m}\left(x-L_{1x}-k_{2}\frac{2\pi\ell^{2}}{L_{2}}\right)\right)

(35)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle e^{2\pi ik_{2}\frac{L_{1y}}{L_{2}}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{\ell L_{2}}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{\mathcal{N}}}\sum_{s\in\mathbb{Z}}e^{2\pi isk_{1}}\left(\hat{t}^{s}_{\mathbf{L}_{1}}e^{2\pi i\frac{p}{q}\frac{y}{L_{2}}}\hat{T}_{\mathbf{L}_{1}}\left(e^{2\pi ik_{2}\frac{y}{L_{2}}}\varphi_{m}\left(x-k_{2}\frac{2\pi\ell^{2}}{L_{2}}\right)\right)\right)

(36)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle e^{2\pi ik_{2}\frac{L_{1y}}{L_{2}}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{\ell L_{2}}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{\mathcal{N}}}\sum_{s\in\mathbb{Z}}e^{2\pi isk_{1}}\left(\hat{t}^{s+1}_{\mathbf{L}_{1}}e^{2\pi ik_{2}\frac{y}{L_{2}}}\varphi_{m}\left(x-k_{2}\frac{2\pi\ell^{2}}{L_{2}}\right)\right)

(37)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle e^{-2\pi i\left(k_{1}-k_{2}\frac{L_{1y}}{L_{2}}\right)}\chi_{k_{1}k_{2}m}

(38)

The domain for
k_{2}
can also be understood in the context of the fact that
\chi_{k_{1}k_{2}m}
are essentially LLs and thus behave as Chern number
+1
states in
\mathbf{B}\neq 0
. In order to illustrate it, let us discuss the total number of available states for
\chi_{k_{1}k_{2}m}
.
We start by noting that under magnetic translations
\hat{t}_{\mathbf{L}_{1}}
and
\hat{t}^{q}_{\mathbf{L}_{2}}

\displaystyle\hat{t}_{\mathbf{L}_{1}}\chi_{k_{1}k_{2}m}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle e^{-2\pi ik_{1}}\chi_{k_{1}k_{2}m},

(39)

\displaystyle\hat{t}^{q}_{\mathbf{L}_{2}}\chi_{k_{1}k_{2}m}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle e^{-2\pi iqk_{2}}\chi_{k_{1}k_{2}m}.

(40)

If we have a system size
N_{1}\mathbf{L}_{1}
and
qN_{2}\mathbf{L}_{2}
, then

\displaystyle\hat{t}^{N_{1}}_{\mathbf{L}_{1}}\chi_{k_{1}k_{2}m}=\chi_{k_{1}k_{2}m}

(41)

\displaystyle\Rightarrow

\displaystyle e^{-2\pi ik_{1}N_{1}}=1;\;\Rightarrow\;\;k_{1}=0,\frac{1}{N_{1}},\frac{2}{N_{1}},\ldots,1-\frac{1}{N_{1}}

(42)

Similarly,

\displaystyle\hat{t}^{qN_{2}}_{\mathbf{L}_{2}}\chi_{k_{1}k_{2}m}=\chi_{k_{1}k_{2}m}

(43)

\displaystyle\Rightarrow

\displaystyle e^{-2\pi ik_{2}qN_{2}}=1;\;\Rightarrow\;\;k_{2}=0,\frac{1}{qN_{2}},\frac{2}{qN_{2}},\ldots,\frac{p}{q}-\frac{1}{qN_{2}}

(44)

So, the total number of states is
N_{1}\frac{p}{q}qN_{2}=\frac{\Phi}{\Phi_{0}}N_{1}qN_{2}=\frac{\Phi}{\Phi_{0}}N_{1}qN_{2}\frac{L_{1x}L_{2}}{A_{uc}}=\frac{\Phi}{\Phi_{0}}\frac{A_{tot}}{A_{uc}}=\frac{B}{\Phi_{0}}A_{tot}=\frac{\Phi_{tot}}{\Phi_{0}}
.
Here
A_{uc}=\hat{z}\cdot(\mathbf{L}_{1}\times\mathbf{L}_{2})
denotes the area of moiré unit cell. Thus
qA_{uc}
is the area of magnetic unit cell and total area
A_{tot}=N_{1}N_{2}qA_{uc}
. This is a well known result for the degeneracy of the Landau level. Let us now discuss the orthonormalization for the LL MTG eigenstates. The overlaps amidst the MTG LL are given as:

\displaystyle\int d^{2}\mathbf{r}\chi^{*}_{k_{1}k_{2}m}(\mathbf{r})\chi_{k_{1}^{\prime}k_{2}^{\prime}m^{\prime}}(\mathbf{r})=

(45)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\ell L_{2}\mathcal{N}}\sum_{s\in\mathbb{Z}}\sum_{s^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}}e^{-2\pi is\left(k_{1}-\left(k_{2}-\frac{p}{q}\right)\frac{L_{1y}}{L_{2}}\right)}e^{i\pi s(s+1)\frac{p}{q}\frac{L_{1y}}{L_{2}}}e^{2\pi is^{\prime}\left(k^{\prime}_{1}-\left(k^{\prime}_{2}-\frac{p}{q}\right)\frac{L_{1y}}{L_{2}}\right)}e^{-i\pi s^{\prime}(s^{\prime}+1)\frac{p}{q}\frac{L_{1y}}{L_{2}}}

\displaystyle\int dye^{-iy\left(s\frac{p}{q}+k_{2}\right)\frac{2\pi}{L_{2}}}e^{iy\left(s^{\prime}\frac{p}{q}+k^{\prime}_{2}\right)\frac{2\pi}{L_{2}}}\int dx\varphi_{m}\left(x-\left(s\frac{p}{q}+k_{2}\right)\frac{2\pi}{L_{2}}\ell^{2}\right)\varphi_{m^{\prime}}\left(x-\left(s^{\prime}\frac{p}{q}+k^{\prime}_{2}\right)\frac{2\pi}{L_{2}}\ell^{2}\right)

(46)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\ell L_{2}\mathcal{N}}\sum_{s\in\mathbb{Z}}\sum_{s^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}}e^{-2\pi is\left(k_{1}-\left(k_{2}-\frac{p}{q}\right)\frac{L_{1y}}{L_{2}}\right)}e^{i\pi s(s+1)\frac{p}{q}\frac{L_{1y}}{L_{2}}}e^{2\pi is^{\prime}\left(k^{\prime}_{1}-\left(k^{\prime}_{2}-\frac{p}{q}\right)\frac{L_{1y}}{L_{2}}\right)}e^{-i\pi s^{\prime}(s^{\prime}+1)\frac{p}{q}\frac{L_{1y}}{L_{2}}}

\displaystyle n_{tot}L_{2}\delta_{s,s^{\prime}}\delta_{k_{2},k_{2}^{\prime}}\ell\delta_{m,m^{\prime}}

(47)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{L_{2}\mathcal{N}}\sum_{s\in\mathbb{Z}}e^{2\pi is(k_{1}-k_{1}^{\prime})}n_{tot}L_{2}\delta_{k_{2},k_{2}^{\prime}}\delta_{m,m^{\prime}}

(48)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{L_{2}\mathcal{N}}s_{tot}\delta_{k_{1},k_{1}^{\prime}}n_{tot}L_{2}\delta_{k_{2},k_{2}^{\prime}}\delta_{m,m^{\prime}}

(49)

\displaystyle=\delta_{k_{1},k_{1}^{\prime}}\delta_{k_{2},k_{2}^{\prime}}\delta_{m,m^{\prime}}.

(50)

where in Eq.(
46
)-(
47
), we have used the fact that
y
-integral implies
k_{2}-k^{\prime}_{2}=(s^{\prime}-s)\frac{p}{q}
. Now since
k_{2},k^{\prime}_{2}\in[0,\frac{p}{q})
, the integral evaluates to
n_{tot}L_{2}\delta_{k_{2},k_{2}^{\prime}}\delta_{s,s^{\prime}}
. Moreover each harmonic oscillator function in the
x
-integral can be shifted by the same amount since the arguments coincide. Therefore the x-integral evaluates to
\ell\delta_{mm^{\prime}}
. Eventually in Eq.(
48
)-(
49
), the
s
summation implies
k_{1}-k^{\prime}_{1}=\text{integer}
. But since
k_{1},k^{\prime}_{1}\in[0,1)
, we must have
k_{1}=k^{\prime}_{1}
and the summation evaluates to
s_{tot}\delta_{k_{1},k^{\prime}_{1}}
. Therefore
\chi
’s are orthogonal and normalized. To have the same quantum number label as that of
\mathbf{B}\neq 0

f
fermion states, we relabel the MTG LLs as

\displaystyle\chi_{krm}(\mathbf{r})=\chi_{k_{1}k_{2}+\frac{r}{q}m}(\mathbf{r})\;\;\text{,}\;\;k=(k_{1},k_{2})\in[0,1)\otimes[0,1/q)\;\;\text{,}\;\;r=0,1\ldots p-1.

(51)

Thus for given
k
, we have
p
states, corresponding to
r\in\{0\ldots p-1\}
, i.e.
\frac{p}{q}
states per moiré unit cell per valley per spin as expected. Finally we have the finite
\mathbf{B}
c fermion basis states as
\Psi_{a\tau}\chi_{krm}
, where

\Psi_{a\tau}=\sqrt{NA_{uc}}\tilde{\Psi}_{\Gamma a\tau}

(52)

with
N
being the total number of moiré unit cells. The
\sqrt{NA_{uc}}
factor is required due to normalization of
\chi
. Note that since
\Psi_{a\tau}
is a Bloch state at
\Gamma
,

\displaystyle\hat{T}_{\mathbf{L}_{i}}\Psi_{a\tau}(\mathbf{r})=\Psi_{a\tau}(\mathbf{r})

(53)

\displaystyle\implies\hat{t}_{\mathbf{L}_{i}}\left(\Psi_{a\tau}(\mathbf{r})\chi_{krm}\right)(\mathbf{r})=\Psi_{a\tau}\hat{t}_{\mathbf{L}_{i}}\chi_{krm}(\mathbf{r}),

(54)

where
i\in\{1,2\}
. Thus
\Psi_{a\tau}\chi_{krm}
are MTG eigenstates with same eigenvalue as that of
\chi_{krm}
. Now let us discuss the orthonormalization for these states. Since in this work we focus on
\frac{1}{q}
sequence, for the following discussion on orthonormality we set index
r
in
\chi_{krm}
to zero and work with
\chi_{k0m}
. The Bloch periodicity for
\Psi_{\Gamma a\tau}(\mathbf{r})
allows us to expand it as

\displaystyle\Psi_{\Gamma a\tau}(\mathbf{r})=\frac{1}{\sqrt{NA_{uc}}}\sum_{\mathbf{G}}e^{i\mathbf{G}\cdot\mathbf{r}}C_{a\tau\mathbf{G}},

(55)

where
\mathbf{G}=n_{1}\mathbf{g}_{1}+n_{2}\mathbf{g}_{2}
,
n_{1,2}\in\mathbb{Z}
, denote moiré reciprocal lattice vectors and

\displaystyle C_{a\tau\mathbf{G}}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{NA_{uc}}}\int d^{2}\mathbf{r}e^{-i\mathbf{G}\cdot\mathbf{r}}\tilde{\Psi}_{\Gamma a\tau}(\mathbf{r})

(56)

can be found in
Călugăru et al. 2023
. Now using Eq.(
52
), the overlap matrix can be given as

\displaystyle O^{(q,m_{max})}_{[m^{\prime},a^{\prime}][m,a]}=\int d^{2}\mathbf{r}\left(\Psi_{a^{\prime}\tau}\chi_{k^{\prime}0m^{\prime}}\right)^{\ast}\Psi_{a\tau}\chi_{k0m}=\sum_{\mathbf{G}_{1},\mathbf{G}_{2}}C^{\ast}_{a^{\prime}\tau\mathbf{G}_{1}}C_{a\tau\mathbf{G}_{2}}\int d^{2}\mathbf{r}e^{i(\mathbf{G}_{2}-\mathbf{G}_{1})\cdot\mathbf{r}}\chi^{\ast}_{k^{\prime}0m^{\prime}}\chi_{k0m},

(57)

where
1/q
is the flux per unit cell per flux quantum
\Phi_{0}
and
m_{max}
denotes the upper cutoff on the indices
m
and
m^{\prime}
. The integral can be computed as:

\displaystyle\int d^{2}\mathbf{r}e^{i\mathbf{g}\cdot\mathbf{r}}\chi^{*}_{k^{\prime}0m^{\prime}}(\mathbf{r})\chi_{k0m}(\mathbf{r})

(58)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\ell L_{2}\mathcal{N}}\sum_{s,s^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}}e^{2\pi i(k_{1}s-k^{\prime}_{1}s^{\prime})}\int d^{2}\mathbf{r}(e^{2\pi ik_{2}^{\prime}\frac{y}{L_{2}}}\varphi_{m^{\prime}}(x-k^{\prime}_{2}\frac{2\pi\ell^{2}}{L_{2}}))^{*}t^{-s^{\prime}}_{L_{1}}e^{i\mathbf{g}\cdot\mathbf{r}}t^{s}_{L_{1}}(e^{2\pi ik_{2}\frac{y}{L_{2}}}\varphi_{m}(x-k_{2}\frac{2\pi\ell^{2}}{L_{2}}))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\delta_{k_{1},k^{\prime}_{1}}\frac{s_{tot}}{\ell L_{2}\mathcal{N}}\sum_{s\in\mathbb{Z}}e^{2\pi isk_{1}}\int d^{2}\mathbf{r}(e^{2\pi ik_{2}^{\prime}\frac{y}{L_{2}}}\varphi_{m^{\prime}}(x-k^{\prime}_{2}\frac{2\pi\ell^{2}}{L_{2}}))^{*}e^{i\mathbf{g}\cdot\mathbf{r}}t^{s}_{L_{1}}(e^{2\pi ik_{2}\frac{y}{L_{2}}}\varphi_{m}(x-k_{2}\frac{2\pi\ell^{2}}{L_{2}}))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\delta_{k_{1},k^{\prime}_{1}}\sum_{s\in\mathbb{Z}}\delta_{k_{2}+\frac{s}{q},k_{2}^{\prime}-\frac{L_{2}g_{y}}{2\pi}}e^{2\pi is(k_{1}-\frac{k_{2}}{2})}e^{-i\pi s(s-1)\frac{1}{2q}}\frac{1}{\ell}\int dx\varphi_{m^{\prime}}(x-k^{\prime}_{2}\frac{2\pi\ell^{2}}{L_{2}})e^{ig_{x}x}\varphi_{m}(x-(k_{2}+\frac{s}{q})\frac{2\pi\ell^{2}}{L_{2}})

The delta function constraint implies

\displaystyle\delta_{k_{2}+\frac{s}{q},k_{2}^{\prime}-\frac{L_{2}g_{y}}{2\pi}}=\delta_{k_{2},k^{\prime}_{2}}\delta_{s,-qg_{2}},

(59)

where
g_{2}=g_{y}\frac{L_{2}}{2\pi}\in\mathbb{Z}
. Note that getting
\delta_{k_{1(2)},k_{1^{\prime}(2^{\prime})}}
from the overlap is a direct consequence of Eqs.(
39
)-(
40
) for
\frac{1}{q}
sequence. The above integral can be evaluated as

Supplementary Figure 1:
Figures showing the diagonal values of Overlap matrix
O^{(q,m_{max})}_{[m^{\prime},a^{\prime}][m,a]}
and frobenius norm of
O^{(q,m_{max})}-\mathbb{I}_{m_{max}}

\forall

m_{max}=0\ldots q-1
at
w_{0}/w_{1}=0.8
for (a,b)
q=30
, (c,d)
q=50
, (e,f)
q=60
and (g,h)
q=70
respectively. For higher values of
q
, the value of
m_{max}
for which diagonal values start deviating from 1 have been (i) plotted using
\bullet
.

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\ell}\int dx\varphi_{m^{\prime}}(x-k_{2}\frac{2\pi\ell^{2}}{L_{2}})e^{ig_{x}x}\varphi_{m}(x-(k_{2}-g_{2})\frac{2\pi\ell^{2}}{L_{2}})

(60)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\ell}e^{ig_{x}k_{2}\frac{2\pi\ell^{2}}{L_{2}}}\int dx\varphi_{m^{\prime}}(x)e^{ig_{x}x}\varphi_{m}(x+g_{2}\frac{2\pi\ell^{2}}{L_{2}})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\ell}e^{ig_{x}k_{2}\frac{2\pi\ell^{2}}{L_{2}}}\int dx\varphi_{m^{\prime}}(x)e^{ig_{x}x}e^{ig_{y}\ell^{2}p_{x}}\varphi_{m}(x)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\ell}e^{ig_{x}k_{2}\frac{2\pi\ell^{2}}{L_{2}}}e^{-\frac{i}{2}g_{x}g_{y}\ell^{2}}\int dx\varphi_{m^{\prime}}(x)e^{c_{+}a^{\dagger}+c_{-}a}\varphi_{m}(x)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle e^{ig_{x}k_{2}\frac{2\pi\ell^{2}}{L_{2}}}e^{-\frac{i}{2}g_{x}g_{y}\ell^{2}}\bra{m'}e^{c_{+}a^{\dagger}+c_{-}a}\ket{m},

where
p_{x}
is momentum operator along
x
direction later changed to harmonic oscillator(h.o.) basis:
x=\frac{\ell}{\sqrt{2}}(a+a^{\dagger})
, and
p_{x}=\frac{i}{\sqrt{2\ell}}(a^{\dagger}-a)
. The h.o. kets are defined as
\langle m\mid x\rangle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\ell}}\varphi_{m}(x)
, such that
\langle m\mid n\rangle=\delta_{m,n}
. Eventually we use the BCH formula,
e^{X}e^{Y}=e^{X+Y+\frac{1}{2}[X,Y]+\ldots}
, and

\displaystyle c_{\pm}=\frac{i\ell}{\sqrt{2}}(g_{x}\pm ig_{y}).

(61)

We also know the identity

\displaystyle\bra{m'}e^{c_{+}a^{\dagger}+c_{-}a}\ket{m}=\begin{cases}e^{\frac{1}{2}c_{+}c_{-}}\sqrt{\frac{m!}{n!}}(c_{+})^{n-m}L^{n-m}_{m}(-c_{+}c_{-})&\text{for}\penalty\ \penalty\ n\geq m,\\
e^{\frac{1}{2}c_{+}c_{-}}\sqrt{\frac{n!}{m!}}(c_{-})^{m-n}L^{m-n}_{n}(-c_{+}c_{-})&\text{for}\penalty\ \penalty\ n<m,\end{cases}

(62)

where
L^{m-n}_{n}(x)
is the associated Laguerre polynomial,

\displaystyle L^{m}_{N}(x)=\sum_{k=0}^{N}\frac{(N+m)!}{(N-k)!(m+k)!k!}(-x)^{k}.

(63)

As shown in Eq.(
62
), the overlaps are exponentially small in
(c_{+}c_{-})
, i.e.
(g\ell)^{2}
, and thus negligible. However, beyond an upper-bound on
m
, the orthonormality begins to fail. Supplementary Fig.(
1
-a)-(
1
-h) show that the diagonal values of overlap matrix in Eq.(
57
) start deviating from
1
beyond some upper-bound on
m
exactly when the Frobenius norm of
O-\mathbb{I}
, with
\mathbb{I}
being identity matrix of dimension same as that of
O
, starts deviating from
0
. Thus the deviation of diagonal values from
1
is a good indicative of the breaking up of orthonormality. In this work we choose
m_{max}=\lceil\frac{q-3}{2}\rceil
, which is well below the upper-bound as shown in Supplementary Fig.(
1
-i).

Supplementary note 4
Computation of Matrix elements

Supplementary note 4.1
c-c Coupling

The
c-c
coupling is given as

\displaystyle H^{\tau}_{cc}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{k\in[0,1)\otimes[0,\frac{1}{q})}\sum_{a,a^{\prime}=1}^{4}\sum_{m=0}^{m_{a,\tau}}\sum_{m^{\prime}=0}^{m_{a^{\prime},\tau}}\sum_{r,\tilde{r}=0}^{p-1}\tilde{h}^{\tau}_{[amr],[a^{\prime}m^{\prime}\tilde{r}]}(k)c^{\dagger}_{a\tau krm}c_{a^{\prime}\tau k\tilde{r}m^{\prime}},

(64)

where

\displaystyle\tilde{h}^{\tau}_{[amr],[a^{\prime}m^{\prime}r^{\prime}]}(k_{1},k_{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int d^{2}\mathbf{r}\Psi^{*}_{a\tau}(\mathbf{r})\chi^{*}_{krm}(\mathbf{r})H^{\tau}_{BM}\left(p_{x},p_{y}-\frac{eB}{c}x\right)\Psi_{a^{\prime}\tau}(\mathbf{r})\chi_{kr^{\prime}m^{\prime}}(\mathbf{r}).

(65)

Since the BM Hamiltonian is linear in
\mathbf{p}-\frac{e}{c}\mathbf{A}
, we can write it as

\displaystyle H^{\tau}_{BM}\left(\mathbf{p}-\frac{e}{c}\mathbf{A}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}^{\tau}_{\mu}\left(p_{\mu}-\frac{e}{c}A_{\mu}(\mathbf{r})\right)+\mathcal{T}^{\tau}(\mathbf{r})

(66)

where
\mathcal{M}^{+1}_{\mu}=v_{F}\mathbb{I}_{2}\otimes\mathbf{\sigma}
,
\mathcal{M}^{-1}_{\mu}=-v_{F}\mathbb{I}_{2}\otimes\mathbf{\bar{\sigma}}
with
\mathbf{\sigma}=(\sigma_{x},\sigma_{y})
and
\bar{\sigma}=(\sigma_{x},-\sigma_{y})
.
\mathcal{T}^{\tau}
denotes the remaining factors in BM Hamiltonian. So,

\displaystyle H^{\tau}_{BM}\left(\mathbf{p}-\frac{e}{c}\mathbf{A}\right)\Psi_{a^{\prime}\tau}(\mathbf{r})\chi_{kr^{\prime}m^{\prime}}(\mathbf{r})=\mathcal{M}^{\tau}_{\mu}\left(p_{\mu}-\frac{e}{c}A_{\mu}(\mathbf{r})\right)\Psi_{a^{\prime}}(\mathbf{r})\chi_{k_{1},k_{2}+\frac{r^{\prime}}{q},m^{\prime}}(\mathbf{r})+\mathcal{T}^{\tau}(\mathbf{r})\Psi_{a^{\prime}\tau}(\mathbf{r})\chi_{kr^{\prime}m^{\prime}}(\mathbf{r})

(67)

\displaystyle=\left(\mathcal{M}^{\tau}_{\mu}p_{\mu}\Psi_{a^{\prime}\tau}(\mathbf{r})+\mathcal{T}^{\tau}(\mathbf{r})\Psi_{a^{\prime}\tau}(\mathbf{r})\right)\chi_{kr^{\prime}m^{\prime}}(\mathbf{r})+\mathcal{M}^{\tau}_{\mu}\Psi_{a^{\prime}\tau}(\mathbf{r})\left(\left(p_{\mu}-\frac{e}{c}A_{\mu}(\mathbf{r})\right)\chi_{kr^{\prime}m^{\prime}}(\mathbf{r})\right)

(68)

\displaystyle=\varepsilon^{\tau}_{a^{\prime\prime}a^{\prime}}\Psi_{a^{\prime\prime}\tau}(\mathbf{r})\chi_{kr^{\prime}m^{\prime}}(\mathbf{r})+\mathcal{M}^{\tau}_{\mu}\Psi_{a^{\prime}\tau}(\mathbf{r})\left(p_{\mu}-\frac{e}{c}A_{\mu}(\mathbf{r})\right)\chi_{krm^{\prime}}(\mathbf{r}),

(69)

where the matrix
\varepsilon^{\tau}
is given in Eq.(
Supplementary note 4.1
) is
\mathbf{k}
independent because
\Psi_{a\tau}
is defined at
\Gamma
. So,

\displaystyle\tilde{h}^{\tau}_{[amr],[a^{\prime}m^{\prime}r^{\prime}]}(k_{1},k_{2})=\int d^{2}\mathbf{r}\Psi^{*}_{a\tau}(\mathbf{r})\varepsilon^{\tau}_{a^{\prime\prime}a^{\prime}}\Psi_{a^{\prime\prime}\tau}(\mathbf{r})\chi^{*}_{krm}(\mathbf{r})\chi_{kr^{\prime}m^{\prime}}(\mathbf{r})

(70)

\displaystyle+\int d^{2}\mathbf{r}\Psi^{*}_{a\tau}(\mathbf{r})\mathcal{M}^{\tau}_{\mu}\Psi_{a^{\prime}\tau}(\mathbf{r})\chi^{*}_{krm}(\mathbf{r})\left(p_{\mu}-\frac{e}{c}A_{\mu}(\mathbf{r})\right)\chi_{kr^{\prime}m^{\prime}}(\mathbf{r})

(71)

Since the factors
\varepsilon^{\tau}_{a^{\prime\prime}a^{\prime}}\Psi^{*}_{a\tau}(\mathbf{r})\Psi_{a^{\prime\prime}\tau}(\mathbf{r})
and
\Psi^{*}_{a\tau}(\mathbf{r})\mathcal{M}^{\tau}_{\mu}\Psi_{a^{\prime}\tau}(\mathbf{r})
are periodic with respect to primitive moiré lattice vectors
\mathbf{L}_{1}
and
\mathbf{L}_{2}
, we can perform a Fourier expansion as

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{\tau}_{a^{\prime\prime}a^{\prime}}\Psi^{*}_{a\tau}(\mathbf{r})\Psi_{a^{\prime\prime}\tau}(\mathbf{r})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{\tau}_{a^{\prime\prime}a^{\prime}}\sum_{\mathbf{g}}e^{i\mathbf{g}\cdot\mathbf{r}}\left(\frac{1}{A_{tot}}\int d^{2}\mathbf{r}^{\prime}e^{-i\mathbf{g}\cdot\mathbf{r}^{\prime}}\Psi^{*}_{a\tau}(\mathbf{r}^{\prime})\Psi_{a^{\prime\prime}\tau}(\mathbf{r}^{\prime})\right)

(72)

\displaystyle\Psi^{*}_{a\tau}(\mathbf{r})\mathcal{M}^{\tau}_{\mu}\Psi_{a^{\prime}\tau}(\mathbf{r})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\mathbf{g}}e^{i\mathbf{g}\cdot\mathbf{r}}\left(\frac{1}{A_{tot}}\int d^{2}\mathbf{r}^{\prime}e^{-i\mathbf{g}\cdot\mathbf{r}^{\prime}}\Psi^{*}_{a\tau}(\mathbf{r}^{\prime})\mathcal{M}^{\tau}_{\mu}\Psi_{a^{\prime}\tau}(\mathbf{r}^{\prime})\right)

(73)

As shown in previous section, at small
B
the overlaps of the MTG LL with
e^{i\mathbf{g}\cdot\mathbf{r}}
are suppressed by a factor of
\exp\left(-\mathbf{g}^{2}\ell^{2}/4\right)
. So at small enough
B
(large enough
\ell
) the overlaps with non-zero
\mathbf{g}
components are negligible. Keeping only the
\mathbf{g}=0
Fourier component then gives

\displaystyle\tilde{h^{\tau}}_{[amr],[a^{\prime}m^{\prime}r^{\prime}]}(k_{1},k_{2})\approx\left(\frac{1}{A_{tot}}\int d^{2}\mathbf{r}\Psi^{*}_{a\tau}(\mathbf{r})\varepsilon^{\tau}_{a^{\prime\prime}a^{\prime}}\Psi_{a^{\prime\prime}\tau}(\mathbf{r})\right)\left(\int d^{2}\mathbf{r}\chi^{*}_{km}(\mathbf{r})\chi_{kr^{\prime}m^{\prime}}(\mathbf{r})\right)+

\displaystyle\left(\frac{1}{A_{tot}}\int d^{2}\mathbf{r}\Psi^{*}_{a\tau}(\mathbf{r})\mathcal{M^{\tau}}_{\mu}\Psi_{a^{\prime}\tau}(\mathbf{r})\right)\left(\int d^{2}\mathbf{r}\chi^{*}_{krm}(\mathbf{r})\left(p_{\mu}-\frac{e}{c}A_{\mu}(\mathbf{r})\right)\chi_{kr^{\prime}m^{\prime}}(\mathbf{r})\right)+\mathcal{O}\left(\exp\left(-\mathbf{g}^{2}\ell^{2}/4\right)\right)

(74)

\displaystyle=\varepsilon^{\tau}_{aa^{\prime}}\delta_{rr^{\prime}}\delta_{mm^{\prime}}+\mathcal{M}^{\tau,\mu}_{aa^{\prime}}\left(\int d^{2}\mathbf{r}\chi^{*}_{krm}(\mathbf{r})\left(p_{\mu}-\frac{e}{c}A_{\mu}(\mathbf{r})\right)\chi_{kr^{\prime}m^{\prime}}(\mathbf{r})\right)+\mathcal{O}\left(\exp\left(-\mathbf{g}^{2}\ell^{2}/4\right)\right),

(75)

where

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}^{\tau,\mu}_{aa^{\prime}}=\frac{1}{A_{tot}}\int d^{2}\mathbf{r}\Psi^{*}_{a\tau}(\mathbf{r})\mathcal{M}^{\tau}_{\mu}\Psi_{a^{\prime}\tau}(\mathbf{r})

(76)

The matrices
\epsilon^{\tau}_{a,a^{\prime}}
and
\mathcal{M}^{\tau,\mu}_{aa^{\prime}}
can be obtained from the zero field
cc
coupling matrix

\displaystyle H^{c,\tau}_{aa^{\prime}}(\mathbf{k})=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}0_{2\times 2}&v_{\ast}(\tau k_{x}\sigma_{0}+ik_{y}\sigma_{z})\\
v_{\ast}(\tau k_{x}\sigma_{0}-ik_{y}\sigma_{z})&M\sigma_{x}\end{array}\right)

as

\displaystyle M^{\tau,x}_{aa^{\prime}}=\frac{\tau v_{\ast}}{\hbar}\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}0&\sigma_{0}\\
\sigma_{0}&0\end{array}\right)_{aa^{\prime}}\;\;\text{,}\;\;M^{\tau,y}_{aa^{\prime}}=\frac{iv_{\ast}}{\hbar}\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}0&\sigma_{z}\\
-\sigma_{z}&0\end{array}\right)_{aa^{\prime}}\;\;\text{,}\;\;\varepsilon^{\tau}_{aa^{\prime}}=M\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}0&0\\
0&\sigma_{x}\end{array}\right)_{aa^{\prime}}.

where the Pauli matrices
\sigma
act in the orbital space of
c
fermions. Since
\left[\hat{t}_{\mathbf{L}_{1,2}},p_{\mu}-\frac{e}{c}A_{\mu}(\mathbf{r})\right]=0
, we have

\displaystyle\left(p_{\mu}-\frac{e}{c}A_{\mu}(\mathbf{r})\right)\chi_{kr^{\prime}m^{\prime}}(\mathbf{r})=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\ell L_{2}\mathcal{N}}}\sum_{s\in\mathbb{Z}}e^{2\pi isk_{1}}\left(\hat{t}^{s}_{\mathbf{L}_{1}}\left(p_{\mu}-\frac{e}{c}A_{\mu}(\mathbf{r})\right)e^{2\pi i\left(k_{2}+\frac{r^{\prime}}{q}\right)\frac{y}{L_{2}}}\varphi_{m^{\prime}}\left(x-\left(k_{2}+\frac{r^{\prime}}{q}\right)\frac{2\pi\ell^{2}}{L_{2}}\right)\right).

The action of the two components of
p_{\mu}-\frac{e}{c}A_{\mu}(\mathbf{r})
in Landau gauge can be calculated as

\displaystyle\frac{\hbar}{i}\frac{\partial}{\partial x}e^{2\pi i\left(k_{2}+\frac{r^{\prime}}{q}\right)\frac{y}{L_{2}}}\varphi_{m^{\prime}}\left(x-\left(k_{2}+\frac{r^{\prime}}{q}\right)\frac{2\pi\ell^{2}}{L_{2}}\right)=\frac{\hbar}{i}\frac{1}{\ell}e^{2\pi i\left(k_{2}+\frac{r^{\prime}}{q}\right)\frac{y}{L_{2}}}\ell\frac{\partial}{\partial x}\varphi_{m^{\prime}}\left(x-\left(k_{2}+\frac{r^{\prime}}{q}\right)\frac{2\pi\ell^{2}}{L_{2}}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{\hbar}{i}\frac{1}{\ell}e^{2\pi i\left(k_{2}+\frac{r^{\prime}}{q}\right)\frac{y}{L_{2}}}\frac{\hat{a}-\hat{a}^{\dagger}}{\sqrt{2}}\varphi_{m^{\prime}}\left(x-\left(k_{2}+\frac{r^{\prime}}{q}\right)\frac{2\pi\ell^{2}}{L_{2}}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{\hbar}{i}\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}\ell}e^{2\pi i\left(k_{2}+\frac{r^{\prime}}{q}\right)\frac{y}{L_{2}}}\left(\sqrt{m^{\prime}}\varphi_{m^{\prime}-1}\left(x-\left(k_{2}+\frac{r^{\prime}}{q}\right)\frac{2\pi\ell^{2}}{L_{2}}\right)-\sqrt{m^{\prime}+1}\varphi_{m^{\prime}+1}\left(x-\left(k_{2}+\frac{r^{\prime}}{q}\right)\frac{2\pi\ell^{2}}{L_{2}}\right)\right)

and

\displaystyle\left(\frac{\hbar}{i}\frac{\partial}{\partial y}-\frac{eB}{c}x\right)e^{2\pi i\left(k_{2}+\frac{r^{\prime}}{q}\right)\frac{y}{L_{2}}}\varphi_{m^{\prime}}\left(x-\left(k_{2}+\frac{r^{\prime}}{q}\right)\frac{2\pi\ell^{2}}{L_{2}}\right)=

\displaystyle\frac{\hbar}{\ell}\left(\left(k_{2}+\frac{r^{\prime}}{q}\right)\frac{2\pi\ell}{L_{2}}-\frac{x}{\ell}\right)e^{2\pi i\left(k_{2}+\frac{r^{\prime}}{q}\right)\frac{y}{L_{2}}}\varphi_{m^{\prime}}\left(x-\left(k_{2}+\frac{r^{\prime}}{q}\right)\frac{2\pi\ell^{2}}{L_{2}}\right)=

(89)

\displaystyle-\frac{\hbar}{\ell}e^{2\pi i\left(k_{2}+\frac{r^{\prime}}{q}\right)\frac{y}{L_{2}}}\frac{a+a^{\dagger}}{\sqrt{2}}\varphi_{m^{\prime}}\left(x-\left(k_{2}+\frac{r^{\prime}}{q}\right)\frac{2\pi\ell^{2}}{L_{2}}\right)=

(90)

\displaystyle-\frac{\hbar}{\sqrt{2}\ell}e^{2\pi i\left(k_{2}+\frac{r^{\prime}}{q}\right)\frac{y}{L_{2}}}\left(\sqrt{m^{\prime}}\varphi_{m^{\prime}-1}\left(x-\left(k_{2}+\frac{r^{\prime}}{q}\right)\frac{2\pi\ell^{2}}{L_{2}}\right)+\sqrt{m^{\prime}+1}\varphi_{m^{\prime}+1}\left(x-\left(k_{2}+\frac{r^{\prime}}{q}\right)\frac{2\pi\ell^{2}}{L_{2}}\right)\right).

(91)

Substituting back to
\chi
, and using the orthogonality of
\chi
’s and Eq.(
Supplementary note 4.1
), we finally have,

\displaystyle\tilde{h}^{+1}_{[amr],[a^{\prime}m^{\prime}\bar{r}]}(k_{1},k_{2})\approx\delta_{r\bar{r}}\left(\begin{array}[]{cccc}0&0&-i\frac{\sqrt{2}v_{\ast}}{\ell}\sqrt{m^{\prime}}\delta_{m+1,m^{\prime}}&0\\
0&0&0&i\frac{\sqrt{2}v_{\ast}}{\ell}\sqrt{m}\delta_{m,m^{\prime}+1}\\
i\frac{\sqrt{2}v_{\ast}}{\ell}\sqrt{m}\delta_{m,m^{\prime}+1}&0&0&M\delta_{mm^{\prime}}\\
0&-i\frac{\sqrt{2}v_{\ast}}{\ell}\sqrt{m^{\prime}}\delta_{m+1,m^{\prime}}&M\delta_{mm^{\prime}}&0\end{array}\right)_{aa^{\prime}},

\displaystyle\tilde{h}^{-1}_{[amr],[a^{\prime}m^{\prime}\bar{r}]}(k_{1},k_{2})\approx\delta_{r\bar{r}}\left(\begin{array}[]{cccc}0&0&-i\frac{\sqrt{2}v_{\ast}}{\ell}\sqrt{m}\delta_{m,m^{\prime}+1}&0\\
0&0&0&i\frac{\sqrt{2}v_{\ast}}{\ell}\sqrt{m^{\prime}}\delta_{m+1,m^{\prime}}\\
i\frac{\sqrt{2}v_{\ast}}{\ell}\sqrt{m^{\prime}}\delta_{m+1,m^{\prime}}&0&0&M\delta_{mm^{\prime}}\\
0&-i\frac{\sqrt{2}v_{\ast}}{\ell}\sqrt{m}\delta_{m,m^{\prime}+1}&M\delta_{mm^{\prime}}&0\end{array}\right)_{aa^{\prime}}.

Note that a straightforward canonical substitution in Eq.(
Supplementary note 4.1
),
k_{x}+ik_{y}\rightarrow\sqrt{2B}\hat{a}
, would yield us the same finite field
c-c
coupling, where
\hat{a}
denotes the LL lowering operator for the corresponding
c
fermion LLs.

Supplementary note 4.2
c-f Coupling

The
c-f
coupling at finite
\mathbf{B}
is given as

\displaystyle H^{\tau}_{cf}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{k\in[0,1)\otimes[0,\frac{1}{q})}\sum_{a=1}^{4}\sum_{b=1}^{2}\sum_{m=0}^{m_{a,\tau}}\sum_{r=0}^{p-1}\sum_{r^{\prime}=0}^{q-1}h^{\tau}_{[amr],[br^{\prime}]}(k)c^{\dagger}_{a\tau krm}f_{b\tau kr^{\prime}},

(103)

where

\displaystyle h^{\tau}_{[amr],[br^{\prime}]}(k_{1},k_{2})=\int d^{2}\mathbf{r}\Psi^{*}_{a\tau}(\mathbf{r})\chi^{*}_{krm}(\mathbf{r})H^{\tau}_{BM}\left(p_{x},p_{y}-\frac{eB}{c}x\right)\eta_{b\tau kr^{\prime}}(\mathbf{r})

(104)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\mathcal{N}}}\sum_{s\in\mathbb{Z}}e^{2\pi isk_{1}}\int d^{2}\mathbf{r}\Psi^{*}_{a\tau}(\mathbf{r})\chi^{*}_{k_{1},k_{2}+\frac{r}{q},m}(\mathbf{r})H^{\tau}_{BM}\left(p_{x},p_{y}-\frac{eB}{c}x\right)\hat{t}^{s}_{\mathbf{L}_{1}}w_{b\tau}(\mathbf{r},\left(k_{2}+\frac{r^{\prime}}{q}\right)\mathbf{g}_{2})

(105)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\mathcal{N}}}\sum_{s\in\mathbb{Z}}e^{2\pi isk_{1}}\int d^{2}\mathbf{r}\Psi^{*}_{a\tau}(\mathbf{r})\chi^{*}_{krm}(\mathbf{r})\hat{t}^{s}_{\mathbf{L}_{1}}H^{\tau}_{BM}\left(p_{x},p_{y}-\frac{eB}{c}x\right)w_{b\tau}(\mathbf{r},\left(k_{2}+\frac{r^{\prime}}{q}\right)\mathbf{g}_{2})

(106)

\displaystyle=\frac{s_{tot}}{\sqrt{\mathcal{N}}}\int d^{2}\mathbf{r}\Psi^{*}_{a\tau}(\mathbf{r})\chi^{*}_{krm}(\mathbf{r})H^{\tau}_{BM}\left(p_{x},p_{y}-\frac{eB}{c}x\right)w_{b\tau}\left(\mathbf{r},\left(k_{2}+\frac{r^{\prime}}{q}\right)\mathbf{g}_{2}\right)

(107)

\displaystyle=\frac{s_{tot}}{\sqrt{\mathcal{N}}}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}e^{2\pi i\left(k_{2}+\frac{r^{\prime}}{q}\right)n}\int d^{2}\mathbf{r}\Psi^{*}_{a\tau}(\mathbf{r})\chi^{*}_{krm}(\mathbf{r})H^{\tau}_{BM}\left(p_{x},p_{y}-\frac{eB}{c}x\right)\hat{t}^{n}_{\mathbf{L}_{2}}W_{\mathbf{0},b\tau}(\mathbf{r})

(108)

\displaystyle=\frac{s_{tot}}{\sqrt{\mathcal{N}}}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}e^{2\pi i\left(k_{2}+\frac{r^{\prime}}{q}\right)n}\int d^{2}\mathbf{r}\Psi^{*}_{a\tau}(\mathbf{r})\left(\hat{t}^{-n}_{\mathbf{L}_{2}}\chi_{krm}(\mathbf{r})\right)^{*}H^{\tau}_{BM}\left(p_{x},p_{y}-\frac{eB}{c}x\right)W_{\mathbf{0},b\tau}(\mathbf{r})

(109)

\displaystyle=\frac{s_{tot}}{\sqrt{\mathcal{N}}}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}e^{2\pi i\left(k_{2}+\frac{r^{\prime}}{q}\right)n}\int d^{2}\mathbf{r}\Psi^{*}_{a\tau}(\mathbf{r})\left(\hat{t}^{-n}_{\mathbf{L}_{2}}\chi_{krm}(\mathbf{r})\right)^{*}H^{\tau}_{BM}\left(p_{x},p_{y}\right)W_{\mathbf{0},b\tau}(\mathbf{r})

\displaystyle-\frac{s_{tot}}{\sqrt{\mathcal{N}}}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}e^{2\pi i\left(k_{2}+\frac{r^{\prime}}{q}\right)n}\int d^{2}\mathbf{r}\Psi^{*}_{a\tau}(\mathbf{r})\left(\hat{t}^{-n}_{\mathbf{L}_{2}}\chi_{krm}(\mathbf{r})\right)^{*}\left(\frac{eBx}{c}\mathcal{M}^{\tau}_{y}\right)W_{\mathbf{0},b\tau}(\mathbf{r})

(110)

Note that the
B
-field term eventually turns out negligible because it acts on a well localized function and so at small
B
it is exponentially suppressed in the region where the vector potential is appreciable.

Using the fact
\hat{t}^{-1}_{\mathbf{L}_{2}}\hat{t}_{\mathbf{L}_{1}}=e^{2\pi i\frac{p}{q}}\hat{t}_{\mathbf{L}_{1}}\hat{t}^{-1}_{\mathbf{L}_{2}}
, we have

\displaystyle\hat{t}^{-n}_{\mathbf{L}_{2}}\chi_{krm}(\mathbf{r})=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\ell L_{2}\mathcal{N}}}\sum_{s\in\mathbb{Z}}e^{2\pi isk_{1}}\hat{t}^{-n}_{\mathbf{L}_{2}}\hat{t}^{s}_{\mathbf{L}_{1}}e^{2\pi i\left(k_{2}+\frac{r}{q}\right)\frac{y}{L_{2}}}\varphi_{m}\left(x-\left(k_{2}+\frac{r}{q}\right)\frac{2\pi\ell^{2}}{L_{2}}\right)

(111)

\displaystyle=e^{2\pi i\left(k_{2}+\frac{r}{q}\right)n}\frac{1}{\sqrt{\ell L_{2}\mathcal{N}}}\sum_{s\in\mathbb{Z}}e^{2\pi isk_{1}}e^{2\pi isn\frac{p}{q}}\hat{t}^{s}_{\mathbf{L}_{1}}e^{2\pi i\left(k_{2}+\frac{r}{q}\right)\frac{y}{L_{2}}}\varphi_{m}\left(x-\left(k_{2}+\frac{r}{q}\right)\frac{2\pi\ell^{2}}{L_{2}}\right)

(112)

\displaystyle=e^{2\pi i\left(k_{2}+\frac{r}{q}\right)n}\chi_{\text{mod}\left(k_{1}+n\frac{p}{q},1\right)k_{2}rm}(\mathbf{r})

(113)

Therefore,

\displaystyle h^{\tau}_{[amr],[br^{\prime}]}(k_{1},k_{2})=\frac{s_{tot}}{\sqrt{\mathcal{N}}}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}e^{2\pi in\frac{r^{\prime}-r}{q}}\int d^{2}\mathbf{r}\chi^{*}_{\text{mod}\left(k_{1}+n\frac{p}{q},1\right),k_{2}rm}(\mathbf{r})\Psi^{*}_{a\tau}(\mathbf{r})H\left(p_{x},p_{y}\right)W_{\mathbf{0},b\tau}(\mathbf{r})

(114)

\displaystyle-\left(\frac{eB}{c}\right)\frac{s_{tot}}{\sqrt{\mathcal{N}}}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}e^{2\pi in\frac{r^{\prime}-r}{q}}\int d^{2}\mathbf{r}\left(x\chi^{*}_{\text{mod}\left(k_{1}+n\frac{p}{q},1\right),k_{2}rm}(\mathbf{r})\right)\Psi^{*}_{a\tau}(\mathbf{r})\mathcal{M}^{\tau}_{y}W_{\mathbf{0},b\tau}(\mathbf{r})

(115)

In order to proceed we use the fact that
Song and Bernevig 2022

\displaystyle\int d^{2}\mathbf{r}e^{-i\mathbf{k}^{\prime}\cdot\mathbf{r}}\Psi^{*}_{a\tau}(\mathbf{r})H^{\tau}_{BM}\left(p_{x},p_{y}\right)W_{\mathbf{0}b\tau}(\mathbf{r})\approx\sqrt{A_{uc}}e^{-\mathbf{k}^{\prime 2}\lambda^{2}/2}H^{cf,\tau}_{ab}(\mathbf{k}^{\prime}),

(116)

which is
\sqrt{NA_{uc}}
bigger than in
Song and Bernevig 2022
because of choice of normalisation of
\chi
discussed in previous section. Using

\displaystyle H^{cf,\tau}_{ab}(\mathbf{k}^{\prime})=\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\gamma\sigma_{0}+v^{\prime}_{\ast}(\eta_{v}k_{x}\sigma_{x}+k_{y}\sigma_{y})\\
0_{2\times 2}\end{array}\right)_{ab},

we have

\displaystyle\Psi^{*}_{a\tau}(\mathbf{r})H^{\tau}_{BM}\left(p_{x},p_{y}\right)W_{\mathbf{0},b\tau}(\mathbf{r})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int\frac{d^{2}\mathbf{k}^{\prime}}{(2\pi)^{2}}e^{i\mathbf{k}^{\prime}\cdot\mathbf{r}}\int d^{2}\mathbf{r}^{\prime}e^{-i\mathbf{k}^{\prime}\cdot\mathbf{r}^{\prime}}\Psi^{*}_{a\tau}(\mathbf{r}^{\prime})H^{\tau}_{BM}\left(p^{\prime}_{x},p^{\prime}_{y}\right)W_{\mathbf{0},b\tau}(\mathbf{r}^{\prime})

(120)

\displaystyle\approx

\displaystyle\sqrt{A_{uc}}\int\frac{d^{2}\mathbf{k}}{(2\pi)^{2}}e^{i\mathbf{k}\cdot\mathbf{r}}e^{-\mathbf{k}^{2}\lambda^{2}/2}H^{cf,\tau}_{ab}(\mathbf{k})

(121)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sqrt{A_{uc}}H^{cf,\tau}_{ab}\left(\frac{1}{i}\frac{\partial}{\partial\mathbf{r}}\right)\int\frac{d^{2}\mathbf{k}}{(2\pi)^{2}}e^{i\mathbf{k}\cdot\mathbf{r}}e^{-\mathbf{k}^{2}\lambda^{2}/2}

(122)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sqrt{A_{uc}}H^{cf,\tau}_{ab}\left(\frac{1}{i}\frac{\partial}{\partial\mathbf{r}}\right)\frac{1}{2\pi\lambda^{2}}e^{-\mathbf{r}^{2}/(2\lambda^{2})}.

(123)

Moreover since
H^{\tau}_{BM}(\mathbf{p})
is linear in
\mathbf{p}
, we can approximate

\int d^{2}\mathbf{r}e^{-i\mathbf{k}^{\prime}\cdot\mathbf{r}}\Psi^{*}_{a\tau}(\mathbf{r})\mathcal{M}^{\tau}_{y}W_{\mathbf{0},b\tau}(\mathbf{r})\approx\sqrt{A_{uc}}e^{-\mathbf{k}^{\prime 2}\lambda^{2}/2}M^{\tau}_{ab},

(124)

where
M^{\tau}_{ab}
can be read of from Eq.(
Supplementary note 4.2
) to be

M^{\tau}_{ab}=-\frac{iv_{*}^{\prime}}{\hbar}\begin{pmatrix}0&1\\
-1&0\\
0&0\\
0&0\end{pmatrix}_{(ab)}=M_{ab}.

(125)

Thus we have

\displaystyle\Psi^{*}_{a\tau}(\mathbf{r})\mathcal{M}^{\tau}_{y}W_{\mathbf{0},b\tau}(\mathbf{r})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int\frac{d^{2}\mathbf{k}^{\prime}}{(2\pi)^{2}}e^{-i\mathbf{k}^{\prime}\cdot\mathbf{r}}\int d^{2}\mathbf{r}^{\prime}e^{-i\mathbf{k}^{\prime}\cdot\mathbf{r}^{\prime}}\Psi^{*}_{a\tau}(\mathbf{r}^{\prime})\mathcal{M}^{\tau}_{y}W_{\mathbf{0},b\tau}(\mathbf{r}^{\prime})

(126)

\displaystyle\approx

\displaystyle\sqrt{A_{uc}}\int\frac{d^{2}\mathbf{k}^{\prime}}{(2\pi)^{2}}e^{-i\mathbf{k}^{\prime}\cdot\mathbf{r}}e^{-\frac{{\mathbf{k}^{\prime 2}}\lambda^{2}}{2}}M_{ab}

(127)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sqrt{A_{uc}}e^{-\mathbf{r}^{2}/(2\lambda^{2})}\frac{1}{2\pi\lambda^{2}}M_{ab}.

(128)

Therefore,

\displaystyle h^{\tau}_{[amr],[br^{\prime}]}(k_{1},k_{2})\approx\frac{s_{tot}\sqrt{A_{uc}}}{\sqrt{\mathcal{N}}}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}e^{2\pi in\frac{r^{\prime}-r}{q}}\int d^{2}\mathbf{r}\chi^{*}_{\text{mod}\left(k_{1}+n\frac{p}{q},1\right)k_{2}rm}(\mathbf{r})H^{cf,\tau}_{ab}\left(\frac{1}{i}\frac{\partial}{\partial\mathbf{r}}\right)\frac{1}{2\pi\lambda^{2}}e^{-\mathbf{r}^{2}/(2\lambda^{2})}

\displaystyle-\frac{eB}{c}\frac{s_{tot}\sqrt{A_{uc}}}{\sqrt{\mathcal{N}}}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}e^{2\pi in\frac{r^{\prime}-r}{q}}\int d^{2}\mathbf{r}x\chi^{*}_{\text{mod}\left(k_{1}+n\frac{p}{q},1\right)k_{2}rm}(\mathbf{r})M_{ab}\frac{1}{2\pi\lambda^{2}}e^{-\mathbf{r}^{2}/(2\lambda^{2})}

(129)

\displaystyle=\frac{s_{tot}\sqrt{A_{uc}}}{\sqrt{\mathcal{N}}}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}e^{2\pi in\frac{r^{\prime}-r}{q}}\int d^{2}\mathbf{r}\left(H^{cf,\tau}_{ab}\left(\frac{1}{i}\frac{\partial}{\partial\mathbf{r}}\right)\chi_{\text{mod}\left(k_{1}+n\frac{p}{q},1\right)k_{2}rm}(\mathbf{r})\right)^{*}\frac{1}{2\pi\lambda^{2}}e^{-\mathbf{r}^{2}/(2\lambda^{2})}

\displaystyle-\frac{eB}{c}\frac{s_{tot}\sqrt{A_{uc}}}{\sqrt{\mathcal{N}}}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}e^{2\pi in\frac{r^{\prime}-r}{q}}\int d^{2}\mathbf{r}\left(x\chi_{\text{mod}\left(k_{1}+n\frac{p}{q},1\right)k_{2}rm}(\mathbf{r})\right)^{*}\frac{1}{2\pi\lambda^{2}}e^{-\mathbf{r}^{2}/(2\lambda^{2})}M_{ab}.

(130)

Using
\hat{t}^{s}_{\mathbf{L}_{1}}=e^{-\pi is(s-1)\frac{p}{q}\frac{L_{1y}}{L_{2}}}e^{2\pi is\frac{p}{q}\frac{y}{L_{2}}}\hat{T}^{s}_{\mathbf{L}_{1}}
, derived in the
Supplementary note 2.1
, we have

\displaystyle\chi_{\text{mod}\left(k_{1}+n\frac{p}{q},1\right),k_{2}rm}(\mathbf{r})=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\ell L_{2}\mathcal{N}}}\sum_{s\in\mathbb{Z}}e^{2\pi isn\frac{p}{q}}e^{2\pi isk_{1}}\hat{t}^{s}_{\mathbf{L}_{1}}e^{2\pi i(k_{2}+\frac{r}{q})\frac{y}{L_{2}}}\varphi_{m}\left(x-(k_{2}+\frac{r}{q})\frac{2\pi\ell^{2}}{L_{2}}\right)

(131)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{\ell L_{2}\mathcal{N}}}\sum_{s\in\mathbb{Z}}e^{2\pi isn\frac{p}{q}}e^{2\pi isk_{1}}e^{-\pi is(s-1)\frac{p}{q}\frac{L_{1y}}{L_{2}}}e^{2\pi is\frac{p}{q}\frac{y}{L_{2}}}\hat{T}^{s}_{\mathbf{L}_{1}}e^{2\pi i(k_{2}+\frac{r}{q})\frac{y}{L_{2}}}\varphi_{m}\left(x-(k_{2}+\frac{r}{q})\frac{2\pi\ell^{2}}{L_{2}}\right)

(132)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{\ell L_{2}\mathcal{N}}}\sum_{s\in\mathbb{Z}}e^{2\pi isn\frac{p}{q}}e^{2\pi isk_{1}}e^{-\pi is(s-1)\frac{p}{q}\frac{L_{1y}}{L_{2}}}e^{2\pi is\frac{p}{q}\frac{y}{L_{2}}}e^{2\pi i(k_{2}+\frac{r}{q})\frac{y-sL_{1y}}{L_{2}}}\varphi_{m}\left(x-sL_{1x}-(k_{2}+\frac{r}{q})\frac{2\pi\ell^{2}}{L_{2}}\right)

(133)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{\ell L_{2}\mathcal{N}}}\sum_{s\in\mathbb{Z}}e^{2\pi isn\frac{p}{q}}e^{2\pi isk_{1}}e^{-2\pi is(k_{2}+\frac{r}{q})\frac{L_{1y}}{L_{2}}}e^{-\pi is(s-1)\frac{p}{q}\frac{L_{1y}}{L_{2}}}e^{2\pi i\left(k_{2}+\frac{r}{q}+s\frac{p}{q}\right)\frac{y}{L_{2}}}\varphi_{m}\left(x-sL_{1x}-(k_{2}+\frac{r}{q})\frac{2\pi\ell^{2}}{L_{2}}\right)

(134)

Substituting Eq.(
134
) back in Eq.(
130
), we have

\displaystyle h^{\tau}_{[amr],[br^{\prime}]}(k_{1},k_{2})\approx\frac{s_{tot}\sqrt{A_{uc}}}{\mathcal{N}\sqrt{\ell L_{2}}}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\sum_{s\in\mathbb{Z}}e^{2\pi in\frac{r^{\prime}-r}{q}}e^{-2\pi isn\frac{p}{q}}e^{-2\pi isk_{1}}e^{2\pi is\left(k_{2}+\frac{r}{q}\right)\frac{L_{1y}}{L_{2}}}e^{\pi is(s-1)\frac{p}{q}\frac{L_{1y}}{L_{2}}}

\displaystyle\bigg[

\displaystyle\int d^{2}\mathbf{r}e^{-2\pi i\left(k_{2}+\frac{r}{q}+s\frac{p}{q}\right)\frac{y}{L_{2}}}\varphi_{m}\left(x-sL_{1x}-\left(k_{2}+\frac{r}{q}\right)\frac{2\pi\ell^{2}}{L_{2}}\right)H^{cf,\tau}_{ab}\left(\frac{1}{i}\frac{\partial}{\partial\mathbf{r}}\right)\frac{1}{2\pi\lambda^{2}}e^{-\mathbf{r}^{2}/(2\lambda^{2})}

(136)

\displaystyle-\frac{eB}{c}\int d^{2}\mathbf{r}e^{-2\pi i\left(k_{2}+\frac{r}{q}+s\frac{p}{q}\right)\frac{y}{L_{2}}}x\varphi_{m}\left(x-sL_{1x}-\left(k_{2}+\frac{r}{q}\right)\frac{2\pi\ell^{2}}{L_{2}}\right)M_{ab}\frac{1}{2\pi\lambda^{2}}e^{-\mathbf{r}^{2}/(2\lambda^{2})}\bigg]

\displaystyle\approx\frac{s_{tot}n_{tot}\sqrt{A_{uc}}}{\mathcal{N}\sqrt{\ell L_{2}}}\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\sum_{s\in\mathbb{Z}}\delta_{r^{\prime}-r,jq+sp}e^{-2\pi isk_{1}}e^{2\pi is\left(k_{2}+\frac{r}{q}\right)\frac{L_{1y}}{L_{2}}}e^{\pi is(s-1)\frac{p}{q}\frac{L_{1y}}{L_{2}}}

\displaystyle\bigg[\int d^{2}\mathbf{r}e^{-2\pi i\left(k_{2}+\frac{r}{q}+s\frac{p}{q}\right)\frac{y}{L_{2}}}\varphi_{m}\left(x-sL_{1x}-\left(k_{2}+\frac{r}{q}\right)\frac{2\pi\ell^{2}}{L_{2}}\right)H^{cf,\tau}_{ab}\left(\frac{1}{i}\frac{\partial}{\partial\mathbf{r}}\right)\frac{1}{2\pi\lambda^{2}}e^{-\mathbf{r}^{2}/(2\lambda^{2})}

\displaystyle-\frac{eB}{c}\int d^{2}\mathbf{r}e^{-2\pi i\left(k_{2}+\frac{r}{q}+s\frac{p}{q}\right)\frac{y}{L_{2}}}x\varphi_{m}\left(x-sL_{1x}-\left(k_{2}+\frac{r}{q}\right)\frac{2\pi\ell^{2}}{L_{2}}\right)M_{ab}\frac{1}{2\pi\lambda^{2}}e^{-\mathbf{r}^{2}/(2\lambda^{2})}\bigg]

where the sum over
n
above led to the Diophantine equation

\displaystyle\frac{r^{\prime}-r-sp}{q}=\text{integer}\equiv j.

(137)

Plugging in the normalization factors results in

\displaystyle h^{\tau}_{[amr],[br^{\prime}]}(k_{1},k_{2})\approx\frac{\sqrt{L_{1x}}}{\sqrt{\ell}}\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\sum_{s\in\mathbb{Z}}\delta_{r^{\prime}-r,jq+sp}e^{-2\pi isk_{1}}e^{2\pi is\left(k_{2}+\frac{r}{q}\right)\frac{L_{1y}}{L_{2}}}e^{\pi is(s-1)\frac{p}{q}\frac{L_{1y}}{L_{2}}}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2\pi\lambda^{2}}\bigg[\int d^{2}\mathbf{r}e^{-2\pi i\left(k_{2}+\frac{r}{q}+s\frac{p}{q}\right)\frac{y}{L_{2}}}\varphi_{m}\left(x-sL_{1x}-\left(k_{2}+\frac{r}{q}\right)\frac{2\pi\ell^{2}}{L_{2}}\right)H^{cf,\tau}_{ab}\left(\frac{1}{i}\frac{\partial}{\partial\mathbf{r}}\right)e^{-\mathbf{r}^{2}/(2\lambda^{2})}

\displaystyle-\frac{\hbar}{\ell^{2}}\int d^{2}\mathbf{r}e^{-2\pi i\left(k_{2}+\frac{r}{q}+s\frac{p}{q}\right)\frac{y}{L_{2}}}x\varphi_{m}\left(x-sL_{1x}-\left(k_{2}+\frac{r}{q}\right)\frac{2\pi\ell^{2}}{L_{2}}\right)M_{ab}e^{-\mathbf{r}^{2}/(2\lambda^{2})}\bigg].

(138)

Supplementary Figure 2:
Schematic representation of the
cf
coupling for
\phi/\phi_{0}=1/q
discussed in the methods section A of main text. Each tick represents (the 1D projection of) a moire unit cell, illustrating
the overlap between a 2D localized heavy state with size
\lambda
siting at the origin (red) and a Landau level (LL) i.e. a 1D harmonic oscillator (h.o.) shifted in the
x
-direction, its wavefunctions sketched by blue, with a plane wave phase variation in the
y
-direction (not shown) that depends on the shift. The
r^{\prime}
determines the momentum absorbed by the LL as well as the unit cell to which the h.o. is shifted and
k_{2}q
fine tunes the shift within the unit cell. The index
j
then determines
q
-unit-cell periodic revival of the h.o. The black parabolas mimic a quadratic potential to accompany the h.o. wavefunctions.

Note that the
y
integrals are elementary. At small
\mathbf{B}
and not too large
m
, the
x
integrals we be performed by taking the advantage of the mismatch in length scales i.e.
\ell\gg\lambda
and Taylor expand the harmonic oscillator functions about the origin. However at larger
\mathbf{B}
we may need to obtain the integrals with higher accuracy.
To this end we will find it useful to employ two-variable Hermite polynomial
\mathcal{H}_{n}(x,y)
result from
Babusci et al. 2012
. It will be useful to first discuss the integrals required for casting Eq.(
Supplementary note 4.2
) into a closed form.

We start by defining the two-variable Hermite polynomial as

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{n}(x,y)=n!\sum_{k=0}^{\left[\frac{n}{2}\right]}\frac{x^{n-2k}y^{k}}{(n-2k)!k!},

(139)

where
[n]
denotes the floor function at
n
. The two-variable Hermite polynomials are related to the Hermite polynomial in harmonic oscillator wavefunction as
H_{m}(x)=\mathcal{H}_{m}(2x,-1)
. The required
x
integral in Eq.(
Supplementary note 4.2
) then reads

\displaystyle A_{m}\left(\lambda,x_{0}\right)=\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}dxe^{-x^{2}/(2\lambda^{2})}\varphi_{m}\left(x-x_{0}\right)=\frac{1}{\pi^{\frac{1}{4}}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{2^{m}m!}}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}dxe^{-x^{2}/(2\lambda^{2})}e^{-(x-x_{0})^{2}/2\ell^{2}}H_{m}((x-x_{0})/\ell)

(140)

Let
y=x/\ell
and
y_{0}=x_{0}/\ell
. Then,

\displaystyle A_{m}\left(\lambda,\ell y_{0}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{\ell}{\pi^{\frac{1}{4}}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{2^{m}m!}}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}dye^{-\frac{\ell^{2}}{2\lambda^{2}}y^{2}}e^{-\frac{1}{2}(y-y_{0})^{2}}H_{m}(y-y_{0})

(143)

\displaystyle=\frac{\ell}{\pi^{\frac{1}{4}}}\frac{e^{-\frac{1}{2}y_{0}^{2}}}{\sqrt{2^{m}m!}}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}dye^{-\frac{1}{2}\left(\frac{\ell^{2}}{\lambda^{2}}+1\right)y^{2}}e^{y_{0}y}H_{m}(y-y_{0})

\displaystyle=\frac{\ell}{\pi^{\frac{1}{4}}}\frac{e^{-\frac{1}{2}y_{0}^{2}}}{\sqrt{2^{m}m!}}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}dye^{-\frac{1}{2}\left(\frac{\ell^{2}}{\lambda^{2}}+1\right)y^{2}}e^{y_{0}y}\mathcal{H}_{m}(2y-2y_{0},-1).

Now, using the formula in
Babusci et al. 2012

\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}dx\mathcal{H}_{n}(ax+b,y)e^{-cx^{2}+\alpha x}=\sqrt{\frac{\pi}{c}}e^{\frac{\alpha^{2}}{4c}}\mathcal{H}_{n}\left(b+\frac{\alpha}{2}\frac{a}{c},y+\frac{a^{2}}{4c}\right)

(144)

we finally have

\displaystyle A_{m}\left(\lambda,x_{0}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{\pi^{\frac{1}{4}}}{\sqrt{2^{m-1}m!}}\sqrt{\frac{\lambda^{2}\ell^{2}}{\ell^{2}+\lambda^{2}}}e^{-\frac{1}{2}x_{0}^{2}/(\ell^{2}+\lambda^{2})}\mathcal{H}_{m}\left(-2x_{0}\frac{\ell}{\ell^{2}+\lambda^{2}},\frac{2\lambda^{2}}{\ell^{2}+\lambda^{2}}-1\right).

(145)

Note that

\displaystyle A_{m}\left(\lambda\rightarrow 0,x_{0}\right)

\displaystyle=\sqrt{2\pi}\lambda\frac{\pi^{-\frac{1}{4}}}{\sqrt{2^{m}m!}}e^{-\frac{1}{2}x_{0}^{2}/\ell^{2}}\mathcal{H}_{m}\left(-2\frac{x_{0}}{\ell},-1\right)=\sqrt{2\pi}\lambda\varphi_{m}(-x_{0})

(146)

as expected because in the limit
\lambda\rightarrow 0
, the harmonic oscillator wavefunction does not vary significantly within the spatial extent of the heavy fermion gaussian, which is set by
\lambda
.

Now, let

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{m}(\lambda,x_{0})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}\lambda}A_{m}(\lambda,x_{0})

(147)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\pi^{\frac{1}{4}}\sqrt{2^{m}m!}}\sqrt{\frac{\ell^{2}}{\ell^{2}+\lambda^{2}}}e^{-\frac{1}{2}x_{0}^{2}/(\ell^{2}+\lambda^{2})}\mathcal{H}_{m}\left(-2x_{0}\frac{\ell}{\ell^{2}+\lambda^{2}},\frac{2\lambda^{2}}{\ell^{2}+\lambda^{2}}-1\right)

(148)

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{m}(\lambda\rightarrow 0,x_{0})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\phi_{m}\left(-x_{0}\right).

(149)

The y integrals in Eq.(
Supplementary note 4.2
) can be performed using

\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}dye^{-2\pi ik\frac{y}{L_{2}}}e^{-y^{2}/2\lambda^{2}}=\sqrt{2\pi}\lambda e^{-2\pi^{2}k^{2}\lambda^{2}/L_{2}^{2}}.

(150)

Using the integrals provided in Eq.(
148
) and Eq.(
150
), we now proceed to evaluate Eq.(
Supplementary note 4.2
).

Supplementary note 4.2.1
Non-derivative or the zeroth-order coupling
\gamma

\displaystyle I^{0}_{[mr],[r^{\prime}]}(k_{1},k_{2})=\frac{\sqrt{L_{1x}}}{\sqrt{\ell}}\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\sum_{s\in\mathbb{Z}}\delta_{r^{\prime}-r,jq+sp}e^{-2\pi isk_{1}}e^{2\pi is\left(k_{2}+\frac{r}{q}\right)\frac{L_{1y}}{L_{2}}}e^{\pi is(s-1)\frac{p}{q}\frac{L_{1y}}{L_{2}}}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2\pi\lambda^{2}}\int d^{2}\mathbf{r}e^{-2\pi i\left(k_{2}+\frac{r}{q}+s\frac{p}{q}\right)\frac{y}{L_{2}}}\varphi_{m}\left(x-sL_{1x}-\left(k_{2}+\frac{r}{q}\right)\frac{2\pi\ell^{2}}{L_{2}}\right)e^{-\mathbf{r}^{2}/(2\lambda^{2})}=

(151)

\displaystyle\frac{\sqrt{L_{1x}}}{\sqrt{\ell}}\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\sum_{s\in\mathbb{Z}}\delta_{r^{\prime}-r,jq+sp}e^{-2\pi isk_{1}}e^{2\pi is\left(k_{2}+\frac{r}{q}\right)\frac{L_{1y}}{L_{2}}}e^{\pi is(s-1)\frac{p}{q}\frac{L_{1y}}{L_{2}}}e^{-2\pi^{2}\left(k_{2}+\frac{r}{q}+s\frac{p}{q}\right)^{2}\frac{\lambda^{2}}{L^{2}_{2}}}\mathcal{F}_{m}\left(\lambda,\left(s+\frac{r}{p}+k_{2}\frac{q}{p}\right)L_{1x}\right)

(152)

Supplementary note 4.2.2
k_{x}
coupling

\displaystyle I^{x}_{[mr],[r^{\prime}]}(k_{1},k_{2})=\frac{\sqrt{L_{1x}}}{\sqrt{\ell}}\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\sum_{s\in\mathbb{Z}}\delta_{r^{\prime}-r,jq+sp}e^{-2\pi isk_{1}}e^{2\pi is\left(k_{2}+\frac{r}{q}\right)\frac{L_{1y}}{L_{2}}}e^{\pi is(s-1)\frac{p}{q}\frac{L_{1y}}{L_{2}}}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2\pi\lambda^{2}}\int d^{2}\mathbf{r}e^{-2\pi i\left(k_{2}+\frac{r}{q}+s\frac{p}{q}\right)\frac{y}{L_{2}}}\varphi_{m}\left(x-sL_{1x}-\left(k_{2}+\frac{r}{q}\right)\frac{2\pi\ell^{2}}{L_{2}}\right)\left(\frac{1}{i}\frac{\partial}{\partial x}\right)e^{-\mathbf{r}^{2}/(2\lambda^{2})}

(153)

\displaystyle=\frac{\sqrt{L_{1x}}}{\sqrt{\ell}}\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\sum_{s\in\mathbb{Z}}\delta_{r^{\prime}-r,jq+sp}e^{-2\pi isk_{1}}e^{2\pi is\left(k_{2}+\frac{r}{q}\right)\frac{L_{1y}}{L_{2}}}e^{\pi is(s-1)\frac{p}{q}\frac{L_{1y}}{L_{2}}}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2\pi\lambda^{2}}\frac{i}{\ell}\int d^{2}\mathbf{r}e^{-2\pi i\left(k_{2}+\frac{r}{q}+s\frac{p}{q}\right)\frac{y}{L_{2}}}\left(\ell\frac{\partial}{\partial x}\varphi_{m}\left(x-sL_{1x}-\left(k_{2}+\frac{r}{q}\right)\frac{2\pi\ell^{2}}{L_{2}}\right)\right)e^{-\mathbf{r}^{2}/(2\lambda^{2})}

(154)

\displaystyle=\frac{\sqrt{L_{1x}}}{\sqrt{\ell}}\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\sum_{s\in\mathbb{Z}}\delta_{r^{\prime}-r,jq+sp}e^{-2\pi isk_{1}}e^{2\pi is\left(k_{2}+\frac{r}{q}\right)\frac{L_{1y}}{L_{2}}}e^{\pi is(s-1)\frac{p}{q}\frac{L_{1y}}{L_{2}}}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2\pi\lambda^{2}}\frac{i\sqrt{m}}{\sqrt{2}\ell}\int d^{2}\mathbf{r}e^{-2\pi i\left(k_{2}+\frac{r}{q}+s\frac{p}{q}\right)\frac{y}{L_{2}}}\varphi_{m-1}\left(x-sL_{1x}-\left(k_{2}+\frac{r}{q}\right)\frac{2\pi\ell^{2}}{L_{2}}\right)e^{-\mathbf{r}^{2}/(2\lambda^{2})}

\displaystyle-\frac{\sqrt{L_{1x}}}{\sqrt{\ell}}\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\sum_{s\in\mathbb{Z}}\delta_{r^{\prime}-r,jq+sp}e^{-2\pi isk_{1}}e^{2\pi is\left(k_{2}+\frac{r}{q}\right)\frac{L_{1y}}{L_{2}}}e^{\pi is(s-1)\frac{p}{q}\frac{L_{1y}}{L_{2}}}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2\pi\lambda^{2}}\frac{i\sqrt{m+1}}{\sqrt{2}\ell}\int d^{2}\mathbf{r}e^{-2\pi i\left(k_{2}+\frac{r}{q}+s\frac{p}{q}\right)\frac{y}{L_{2}}}\varphi_{m+1}\left(x-sL_{1x}-\left(k_{2}+\frac{r}{q}\right)\frac{2\pi\ell^{2}}{L_{2}}\right)e^{-\mathbf{r}^{2}/(2\lambda^{2})}

(155)

\displaystyle=\frac{\sqrt{L_{1x}}}{\sqrt{\ell}}\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\sum_{s\in\mathbb{Z}}\delta_{r^{\prime}-r,jq+sp}e^{-2\pi isk_{1}}e^{2\pi is\left(k_{2}+\frac{r}{q}\right)\frac{L_{1y}}{L_{2}}}e^{\pi is(s-1)\frac{p}{q}\frac{L_{1y}}{L_{2}}}

\displaystyle\frac{i\sqrt{m}}{\sqrt{2}\ell}e^{-2\pi^{2}\left(k_{2}+\frac{r}{q}+s\frac{p}{q}\right)^{2}\frac{\lambda^{2}}{L^{2}_{2}}}\mathcal{F}_{m-1}\left(\lambda,\left(s+\frac{r}{p}+k_{2}\frac{q}{p}\right)L_{1x}\right)

\displaystyle-\frac{\sqrt{L_{1x}}}{\sqrt{\ell}}\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\sum_{s\in\mathbb{Z}}\delta_{r^{\prime}-r,jq+sp}e^{-2\pi isk_{1}}e^{2\pi is\left(k_{2}+\frac{r}{q}\right)\frac{L_{1y}}{L_{2}}}e^{\pi is(s-1)\frac{p}{q}\frac{L_{1y}}{L_{2}}}

\displaystyle\frac{i\sqrt{m+1}}{\sqrt{2}\ell}e^{-2\pi^{2}\left(k_{2}+\frac{r}{q}+s\frac{p}{q}\right)^{2}\frac{\lambda^{2}}{L^{2}_{2}}}\mathcal{F}_{m+1}\left(\lambda,\left(s+\frac{r}{p}+k_{2}\frac{q}{p}\right)L_{1x}\right)

(156)

So, finally

\displaystyle I^{x}_{[mr],[r^{\prime}]}(k_{1},k_{2})=\frac{i}{\sqrt{2}\ell}\frac{\sqrt{L_{1x}}}{\sqrt{\ell}}\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\sum_{s\in\mathbb{Z}}\delta_{r^{\prime}-r,jq+sp}e^{-2\pi isk_{1}}e^{2\pi is\left(k_{2}+\frac{r}{q}\right)\frac{L_{1y}}{L_{2}}}e^{\pi is(s-1)\frac{p}{q}\frac{L_{1y}}{L_{2}}}e^{-2\pi^{2}\left(k_{2}+\frac{r}{q}+s\frac{p}{q}\right)^{2}\frac{\lambda^{2}}{L^{2}_{2}}}

\displaystyle\times\left(\sqrt{m}\mathcal{F}_{m-1}\left(\lambda,\left(s+\frac{r}{p}+k_{2}\frac{q}{p}\right)L_{1x}\right)-\sqrt{m+1}\mathcal{F}_{m+1}\left(\lambda,\left(s+\frac{r}{p}+k_{2}\frac{q}{p}\right)L_{1x}\right)\right)

(157)

Supplementary note 4.2.3
k_{y}
coupling

\displaystyle I^{y}_{[mr],[r^{\prime}]}(k_{1},k_{2})=\frac{\sqrt{L_{1x}}}{\sqrt{\ell}}\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\sum_{s\in\mathbb{Z}}\delta_{r^{\prime}-r,jq+sp}e^{-2\pi isk_{1}}e^{2\pi is\left(k_{2}+\frac{r}{q}\right)\frac{L_{1y}}{L_{2}}}e^{\pi is(s-1)\frac{p}{q}\frac{L_{1y}}{L_{2}}}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2\pi\lambda^{2}}\int d^{2}\mathbf{r}e^{-2\pi i\left(k_{2}+\frac{r}{q}+s\frac{p}{q}\right)\frac{y}{L_{2}}}\varphi_{m}\left(x-sL_{1x}-\left(k_{2}+\frac{r}{q}\right)\frac{2\pi\ell^{2}}{L_{2}}\right)\left(\frac{1}{i}\frac{\partial}{\partial y}\right)e^{-\mathbf{r}^{2}/(2\lambda^{2})}

(158)

\displaystyle=\frac{\sqrt{L_{1x}}}{\sqrt{\ell}}\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\sum_{s\in\mathbb{Z}}\delta_{r^{\prime}-r,jq+sp}e^{-2\pi isk_{1}}e^{2\pi is\left(k_{2}+\frac{r}{q}\right)\frac{L_{1y}}{L_{2}}}e^{\pi is(s-1)\frac{p}{q}\frac{L_{1y}}{L_{2}}}\left(k_{2}+\frac{r}{q}+s\frac{p}{q}\right)\frac{2\pi}{L_{2}}

\displaystyle\times e^{-2\pi^{2}\left(k_{2}+\frac{r}{q}+s\frac{p}{q}\right)^{2}\frac{\lambda^{2}}{L^{2}_{2}}}\mathcal{F}_{m}\left(\lambda,\left(s+\frac{r}{p}+k_{2}\frac{q}{p}\right)L_{1x}\right)

(159)

Supplementary note 4.2.4
Minimal coupling

The minimal coupling term,
e\mathcal{M}_{u}A_{\mu}/c
, can be expressed in terms of the non-derivative coupling
I^{0}_{[mr],[r^{\prime}]}(k_{1},k_{2})
and
k_{y}
coupling
I^{y}_{[mr],[r^{\prime}]}(k_{1},k_{2})
as shown in this section. Let us call this term
I_{A}
, given as

\displaystyle I_{A}=-\frac{\sqrt{L_{1x}}}{\sqrt{\ell}}\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\sum_{s\in\mathbb{Z}}\delta_{r^{\prime}-r,jq+sp}e^{-2\pi isk_{1}}e^{2\pi is\left(k_{2}+\frac{r}{q}\right)\frac{L_{1y}}{L_{2}}}e^{\pi is(s-1)\frac{p}{q}\frac{L_{1y}}{L_{2}}}

\displaystyle\times\frac{1}{2\pi\lambda^{2}}\frac{\hbar}{\ell^{2}}\int d^{2}\mathbf{r}e^{-2\pi i\left(k_{2}+\frac{r}{q}+s\frac{p}{q}\right)\frac{y}{L_{2}}}x\varphi_{m}\left(x-sL_{1x}-\left(k_{2}+\frac{r}{q}\right)\frac{2\pi\ell^{2}}{L_{2}}\right)e^{-\mathbf{r}^{2}/(2\lambda^{2})}

(160)

We can re-express the factor
x\varphi(x-x_{0})
as

\displaystyle x\varphi_{m}(x-x_{0})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\pi^{\frac{1}{4}}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{2^{m}m!}}e^{-(x-x_{0})^{2}/2\ell^{2}}xH_{m}((x-x_{0})/\ell)

(161)

\displaystyle=\ell\frac{1}{\pi^{\frac{1}{4}}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{2^{m}m!}}e^{-(x-x_{0})^{2}/2\ell^{2}}(\frac{x-x_{0}}{\ell})H_{m}((x-x_{0})/\ell)+x_{0}\phi_{m}(x-x_{0}).

Moreover, using recursion relation for Hermite polynomials

\displaystyle xH_{m}(x)=\frac{1}{2}H_{m+1}(x)+mH_{m-1}(x)

(162)

we have

\displaystyle x\varphi_{m}(x-x_{0})

\displaystyle=\ell\frac{1}{\pi^{\frac{1}{4}}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{2^{m}m!}}e^{-(x-x_{0})^{2}/2\ell^{2}}\left(\frac{1}{2}H_{m+1}((x-x_{0})/\ell)+mH_{m-1}((x-x_{0})/\ell)\right)+x_{0}\varphi_{m}(x-x_{0})

(163)

\displaystyle=\ell\sqrt{\frac{m+1}{2}}\varphi_{m+1}(x-x_{0})+\ell\sqrt{\frac{m}{2}}\phi_{m-1}(x-x_{0})+x_{0}\varphi_{m}(x-x_{0})

Substituting Eq.(
163
) into Eq.(
160
), we have:

\displaystyle I_{A}=-\frac{\sqrt{L_{1x}}}{\sqrt{\ell}}\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\sum_{s\in\mathbb{Z}}\delta_{r^{\prime}-r,jq+sp}e^{-2\pi isk_{1}}e^{2\pi is\left(k_{2}+\frac{r}{q}\right)\frac{L_{1y}}{L_{2}}}e^{\pi is(s-1)\frac{p}{q}\frac{L_{1y}}{L_{2}}}

\displaystyle\times\frac{1}{2\pi\lambda^{2}}\frac{\hbar}{\ell}\int d^{2}\mathbf{r}e^{-2\pi i\left(k_{2}+\frac{r}{q}+s\frac{p}{q}\right)\frac{y}{L_{2}}}\bigg[\sqrt{\frac{m+1}{2}}\varphi_{m+1}(x-sL_{1x}-\left(k_{2}+\frac{r}{q}\right)\frac{2\pi\ell^{2}}{L_{2}})

\displaystyle+\sqrt{\frac{m}{2}}\varphi_{m-1}(x-sL_{1x}-\left(k_{2}+\frac{r}{q}\right)\frac{2\pi\ell^{2}}{L_{2}})

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{\ell}(sL_{1x}+\left(k_{2}+\frac{r}{q}\right)\frac{2\pi\ell^{2}}{L_{2}})\varphi_{m}(x-sL_{1x}-\left(k_{2}+\frac{r}{q}\right)\frac{2\pi\ell^{2}}{L_{2}})\bigg]e^{-\mathbf{r}^{2}/(2\lambda^{2})}.

(164)

Using the fact
\ell^{2}=\frac{qL_{1x}L_{2}}{2\pi p}
, note that

\frac{1}{\ell^{2}}(sL_{1x}+\left(k_{2}+\frac{r}{q}\right)\frac{2\pi\ell^{2}}{L_{2}})=\frac{2\pi}{L_{2}}(k_{2}+\frac{r}{p}+s\frac{p}{q}).

(165)

Thus we have

\displaystyle I_{A}=-\hbar\bigg[\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}\ell}\bigg(\sqrt{m}I^{0}_{[m-1r],[r^{\prime}]}(k_{1},k_{2})+\sqrt{m+1}I^{0}_{[m+1r],[r^{\prime}]}(k_{1},k_{2})\bigg)+I^{y}_{[mr],[r^{\prime}]}(k_{1},k_{2})\bigg]

(166)

Supplementary note 4.2.5
Closed form expression for
c
-
f
coupling at finite field

The closed form expression for the
c
-
f
coupling at finite
\mathbf{B}
then reads

\displaystyle H^{cf,\tau}

\displaystyle\approx

\displaystyle\sum_{k\in[0,1)\otimes[0,1/q)]}\sum_{a=1}^{4}\sum_{b=1}^{2}\sum_{m=0}^{m_{a,\tau}}\sum_{r=0}^{p-1}\sum_{r^{\prime}=0}^{q-1}\bigg[\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\gamma I^{0}_{[mr],[r^{\prime}]}(k)\sigma_{0}+v^{\prime}_{\ast}(\tau I^{x}_{[mr],[r^{\prime}]}(k)\sigma_{x}+I^{y}_{[mr],[r^{\prime}]}(k)\sigma_{y})\\
0_{2\times 2}\end{array}\right)_{ab}+

\displaystyle\left(\begin{array}[]{c}-v_{*}^{\prime}\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}\ell}\left(\sqrt{m}I^{0}_{[m-1r],[r^{\prime}]}(k)+\sqrt{m+1}I^{0}_{[m+1r],[r^{\prime}]}(k)\right)\sigma_{y}-v_{*}^{\prime}I^{y}_{[mr],[r^{\prime}]}(k)\sigma_{y}\\
0_{2\times 2}\end{array}\right)_{ab}\bigg]c^{\dagger}_{a\tau krm}f_{b\tau kr}.

Supplementary note 4.3
Low field Analysis of
c
-
f
coupling

For analyzing the low
\mathbf{B}
structure for
c
-
f
hybridization matrix, given in Eq.(
Supplementary note 4.2.5
), we will find it useful to first discuss recursion relations for
\mathcal{F}_{m}(\lambda,x_{0})
defined in Eq.(
148
). We start by considering the generating function for
\mathcal{H}_{m}(x,y)
(defined in Eq.(
139
))

\displaystyle e^{xt+yt^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\frac{t^{n}}{n!}\mathcal{H}_{n}\left(x,y\right).

(174)

Using the generating function, we can derive the following recursion relations

\displaystyle\frac{\partial}{\partial t}e^{xt+yt^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(x+2yt\right)e^{xt+yt^{2}}\Rightarrow\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\frac{t^{n-1}}{(n-1)!}\mathcal{H}_{n}\left(x,y\right)=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\frac{t^{n}}{n!}x\mathcal{H}_{n}\left(x,y\right)+\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\frac{t^{n+1}}{n!}2y\mathcal{H}_{n}\left(x,y\right)

(178)

\displaystyle\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\frac{t^{n}}{n!}\mathcal{H}_{n+1}\left(x,y\right)-\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\frac{t^{n}}{(n-1)!}2y\mathcal{H}_{n-1}\left(x,y\right)=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\frac{t^{n}}{n!}x\mathcal{H}_{n}\left(x,y\right)

\displaystyle n=0:\;\mathcal{H}_{1}\left(x,y\right)=x\mathcal{H}_{0}\left(x,y\right)

\displaystyle n>0:\;\mathcal{H}_{n+1}\left(x,y\right)-2yn\mathcal{H}_{n-1}\left(x,y\right)=x\mathcal{H}_{n}\left(x,y\right)

Therefore

\displaystyle n=0:\;\sqrt{2}\mathcal{F}_{1}\left(\lambda,x_{0}\right)=-2x_{0}\frac{\ell}{\ell^{2}+\lambda^{2}}\mathcal{F}_{0}\left(\lambda,x_{0}\right)

(179)

\displaystyle n>0:\;\sqrt{2^{n+1}(n+1)!}\mathcal{F}_{n+1}\left(\lambda,x_{0}\right)+2\left(1-\frac{2\lambda^{2}}{\ell^{2}+\lambda^{2}}\right)n\sqrt{2^{n-1}(n-1)!}\mathcal{F}_{n-1}\left(\lambda,x_{0}\right)=-2x_{0}\frac{\ell}{\ell^{2}+\lambda^{2}}\sqrt{2^{n}n!}\mathcal{F}_{n}\left(\lambda,x_{0}\right)

\displaystyle\implies\left(1+\frac{\lambda^{2}}{\ell^{2}}\right)\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\left(\sqrt{n+1}\mathcal{F}_{n+1}\left(\lambda,x_{0}\right)+\left(1-\frac{2\lambda^{2}}{\ell^{2}+\lambda^{2}}\right)\sqrt{n}\mathcal{F}_{n-1}\left(\lambda,x_{0}\right)\right)=-\frac{x_{0}}{\ell}\mathcal{F}_{n}\left(\lambda,x_{0}\right)

\displaystyle\implies\left(1+\frac{\lambda^{2}}{\ell^{2}}\right)\sqrt{\frac{n+1}{2}}\mathcal{F}_{n+1}\left(\lambda,x_{0}\right)+\left(1-\frac{\lambda^{2}}{\ell^{2}}\right)\sqrt{\frac{n}{2}}\mathcal{F}_{n-1}\left(\lambda,x_{0}\right)=-\frac{x_{0}}{\ell}\mathcal{F}_{n}\left(\lambda,x_{0}\right).

(180)

From Eq.(
159
), we have

\displaystyle I^{y}_{[mr],[r^{\prime}]}=\frac{\sqrt{L_{1x}}}{\sqrt{\ell}}\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\sum_{s\in\mathbb{Z}}\delta_{r^{\prime}-r,jq+sp}e^{-2\pi isk_{1}}e^{2\pi is\left(k_{2}+\frac{r}{q}\right)\frac{L_{1y}}{L_{2}}}e^{\pi is(s-1)\frac{p}{q}\frac{L_{1y}}{L_{2}}}\left(k_{2}+\frac{r}{q}+s\frac{p}{q}\right)\frac{2\pi}{L_{2}}

\displaystyle\times e^{-2\pi^{2}\left(k_{2}+\frac{r}{q}+s\frac{p}{q}\right)^{2}\frac{\lambda^{2}}{L^{2}_{2}}}\mathcal{F}_{m}\left(\lambda,\left(s+\frac{r}{p}+k_{2}\frac{q}{p}\right)L_{1x}\right)

(181)

\displaystyle=\frac{p}{q}\frac{2\pi\ell^{2}}{\ell L_{1x}L_{2}}\frac{\sqrt{L_{1x}}}{\sqrt{\ell}}\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\sum_{s\in\mathbb{Z}}\delta_{r^{\prime}-r,jq+sp}e^{-2\pi isk_{1}}e^{2\pi is\left(k_{2}+\frac{r}{q}\right)\frac{L_{1y}}{L_{2}}}e^{\pi is(s-1)\frac{p}{q}\frac{L_{1y}}{L_{2}}}\left(qk_{2}+r+sp\right)\frac{L_{1x}}{p}\frac{1}{\ell}

\displaystyle\times e^{-2\pi^{2}\left(k_{2}+\frac{r}{q}+s\frac{p}{q}\right)^{2}\frac{\lambda^{2}}{L^{2}_{2}}}\mathcal{F}_{m}\left(\lambda,\left(sp+r+qk_{2}\right)\frac{L_{1x}}{p}\right).

(182)

. Now using the fact
\frac{2\pi p\ell^{2}}{qL_{1x}L_{2}}=1
and the recursion relation given in Eq.(
180
), we have

\displaystyle I^{y}_{[mr],[r^{\prime}]}(k)=\frac{-1}{\ell}\frac{\sqrt{L_{1x}}}{\sqrt{\ell}}\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\sum_{s\in\mathbb{Z}}\delta_{r^{\prime}-r,jq+sp}e^{-2\pi isk_{1}}e^{2\pi is\left(k_{2}+\frac{r}{q}\right)\frac{L_{1y}}{L_{2}}}e^{\pi is(s-1)\frac{p}{q}\frac{L_{1y}}{L_{2}}}

\displaystyle\times e^{-2\pi^{2}\left(k_{2}+\frac{r}{q}+s\frac{p}{q}\right)^{2}\frac{\lambda^{2}}{L^{2}_{2}}}\bigg(\left(1+\frac{\lambda^{2}}{\ell^{2}}\right)\sqrt{\frac{m+1}{2}}\mathcal{F}_{m+1}\left(\lambda,\left(sp+r+qk_{2}\right)\frac{L_{1x}}{p}\right)

\displaystyle+\left(1-\frac{\lambda^{2}}{\ell^{2}}\right)\sqrt{\frac{m}{2}}\mathcal{F}_{m-1}\left(\lambda,\left(sp+r+qk_{2}\right)\frac{L_{1x}}{p}\right)\bigg)

(183)

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}\ell}\left(\left(1+\frac{\lambda^{2}}{\ell^{2}}\right)\sqrt{m+1}I^{0}_{[m+1r],[r^{\prime}]}(k)+\left(1-\frac{\lambda^{2}}{\ell^{2}}\right)\sqrt{m}I^{0}_{[m-1r],[r^{\prime}]}(k)\right).

(184)

where
I^{0}_{[mr],[r^{\prime}]}
is defined in Eq.(
152
). Using the above derived form for
I^{y}_{[mr],[r^{\prime}]}
in Eq(
184
), the minimal coupling term, given in Eq.(
166
), can be rewritten as

\displaystyle-\frac{I_{A}}{\hbar}=\frac{\lambda^{2}}{\ell^{2}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}\ell}\bigg(\sqrt{m}I^{0}_{[m-1r],[r^{\prime}]}(k)-\sqrt{m+1}I^{0}_{[m+1r],[r^{\prime}]}(k)\bigg)

(185)

Supplementary Figure 3:
These figures justify dropping
O(\frac{\lambda^{2}}{\ell^{2}})
in the prefactor of
I^{0,x,y}
in the
c
-
f
hybridization matrix.(a)Comparison of the unapproximated Hamiltonian spectrum which includes the minimal coupling to the one without minimal coupling at valley
\mathbf{K}^{\prime}
at CNP.(b)Comparison of the unapproximated Hamiltonian spectrum to the one obtained using low field
c
-
f
coupling at valley
\mathbf{K}^{\prime}
at CNP. These figures correspond to parameters
w_{0}/w_{1}=0.7
,
J=18.27meV
,
U_{1}=51.72meV
,
\gamma=-39.11meV
,
v^{\prime}_{\ast}=1.624eV.\mathring{A}
,
v_{\ast}=-4.483eV.\mathring{A}
,
M=3.248meV
and
\lambda=0.3792L_{m}
. We set
k_{1,2}=0
and
m_{max}=\lceil\frac{q-3}{2}\rceil
.

Note that this is an
O(\frac{\lambda^{2}}{\ell^{2}})
term. At low
\mathbf{B}
, i.e
\lambda^{2}\ll\ell^{2}
, this term is negligible as anticipated. This is confirmed numerically as shown in Supplementary Fig.(
3
a). Note that we however still need to keep the full
\frac{\lambda^{2}}{\ell^{2}}
dependence within
\mathcal{F}_{m}
for accuracy in low
\mathbf{B}
range as shown discussed in Supplementary Fig.(
4
). Keeping this in mind, let us now dwell into a low
\mathbf{B}
limit wherein we can drop the
O(\frac{\lambda^{2}}{\ell^{2}})
term in the recursion relation Eq.(
180
), i.e.

\displaystyle\sqrt{\frac{n+1}{2}}\mathcal{F}_{n+1}\left(\lambda,x_{0}\right)+\sqrt{\frac{n}{2}}\mathcal{F}_{n-1}\left(\lambda,x_{0}\right)=-\frac{x_{0}}{\ell}\mathcal{F}_{n}\left(\lambda,x_{0}\right).

(186)

Moreover using Eq.(
184
),
I^{y}_{[mr],[r^{\prime}]}
in this limit reads

\displaystyle I^{y}_{[mr],[r^{\prime}]}(k)=-\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}\ell}\left(\sqrt{m+1}I^{0}_{[m+1r],[r^{\prime}]}(k)+\sqrt{m}I^{0}_{[m-1r],[r^{\prime}]}(k)\right).

(187)

Now using the above and Eq.(
157
), we have

\displaystyle I^{x}_{[mr],[r^{\prime}]}(k)+iI^{y}_{[mr],[r^{\prime}]}(k)

\displaystyle\approx

\displaystyle-i\frac{\sqrt{2}}{\ell}\sqrt{m+1}I^{0}_{[m+1r],[r^{\prime}]}(k)

(188)

\displaystyle I^{x}_{[mr],[r^{\prime}]}(k)-iI^{y}_{[mr],[r^{\prime}]}(k)

\displaystyle\approx

\displaystyle i\frac{\sqrt{2}}{\ell}\sqrt{m}I^{0}_{[m-1r],[r^{\prime}]}(k).

(189)

Then at low field we can re-express the
c
-
f
coupling as

\displaystyle h^{+1}_{[amr][br^{\prime}]}(k)=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\gamma I^{0}_{[mr],[r^{\prime}]}(k)&i\frac{\sqrt{2}v^{\prime}_{*}}{\ell}\sqrt{m}I^{0}_{[m-1r],[r^{\prime}]}(k)\\
-i\frac{\sqrt{2}v^{\prime}_{*}}{\ell}\sqrt{m+1}I^{0}_{[m+1r],[r^{\prime}]}(k)&\gamma I^{0}_{[mr],[r^{\prime}]}(k)\\
0&0\\
0&0\end{array}\right),

\displaystyle h^{-1}_{[amr][br^{\prime}]}(k)=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\gamma I^{0}_{[mr],[r^{\prime}]}(k)&i\frac{\sqrt{2}v^{\prime}_{*}}{\ell}\sqrt{m+1}I^{0}_{[m+1r],[r^{\prime}]}(k)\\
-i\frac{\sqrt{2}v^{\prime}_{*}}{\ell}\sqrt{m}I^{0}_{[m-1r],[r^{\prime}]}(k)&\gamma I^{0}_{[mr],[r^{\prime}]}(k)\\
0&0\\
0&0\end{array}\right).

Supplementary Fig.(
3
b) shows that dropping
O(\frac{\lambda^{2}}{\ell^{2}})
in Eq.(
180
) is indeed an excellent approximation. It is thus justified to study the low field
c
-
f
hybridization matrix, given in Eq.(
Supplementary note 4.3
) and Eq.(
Supplementary note 4.3
), in pursuit of obtaining an analytical solution.

Supplementary note 5
Singular Values for
I^{0}

In this section, we study the singular values of
I^{0}_{m,r^{\prime}}(k)=I^{0}_{[m0],[r^{\prime}]}(k)
, where
I^{0}_{[m0],[r^{\prime}]}(k)
is defined in Eq.(
152
). As discussed in the main text, these singular values controls the hybridization strength between the heavy and conduction fermions in finite
\mathbf{B}
. Let us start by considering the singular value decomposition of
I^{0}_{m,r^{\prime}}(k)
:

I^{0}_{m,r^{\prime}}(k)=U_{mm^{\prime}}\Sigma_{m^{\prime}\tilde{r}}V_{\tilde{r}r^{\prime}},

(200)

where
U,V
are are
(m_{a,\tau}+1)\times(m_{a,\tau}+1)
and
q\times q
unitary matrices respectively, and the
(m_{a,\tau}+1)\times q
rectangular matrix
\Sigma
contains the singular values along the main diagonal and zeros elsewhere, where summation convention on repeated indices is implied. The columns of matrix
U
are the eigenvectors of the following matrix

\displaystyle\Lambda^{0}_{mm^{\prime}}(k)=\sum_{r^{\prime}=0}^{q-1}I^{0}_{mr^{\prime}}(k)I^{0^{\ast}}_{m^{\prime}r^{\prime}}(k).

(201)

Since
r^{\prime}
is being summed over, we are allowed to shift its range to
-\lfloor\frac{q}{2}\rfloor,-\lfloor\frac{q}{2}\rfloor+1,\ldots,q-1-\lfloor\frac{q}{2}\rfloor
and thus set
j=0
in Eq.(
152
) based on the discussion in main text. Since
\mathcal{F}_{m}
in Eq.(
152
) decays exponentially at
\pm\lfloor\frac{q}{2}\rfloor
, in the low field limit, we can replace the bounds of this sum by
\pm\infty
and have

\displaystyle\Lambda^{0}_{mm^{\prime}}(k)\approx\frac{L_{1x}}{\ell}\sum_{r^{\prime}=-\infty}^{\infty}e^{-4\pi^{2}\frac{\lambda^{2}}{L^{2}_{2}}\left(k_{2}+\frac{r^{\prime}}{q}\right)^{2}}\mathcal{F}_{m}\left(\lambda,\left(r^{\prime}+k_{2}q\right)L_{1x}\right)\mathcal{F}_{m^{\prime}}\left(\lambda,\left(r^{\prime}+k_{2}q\right)L_{1x}\right).

(202)

Using the Dirac comb identity,
\sum_{r^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}}\delta(\rho-r^{\prime})=\sum_{t\in\mathbb{Z}}e^{2\pi it\rho}
, we can convert this summation to an integral as

\displaystyle\Lambda^{0}_{mm^{\prime}}(k)

\displaystyle\approx

\displaystyle\frac{L_{1x}}{\ell}\sum_{t\in\mathbb{Z}}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}d\rho e^{i2\pi t\rho}e^{-4\pi^{2}\frac{\lambda^{2}}{L^{2}_{2}}\left(k_{2}+\frac{\rho}{q}\right)^{2}}\mathcal{F}_{m}\left(\lambda,\left(\rho+k_{2}q\right)L_{1x}\right)\mathcal{F}_{m^{\prime}}\left(\lambda,\left(\rho+k_{2}q\right)L_{1x}\right)

(203)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\ell}\sum_{t\in\mathbb{Z}}e^{-i2\pi tk_{2}q}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}d\rho e^{i2\pi t\rho/L_{1x}}e^{-\frac{\lambda^{2}}{\ell^{2}}\frac{\rho^{2}}{\ell^{2}}}\mathcal{F}_{m}\left(\lambda,\rho\right)\mathcal{F}_{m^{\prime}}\left(\lambda,\rho\right).

Because
2\pi t/L_{1x}
is a large wavevector compared to the length scale at which the h.o. wavefunctions vary,
\sim\ell
, i.e.
\frac{L_{1x}}{2\pi t}\ll\ell
at low field, the overlaps are exponentially suppressed in
(\frac{2\pi t\ell}{L_{1x}})^{2}
. We can thus set
t=0
in the above sum. Moreover since the exponential factor contains
\frac{\lambda^{2}}{\ell^{2}}
, the off-diagonal terms can be neglected and thus
\Lambda^{0}_{mm^{\prime}}
is diagonal up to a good approximation. This implies that the matrix
U
is close to identity and the low
\mathbf{B}
singular values can be approximated as
\Sigma_{m}=\sqrt{\Lambda^{0}_{mm}}
. Note that the information of magnetic quantum number
k
gets dissolved automatically due to the low field limit where the magnetic sub-bands are not
k
dispersive. We thus have

\displaystyle\Sigma^{2}_{m}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\ell}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}d\rho e^{-\frac{\lambda^{2}}{\ell^{2}}\frac{\rho^{2}}{\ell^{2}}}\mathcal{F}_{m}\left(\lambda,\rho\right)\mathcal{F}_{m}\left(\lambda,\rho\right)

(204)

\mathcal{F}_{m}(\lambda,\rho)
defined in Eq.(
148
) can re-expressed as:

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{m}(\lambda,\rho)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\pi^{\frac{1}{4}}\sqrt{2^{m}m!}}\sqrt{\frac{1}{1+(\frac{\lambda}{\ell})^{2}}}e^{-\frac{1}{2}(\frac{\rho}{\ell})^{2}/(1+(\frac{\lambda}{\ell})^{2})}\mathcal{H}_{m}\left(\frac{-2(\frac{\rho}{\ell})}{1+(\frac{\lambda}{\ell})^{2}},\frac{2(\frac{\lambda}{\ell})^{2}}{1+(\frac{\lambda}{\ell})^{2}}-1\right)

(205)

Naively one would expect that dropping
O(\frac{\lambda^{2}}{\ell^{2}})
in
\mathcal{F}_{m}(\lambda,x_{0})
, i.e. replacing
\mathcal{F}_{m}(\lambda,x_{0})
by
\varphi(-x_{0})
, to be good enough approximation at low field. However as discussed in main text, the fairly strong
m
dependence of singular values gets compromised under such an approximation, as shown in Supplementary Fig.(
4
a).

Supplementary Figure 4:
These figures illustrate the importance of including the
O(\frac{\lambda^{2}}{\ell^{2}})
in
\mathcal{F}_{m}(\lambda,x_{0})
for obtaining the correct interacting heavy fermion Hofstadter spectrum. (a) The comparison of numerically obtained singular values for
I^{0}
with the ones obtained numerically post setting
\frac{\lambda}{\ell}=0
in
\mathcal{F}_{m}(\lambda,x_{0})
. (b) The figure comparing unapproximated Hamiltonian spectrum with the one calculated using singular values marked in
red
from the left panel at
w_{0}/w_{1}=0.7
in flat band limit at valley
\mathbf{K}^{\prime}
at CNP.
m_{max}
is set to 5 for illustration.

Let
\rho/\ell=x
and
\kappa=\lambda/\ell
. Then

\displaystyle\Sigma^{2}_{m}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\pi}}\frac{1}{2^{m}m!}\frac{1}{1+\kappa^{2}}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}dxe^{-\left(\kappa^{2}+\frac{1}{1+\kappa^{2}}\right)x^{2}}\mathcal{H}^{2}_{m}\left(\frac{-2}{1+\kappa^{2}}x,\frac{\kappa^{2}-1}{\kappa^{2}+1}\right)

(206)

Now, using the result from
Babusci et al. 2012
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}dx\mathcal{H}_{m}(ax+b,y)\mathcal{H}_{n}(cx+d,y)e^{-fx^{2}+\alpha x}=\sqrt{\frac{\pi}{f}}e^{\frac{\alpha^{2}}{4f}}\mathcal{H}_{m,n}\left(b+\frac{a\alpha}{2f},y+\frac{a^{2}}{2f};d+\frac{c\alpha}{2f},z+\frac{c^{2}}{2f}\mid\frac{ac}{2f}\right)

(207)

where

\displaystyle{}\mathcal{H}_{m,n}\left(x,y;w,z|\beta\right)=\sum^{min(m,n)}_{k=0}\frac{m!n!}{(n-k)!(m-k)!k!}\beta^{k}\mathcal{H}_{m-k}(x,y)\mathcal{H}_{n-k}(w,z),

(208)

and the two variable Hermite polynomial
\mathcal{H}_{n}(x,y)
is defined in Eq.(
139
),
we have

\displaystyle\Sigma^{2}_{m}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\xi(\kappa)}}\frac{1}{2^{m}m!}\mathcal{H}_{mm}(0,\frac{\kappa^{6}}{\xi(\kappa)};0,\frac{\kappa^{6}}{\xi(\kappa)}\mid\frac{2}{\xi(\kappa)}),

(209)

where
\xi(\kappa)
is given as

\displaystyle\xi(\kappa)=(1+\kappa^{2}+\kappa^{4})(1+\kappa^{2}).

(210)

Thus

\displaystyle{}\Sigma_{m}=\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{\xi(\kappa)}}\frac{1}{2^{m}m!}\mathcal{H}_{mm}(0,\frac{\kappa^{6}}{\xi(\kappa)};0,\frac{\kappa^{6}}{\xi(\kappa)}\mid\frac{2}{\xi(\kappa)})\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

(211)

Note that

\displaystyle\kappa^{2}=\frac{\lambda^{2}}{\ell^{2}}=\frac{2\pi\lambda^{2}}{A_{uc}}\frac{p}{q}=\frac{2\pi\lambda^{2}}{A_{uc}}\frac{\phi}{\phi_{0}}

(212)

where
A_{uc}
is moire unit cell area. Since
\Sigma^{2}_{m}
is an analytic function in
\kappa^{2}
, we have an expression for the singular values continuously down to zero flux. Also note that as
\frac{\phi}{\phi_{0}}\rightarrow 0\implies\kappa^{2}\rightarrow 0\implies\xi(\kappa)\rightarrow 1\implies\mathcal{H}_{mm}(0,\frac{\kappa^{6}}{\xi(\kappa)};0,\frac{\kappa^{6}}{\xi(\kappa)}\mid\frac{2}{\xi(\kappa)})\rightarrow 2^{m}m!\implies\Sigma_{m}\rightarrow 1
, consistent with numerical results. At extremely low flux, we can approximate the singular values as

\displaystyle lt_{\kappa\rightarrow 0}\Sigma_{m}(\kappa)=1-\left(m+\frac{1}{2}\right)\kappa^{2},

(213)

i.e. approaching
1
at zero flux with a negative slope slope of
\left(m+\frac{1}{2}\right)\frac{2\pi\lambda^{2}}{A_{uc}}
as shown in Supplementary Fig.(
5
c). Note that even at extremely low flux, the
m
dependence of singular values grow linearly.

It would prove feasible to discuss the asymptote of
\Sigma^{2}_{m}
for large values of m.
Let us start expanding the four variable Hermite polynomial in Eq.(
209
).

\displaystyle\Sigma^{2}_{m}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{\xi(\kappa)}}\frac{1}{2^{m}m!}\Big[\sum_{k=0}^{m}\frac{m!m!\tau^{k}}{(m-k)!(m-k)!k!}\mathcal{H}_{m-k}(0,y)\mathcal{H}_{m-k}(0,y)\Big]

(214)

where for brevity we denote
\frac{\kappa^{6}}{\xi(\kappa)}
and
\frac{2}{\xi(\kappa)}
by
y
and
\beta
respectively. Then

\displaystyle\Sigma^{2}_{m}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{\xi(\kappa)}}\frac{1}{2^{m}}\Big[\frac{1}{m!}\mathcal{H}_{m}(0,y)\mathcal{H}_{m}(0,y)\Big]

(215)

\displaystyle+
