\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{\xi(\kappa)}}\frac{1}{2^{m}}\Big[\sum_{k=1}^{m-1}\frac{m!\beta^{k}}{(m-k)!(m-k)!k!}\mathcal{H}_{m-k}(0,y)\mathcal{H}_{m-k}(0,y)\Big]

(216)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{\xi(\kappa)}}\frac{1}{2^{m}}\beta^{m},

(217)

Supplementary Figure 5:
(a) The comparison of first 25 numerically obtained singular values with the ones obtained using the analytical asymptotes for singular values. (b) The comparison of asymptotes with exact expression for singular values. (c) Comparing
1-(m+\frac{1}{2})\kappa^{2}
with analytically computed singular values at extremely low field.

where we divided the sum over
k
into
k=0
,
k=1\ldots m-1
and
k=m
, shown in Eq.(
215
), Eq.(
216
) and Eq.(
217
) respectively. Now note that
\mathcal{H}_{m}(0,y)
is non-zero only if
m
is even, so

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{m}(0,y)=\left(1-\text{mod}(m,2)\right)m!\frac{y^{\frac{m}{2}}}{\frac{m}{2}!}

(218)

\displaystyle\implies\mathcal{H}_{m}^{2}(0,y)=\left(1-\text{mod}(m,2)\right)\frac{m!m!y^{m}}{\frac{m}{2}!\frac{m}{2}!}.

(219)

Using Eq.(
219
), we have

\displaystyle\Sigma^{2}_{m}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{\xi(\kappa)}}\left(\left(1-\text{mod}(m,2)\right)\left(\frac{y}{2}\right)^{m}\frac{m!}{\frac{m}{2}!\frac{m}{2}!}+\left({\frac{\beta}{2}}\right)^{m}\right)

(220)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{\xi(\kappa)}}\frac{1}{2^{m}}\Big[\sum_{k=1}^{m-1}\left(1-\text{mod}(m-k,2)\right)\frac{m!\beta^{k}y^{m-k}}{(\frac{m-k}{2})!(\frac{m-k}{2})!k!}\Big].

Now consider

\displaystyle\bar{A}(m)=\frac{m!}{(\frac{m}{2})!(\frac{m}{2})!}

(221)

\displaystyle\implies ln(\bar{A}(m))=ln(m!)-2ln\left(\left(\frac{m}{2}\right)!\right)\approx mln(2)

(222)

\displaystyle\implies\bar{A}(m)=2^{m},

(223)

where in Eq.(
222
), we use the Stirling’s approximation for large
m
, i.e.
ln(m!)=mln(m)-m
. Similarly consider

\displaystyle\bar{B}_{k}(m)=\frac{m!}{(\frac{m-k}{2})!(\frac{m-k}{2})!}

(224)

\displaystyle\implies ln(\bar{B}_{k}(m))=ln(m!)-2ln\left(\left(\frac{m-k}{2}!\right)\right)-ln(k!)

(225)

\displaystyle\approx mln(m)-mln\left(\frac{m-k}{2}\right)+kln\left(\frac{m-k}{2}\right)-kln(k)

(226)

\displaystyle=mln\left(\frac{2m}{m-k}\right)+kln\left(\frac{m-k}{2k}\right)

(227)

\displaystyle\implies\bar{B}_{k}(m)=2^{m-k}e^{mln\left(\frac{m}{m-k}\right)+kln\left(\frac{m-k}{k}\right)}

(228)

where in Eq.(
226
) we again have used the Stirling’s approximation. Now substituting back Eq.(
223
) and Eq.(
228
) in Eq.(
220
) we have the following asymptote:

•

If m is even

\displaystyle\Sigma_{m}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{\xi(\kappa)}}\bigg[\left(\frac{1}{\xi(\kappa)}\right)^{m}+\left(\frac{\kappa^{6}}{\xi(\kappa)}\right)^{m}+\sum_{k=2,4,6,\ldots}^{m-2}e^{mln(\frac{m}{m-k})+kln(\frac{m-k}{k})}\left(\frac{1}{\xi(\kappa)}\right)^{k}\left(\frac{\kappa^{6}}{\xi(\kappa)}\right)^{m-k}\bigg].

(229)

•

If m is odd

\displaystyle\Sigma_{m}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{\xi(\kappa)}}\bigg[\left(\frac{1}{\xi(\kappa)}\right)^{m}+\sum_{k=1,3,5,\ldots}^{m-1}e^{mln(\frac{m}{m-k})+kln(\frac{m-k}{k})}\left(\frac{1}{\xi(\kappa)}\right)^{k}\left(\frac{\kappa^{6}}{\xi(\kappa)}\right)^{m-k}\bigg].

(230)

Supplementary Fig.(

b) shows that the asymptotic expression for singular values, given in Eq.(

) and Eq.(

) , exactly matches the analytical singular values, given in Eq.(

.

Supplementary note 6
Discussion For Valley
\mathbf{K}

In this section we discuss finite
\mathbf{B}
THFM at valley
\mathbf{K}
, i.e.
\tau=+1
. Note that summation convention on repeated indices is implied unless explicitly stated.
Using the SVD decomposition discussed we can re-write the matrix elements as

\displaystyle h^{1}_{[1m0],[1r^{\prime}]}c^{\dagger}_{11k0ms}f_{11kr^{\prime}s}=\gamma\Sigma_{m^{\prime},\bar{r}}c^{\dagger}_{11k0ms}U_{mm^{\prime}}V_{\bar{r},r^{\prime}}f_{11kr^{\prime}s}=\sum_{m=0}^{m_{1,+1}}\sum_{\bar{r}=0}^{m_{1,+1}}\gamma\Sigma_{m}\delta_{m\bar{r}}c^{\dagger}_{11k0ms}\bar{f}_{11k\bar{r}s},

(231)

where we used the fact that
U
is an identity matrix and the rectangular matrix
\Sigma_{m,r^{\prime}}
is non-zero only along its main diagonal.

\displaystyle h^{1}_{[2m0],[1r^{\prime}]}c^{\dagger}_{21k0m}f_{11kr^{\prime}s}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-i\sqrt{2}\frac{v^{\prime}_{\ast}}{\ell}\sum_{m=0}^{m_{2,+1}}\sqrt{m+1}c^{\dagger}_{21k0m}\Sigma_{m+1,\bar{r}}V_{\bar{r},r^{\prime}}f_{11kr^{\prime}s}

(232)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-i\sqrt{2}\frac{v^{\prime}_{\ast}}{\ell}\sum_{m=0}^{m_{2,+1}}\sum_{\bar{r}=0}^{m_{2,+1}+1}\sqrt{m+1}\Sigma_{m+1}\delta_{m+1,\bar{r}}c^{\dagger}_{21k0ms}\bar{f}_{11k\bar{r}s}

Because
m_{2,+1}+1=m_{1,+1}=m_{max}+1
, the upper bound on the
\bar{r}
summation is the same in (
231
) and (
232
).
Similarly

\displaystyle h^{1}_{[1m0],[2r^{\prime}]}c^{\dagger}_{11k0ms}f_{21kr^{\prime}s}=i\sqrt{2}\sqrt{m}\frac{v^{\prime}_{\ast}}{\ell}\sum_{m=0}^{m_{1,+1}}\sum_{\bar{r}=0}^{m_{1,+1}-1}\Sigma_{\bar{r}}\delta_{m-1,\bar{r}}c^{\dagger}_{11k0ms}\bar{f}_{21k\bar{r}s},

(233)

\displaystyle h^{1}_{[2m0],[2r^{\prime}]}c^{\dagger}_{21k0ms}f_{21kr^{\prime}s}=\gamma\sum_{m=0}^{m_{2,+1}}\sum_{\bar{r}=0}^{m_{2,+1}}\Sigma_{m}\delta_{m,\bar{r}}c^{\dagger}_{21k0ms}\bar{f}_{21k\bar{r}s}.

(234)

Because
m_{1,+1}-1=m_{2,+1}=m_{max}
, the upper bound on
\bar{r}
summation is the same in Eq.(

) and Eq.(
234
). Thus for a given

, out of the available

f-
modes, only

.

Supplementary note 6.1
CNP

The mean-field interactions for
f
-fermion modes at CNP in
\bar{f}
basis is given as

V^{f,\tau=+1,s}=\sum_{k\in[0,1)\otimes[0,\frac{1}{q})}V^{f,\tau=+1,s}_{coupled}+V^{f,\tau=+1,s}_{decoupled},

(235)

V^{f,\tau=+1,s}_{coupled}=-\frac{U_{1}}{2}\left(\sum_{m=0}^{m_{max}+1}\bar{f}^{\dagger}_{11kms}\bar{f}_{11kms}+\sum_{m=0}^{m_{max}}\bar{f}^{\dagger}_{21kms}\bar{f}_{21kms}\right)

(236)

V^{f,\tau=+1,s}_{decoupled}=-\frac{U_{1}}{2}\sum_{b=1}^{2}\sum_{m^{\prime}=m_{max}+\bar{b}}^{q-1}\bar{f}^{\dagger}_{b1km^{\prime}s}\bar{f}_{b1km^{\prime}s},

(237)

where
\bar{1}(\bar{2})=2(1)
.
Note that there are
2q-(2m_{max}+3)
decoupled
f
modes for each
k
. The coupled modes

The coupled modes can then be described as

H^{\tau=+1,s}_{coupled}=\sum_{k}\sum_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}=1}^{6}\sum_{m=0}^{m_{\alpha}}\sum_{m^{\prime}=0}^{m_{\alpha^{\prime}}}\Xi_{m\alpha,m^{\prime}\alpha^{\prime}}d^{\dagger}_{m\alpha s}(k)d_{m^{\prime}\alpha^{\prime}s}(k),

(238)

where
m_{\alpha=1,\ldots,4}=m_{\alpha,+1}
,
m_{5}=m_{max}+1
and
m_{6}=m_{max}
, and

\displaystyle d^{\dagger}_{m\alpha s}(k)=\left(c^{\dagger}_{11k0ms},c^{\dagger}_{21k0ms},c^{\dagger}_{31k0ms},c^{\dagger}_{41k0ms},\bar{f}^{\dagger}_{11kms},\bar{f}^{\dagger}_{21kms}\right)_{\alpha}.

(239)

We now define an operator

\displaystyle\hat{h}^{+1}_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cccccc}0&0&-i\sqrt{2}\frac{v_{\ast}}{\ell}\hat{a}&0&\gamma\Sigma(\hat{a}^{\dagger}\hat{a})&i\sqrt{2}\frac{v^{\prime}_{\ast}}{\ell}\hat{a}^{\dagger}\Sigma(\hat{a}^{\dagger}\hat{a})\\
0&0&0&i\sqrt{2}\frac{v_{\ast}}{\ell}\hat{a}^{\dagger}&-i\sqrt{2}\frac{v^{\prime}_{\ast}}{\ell}\hat{a}\Sigma(\hat{a}^{\dagger}\hat{a})&\gamma\Sigma(\hat{a}^{\dagger}\hat{a})\\
i\sqrt{2}\frac{v_{\ast}}{\ell}\hat{a}^{\dagger}&0&-\frac{J}{2}&M&0&0\\
0&-i\sqrt{2}\frac{v_{\ast}}{\ell}\hat{a}&M&-\frac{J}{2}&0&0\\
\gamma\Sigma(\hat{a}^{\dagger}\hat{a})&i\sqrt{2}\frac{v^{\prime}_{\ast}}{\ell}\Sigma(\hat{a}^{\dagger}\hat{a})\hat{a}^{\dagger}&0&0&-\frac{U_{1}}{2}&0\\
-i\sqrt{2}\frac{v^{\prime}_{\ast}}{\ell}\Sigma(\hat{a}^{\dagger}\hat{a})\hat{a}&\gamma\Sigma(\hat{a}^{\dagger}\hat{a})&0&0&0&-\frac{U_{1}}{2}\end{array}\right)_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}},

where
\hat{a}
is a simple h.o. lowering operator in terms of which
matrix
\Xi_{m\alpha,m^{\prime}\alpha^{\prime}}

can be expressed as

\Xi_{m\alpha,m^{\prime}\alpha^{\prime}}=\langle m|\hat{h}^{+1}_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}|m^{\prime}\rangle.

(247)

Here
|m\rangle
is a simple h.o. eigenstate and
\Sigma(m)=\Sigma_{m}
. The non-interacting Hofstadter spectrum can be obtained by solving Eq.(
Supplementary note 6.1
) after setting the mean field terms
J,U_{1}
to zero. The spectrum for
M\neq 0
including the
2q-(2m_{max}+3
zero modes (contributed by decoupled
f

10
) with
2q
modes at each
k
, i.e. 2 modes per moiré unit cell per valley for each spin projection.

We now discuss the exact solutions to the eigenstates of the operator in Eq.(
Supplementary note 6.1
) in the flat band limit

. The

\theta_{1}=\left[0,0,\ket{0},0,0,0\right]^{T}.

(248)

The rest of the problem can be solved using the following ansätze:

\displaystyle\theta_{3}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left[c^{(3)}_{1}\ket{0},0,c^{(3)}_{3}\ket{1},0,c^{(3)}_{5}\ket{0},0\right]^{T},

(249)

\displaystyle\theta_{5}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left[c^{(5)}_{1}\ket{1},c^{(5)}_{2}\ket{0},c^{(5)}_{3}\ket{2},0,c^{(5)}_{5}\ket{1},c^{(5)}_{6}\ket{0}\right]^{T},

(250)

\displaystyle\theta_{6_{m}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left[c^{(6_{m})}_{1}\ket{m},c^{(6_{m})}_{2}\ket{m-1},c^{(6_{m})}_{3}\ket{m+1},c^{(6_{m})}_{4}\ket{m-2},c^{(6_{m})}_{5}\ket{m},c^{(6_{m})}_{6}\ket{m-1}\right]^{T},

where

(256)

0&0&0&i\sqrt{2m-2}\frac{v_{*}}{\ell}&-i\sqrt{2m}\frac{v_{*}^{\prime}}{\ell}\Sigma_{m}&\gamma\Sigma_{m-1}\\
+i\sqrt{2m+2}\frac{v_{*}}{\ell}&0&-\frac{J}{2}&0&0&0\\

i\frac{\sqrt{2}v_{*}}{\ell}&\nu(W_{3}+\frac{J}{2})&0\\
\gamma\Sigma_{0}&0&\nu(\frac{3}{2}U_{1}+6U_{2})\end{array}\right),

h^{+1,\nu=\pm 1}_{5}=\left(\begin{array}[]{ccccc}\nu W_{1}&0&-i\frac{2v_{*}}{\ell}&\gamma\Sigma_{1}&i\frac{\sqrt{2}v_{*}^{\prime}}{\ell}\Sigma_{0}\\
0&\nu W_{1}&0&-i\frac{\sqrt{2}v_{*}^{\prime}}{\ell}\Sigma_{1}&\gamma\Sigma_{0}\\
i\frac{2v_{*}}{\ell}&0&\nu(W_{3}+\frac{J}{2})&0&0\\
\gamma\Sigma_{1}&i\frac{\sqrt{2}v_{*}^{\prime}}{\ell}\Sigma_{1}&0&\nu(\frac{3U_{1}}{2}+6U_{2})&0\\
-i\frac{\sqrt{2}v_{*}^{\prime}}{\ell}\Sigma_{0}&\gamma\Sigma_{0}&0&0&\nu(\frac{3U_{1}}{2}+6U_{2})\end{array}\right),

(282)

\displaystyle h^{+1,m,\nu=\pm 1}_{6}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\begin{array}[]{cccccc}\nu W_{1}&0&-i\sqrt{2m+2}\frac{v_{*}}{\ell}&0&\gamma\Sigma_{m}&i\sqrt{2m}\frac{v_{*}^{\prime}}{\ell}\Sigma_{m-1}\\
0&\nu W1&0&i\sqrt{2m-2}\frac{v_{*}}{\ell}&-i\sqrt{2m}\frac{v_{*}^{\prime}}{\ell}\Sigma_{m}&\gamma\Sigma_{m-1}\\
+i\sqrt{2m+2}\frac{v_{*}}{\ell}&0&\nu(W_{3}+\frac{J}{2})&0&0&0\\
0&-i\sqrt{2m-2}\frac{v_{*}}{\ell}&0&\nu(W_{3}+\frac{J}{2})&0&0\\
\gamma\Sigma_{m}&i\sqrt{2m}\frac{v_{*}^{\prime}}{\ell}\Sigma_{m}&0&0&\nu(\frac{3U_{1}}{2}+6U_{2})&0\\
-i\sqrt{2m}\frac{v_{*}^{\prime}}{\ell}\Sigma_{m-1}&\gamma\Sigma_{m-1}&0&0&0&\nu(\frac{3U_{1}}{2}+6U_{2})\end{array}\right).

The magnetic subbands within the narrow bands from the coupled modes emanate out of the
\mathbf{B}\rightarrow 0
energy eigenvalue of the above decoupled matrices,
\nu(W_{3}+\frac{J}{2})
, which is
2
fold degenerate for matrix in Eq.(
Supplementary note 6.2
)
\forall m
and singly degenerate for matrices in Eq.(
Supplementary note 6.2
) and Eq.(
282
). Including the decoupled
c
mode, we have
2m_{max}+3
magnetic modes emanating out of this
\mathbf{B}\rightarrow 0
energy eigenvalue. Now recall that we have
2q-(2m_{max}+3)
decoupled
f
modes with energy
\nu(\frac{3U_{1}}{2}+6U_{2})
. Thus in total we have
2q
magnetic modes within the narrow bands, which corresponds to 2 states per moiré unit cell. The spectrum for flat band limit has been shown in Supplementary Fig.(

b). Note that the
\mathbf{B}\rightarrow 0
energies recovered by the decoupled matrices are the corresponding zero field energies of THFM at
\Gamma
in mBZ.

Using the SVD decomposition discussed we can re-write the matrix elements as

\displaystyle h^{-1}_{[1m0],[1r^{\prime}]}c^{\dagger}_{1-1k0ms}f_{1-1kr^{\prime}s}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\gamma\Sigma_{m^{\prime},\bar{r}}c^{\dagger}_{1-1k0ms}U_{mm^{\prime}}V_{\bar{r},r^{\prime}}f_{1-1kr^{\prime}s}=\sum_{m=0}^{m_{1,-1}}\sum_{\bar{r}=0}^{m_{1,-1}}\gamma\Sigma_{m}\delta_{m\bar{r}}c^{\dagger}_{1-1k0ms}\bar{f}_{1-1k\bar{r}s},

where we used the fact that

is an identity matrix and the rectangular matrix

is non-zero only along its main diagonal. Similarly

\displaystyle h^{-1}_{[2m0],[1r^{\prime}]}c^{\dagger}_{2-1k0ms}f_{1-1kr^{\prime}s}

\displaystyle-i\sqrt{2}\frac{v^{\prime}_{\ast}}{\ell}\sum_{m=0}^{m_{2,-1}}\sqrt{m}c^{\dagger}_{2-1k0ms}\Sigma_{m-1,\bar{r}}V_{\bar{r},r^{\prime}}f_{1-1kr^{\prime}s}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-i\sqrt{2}\frac{v^{\prime}_{\ast}}{\ell}\sum_{m=0}^{m_{2,-1}}\sum_{\bar{r}=0}^{m_{2,-1}-1}\sqrt{m}\Sigma_{\bar{r}}\delta_{m-1,\bar{r}}c^{\dagger}_{2-1k0ms}\bar{f}_{1-1k\bar{r}s}.

, the upper bound on the

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle i\sqrt{2}\frac{v^{\prime}_{\ast}}{\ell}\sum_{m=0}^{m_{1,-1}}\sum_{\bar{r}=0}^{m_{1,-1}+1}\sqrt{m+1}c^{\dagger}_{1-1k0ms}\bar{f}_{2-1k\bar{r}s}

2q-(2m_{max}+3)
decoupled
f
modes for each
k
. Physically this corresponds to
2-(2m_{max}+3)/q
states per moiré unit cell.

Supplementary Figure 7:
Interacting heavy fermion Hofstadter spectra for (b) CNP at
\mathbf{K}^{\prime}
contrasted with (a) corresponding zero-field spectra at
w_{0}/w_{1}=0.7
in the flat band limit
M=0
.
m_{max}=\lceil\frac{q-3}{2}\rceil
.

The coupled modes can then be described as

H^{\tau=-1,s}_{coupled}=\sum_{k}\sum_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}=1}^{6}\sum_{m=0}^{m_{\alpha}}\sum_{m^{\prime}=0}^{m_{\alpha^{\prime}}}\bar{\Xi}_{m\alpha,m^{\prime}\alpha^{\prime}}\bar{d}^{\dagger}_{m\alpha s}(k)\bar{d}_{m^{\prime}\alpha^{\prime}s}(k),

(349)

where
m_{\alpha=1,\ldots,4}=m_{\alpha,-1}
,
m_{5}=m_{max}
and
m_{6}=m_{max}+1
, and

\displaystyle\bar{d}^{\dagger}_{m\alpha s}(k)=\left(c^{\dagger}_{1-1k0ms},c^{\dagger}_{2-1k0ms},c^{\dagger}_{3-1k0ms},c^{\dagger}_{4-1k0ms},\bar{f}^{\dagger}_{1-1kms},\bar{f}^{\dagger}_{2-1kms}\right)_{\alpha}.

(350)

The following operator can be defined for
\tau=-1

\displaystyle\hat{h}^{-1}_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cccccc}0&0&-i\sqrt{2}\frac{v_{\ast}}{\ell}\hat{a}^{\dagger}&0&\gamma\Sigma(\hat{a}^{\dagger}\hat{a})&i\sqrt{2}\frac{v^{\prime}_{\ast}}{\ell}\hat{a}\Sigma(\hat{a}^{\dagger}\hat{a})\\
0&0&0&i\sqrt{2}\frac{v_{\ast}}{\ell}\hat{a}&-i\sqrt{2}\frac{v^{\prime}_{\ast}}{\ell}\hat{a}^{\dagger}\Sigma(\hat{a}^{\dagger}\hat{a})&\gamma\Sigma(\hat{a}^{\dagger}\hat{a})\\
i\sqrt{2}\frac{v_{\ast}}{\ell}\hat{a}&0&\frac{J}{2}&M&0&0\\
0&-i\sqrt{2}\frac{v_{\ast}}{\ell}\hat{a}^{\dagger}&M&\frac{J}{2}&0&0\\
\gamma\Sigma(\hat{a}^{\dagger}\hat{a})&i\sqrt{2}\frac{v^{\prime}_{\ast}}{\ell}\Sigma(\hat{a}^{\dagger}\hat{a})\hat{a}&0&0&\frac{U_{1}}{2}&0\\
-i\sqrt{2}\frac{v^{\prime}_{\ast}}{\ell}\Sigma(\hat{a}^{\dagger}\hat{a})\hat{a}^{\dagger}&\gamma\Sigma(\hat{a}^{\dagger}\hat{a})&0&0&0&\frac{U_{1}}{2}\end{array}\right)_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}},

where
\hat{a}
is a simple h.o. lowering operator in terms of which
matrix
\bar{\Xi}_{m\alpha,m^{\prime}\alpha^{\prime}}

can be expressed as

\bar{\Xi}_{m\alpha,m^{\prime}\alpha^{\prime}}=\langle m|\hat{h}^{-1}_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}|m^{\prime}\rangle.

(358)

Here
|m\rangle
is a simple h.o. eigenstate and
\Sigma(m)=\Sigma_{m}
. We now discuss the the exact solutions to the eigenstates of the operator in Eq.(
Supplementary note 7.1
) in flat band limit,
M=0
. The
\mathbf{B}
field independent
J/2
Landau level comes from the anomalous
c
-mode

\theta_{1}=\left[0,0,0,\ket{0},0,0\right]^{T}.

(359)

The rest of the problem can be solved using the following ansätze:

\displaystyle\bar{\theta}_{3}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left[0,c^{(3)}_{2}\ket{0},0,c^{(3)}_{4}\ket{1},0,c^{(3)}_{6}\ket{0}\right]^{T},

(360)

\displaystyle\bar{\theta}_{5}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left[c^{(5)}_{1}\ket{0},c^{(5)}_{2}\ket{1},0,c^{(5)}_{4}\ket{2},c^{(5)}_{5}\ket{0},c^{(5)}_{6}\ket{1}\right]^{T},

\displaystyle\bar{\theta}_{6_{m}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left[c^{(6_{m})}_{1}\ket{m-1},c^{(6_{m})}_{2}\ket{m},c^{(6_{m})}_{3}\ket{m-2},c^{(6_{m})}_{4}\ket{m+1},c^{(6_{m})}_{5}\ket{m-1},c^{(6_{m})}_{6}\ket{m}\right]^{T},

(362)

where
m\in\{2,\ldots,m_{max}+1\}
.
c^{(\beta)}_{\alpha}
denotes the coefficient of corresponding h.o state at index
\alpha
in the 6-component spinor in Eq.(
350
) and
\beta
labels the ansatz index
\theta_{\beta}
. Using the above, we can set up the eigen-equation and solve for the corresponding coefficients.

The ansätze
\bar{\theta}_{3}
and
\bar{\theta}_{5}
yield the
3\times 3
and
5\times 5
Hermitian matrices, whose eigenvectors are
c^{(3)}_{\alpha}
and
c^{(5)}_{\alpha}
, respectively:

\displaystyle h^{-1}_{3}=\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}0&i\frac{\sqrt{2}v_{*}}{\ell}&\gamma\Sigma_{0}\\
-i\frac{\sqrt{2}v_{*}}{\ell}&\frac{J}{2}&0\\
\gamma\Sigma_{0}&0&\frac{U_{1}}{2}\end{array}\right),

h^{-1}_{5}=\left(\begin{array}[]{ccccc}0&0&0&\gamma\Sigma_{0}&i\frac{\sqrt{2}v_{*}^{\prime}}{\ell}\Sigma_{1}\\
0&0&i\frac{2v_{\ast}}{\ell}&-i\frac{\sqrt{2}v_{*}^{\prime}}{\ell}\Sigma_{0}&\gamma\Sigma_{1}\\
0&-i\frac{2v_{*}}{\ell}&\frac{J}{2}&0&0\\
\gamma\Sigma_{0}&i\frac{\sqrt{2}v_{*}^{\prime}}{\ell}\Sigma_{0}&0&\frac{U_{1}}{2}&0\\
-i\frac{\sqrt{2}v_{*}^{\prime}}{\ell}\Sigma_{1}&\gamma\Sigma_{1}&0&0&\frac{U_{1}}{2}\end{array}\right).

(367)

Similarly, the ansatz
\bar{\theta}^{m}_{6}
yields the following
6\times 6
Hermitian matrix for each
m
, whose eigenvectors are
c^{(6,m)}_{\alpha}
:

\displaystyle h^{-1,m}_{6}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\begin{array}[]{cccccc}0&0&-i\sqrt{2m-2}\frac{v_{*}}{\ell}&0&\gamma\Sigma_{m-1}&i\sqrt{2m}\frac{v_{*}^{\prime}}{\ell}\Sigma_{m}\\
0&0&0&i\sqrt{2m+2}\frac{v_{*}}{\ell}&-i\sqrt{2m}\frac{v_{*}^{\prime}}{\ell}\Sigma_{m-1}&\gamma\Sigma_{m}\\
+i\sqrt{2m-2}\frac{v_{*}}{\ell}&0&\frac{J}{2}&0&0&0\\
0&-i\sqrt{2m+2}\frac{v_{*}}{\ell}&0&\frac{J}{2}&0&0\\
\gamma\Sigma_{m-1}&i\sqrt{2m}\frac{v_{*}^{\prime}}{\ell}\Sigma_{m-1}&0&0&\frac{U_{1}}{2}&0\\
-i\sqrt{2m}\frac{v_{*}^{\prime}}{\ell}\Sigma_{m}&\gamma\Sigma_{m}&0&0&0&\frac{U_{1}}{2}\end{array}\right).

The magnetic subbands within the narrow bands from the coupled modes emanate out of the
\mathbf{B}\rightarrow 0
energy eigenvalue of the above decoupled matrices,
\frac{J}{2}
, which is
2
fold degenerate for matrix in Eq.(
Supplementary note 7.1
)
\forall m
and singly degenerate for matrices in Eq.(
Supplementary note 7.1
) and Eq.(
367
). Including the decoupled
c
mode, we have
2m_{max}+3
magnetic modes emanating out of this
\mathbf{B}\rightarrow 0
energy eigenvalue. Now recall that we have
2q-(2m_{max}+3)
decoupled
f
modes with energy
\frac{U_{1}}{2}
. Thus in total we have
2q
magnetic modes within the narrow bands, which corresponds to 2 states per moiré unit cell per spin. The spectrum for flat band limit has been shown in Supplementary Fig.(
7
b). Note that the
\mathbf{B}\rightarrow 0
energies recovered by the decoupled matrices are the corresponding zero field energies of THFM at
\Gamma
in mBZ.

Supplementary note 7.2
\nu=\pm 1
Spin
\uparrow\downarrow

The interactions at
\nu=\pm 1
for valley
\mathbf{K}^{\prime}
spin
\uparrow\downarrow
Song and Bernevig 2022

\displaystyle\nu\sum_{k}\bigg(\sum_{a=1}^{4}\sum_{m=0}^{m_{a,-1}}\sum_{r=0}^{p-1}W_{a}c^{\dagger}_{a-1krms}c_{a-1krms}-\sum_{a=3,4}\sum_{m=0}^{m_{a,-1}}\sum_{r=0}^{p-1}\frac{J}{2}c^{\dagger}_{a-1krms}c_{a-1krms}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\sum_{b=1,2}\sum_{r^{\prime}=0}^{q-1}\left(\frac{1}{2}U_{1}+6U_{2}\right)f^{\dagger}_{b-1kr^{\prime}s}f_{b-1kr^{\prime}s}\bigg),

W_{a\in\{1\ldots 4\}}
and

are mean field coefficients with
W_{1}=W_{2}
and
W_{3}=W_{4}

Song and Bernevig 2022
. The interaction for
f
modes in the
\bar{f}

\displaystyle V^{f,\tau=-1,s}_{\nu=\pm 1}

(377)

\displaystyle V^{f,\tau=-1,s,\nu=\pm 1}_{decoupled}=\nu\sum_{b=1}^{2}\sum_{m^{\prime}=m_{max}+b}^{q-1}\left(\frac{1}{2}U_{1}+6U_{2}\right)\bar{f}^{\dagger}_{b-1km^{\prime}s}\bar{f}_{b-1km^{\prime}s}.

(378)

\nu(\frac{3U_{1}}{4}+6U_{2})
. Thus in total we have
2q
magnetic modes within the narrow bands, which corresponds to 2 states per moiré unit cell for each spin projection. The spectrum for flat band limit has been shown in Supplementary Fig.(
9
b). Note that the
\mathbf{B}\rightarrow 0
energies recovered by the decoupled matrices are the corresponding zero field energy of THFM at
\Gamma
in mBZ.

Supplementary note 8
Naive Minimal Substitution

Supplementary Figure 10:
The spin valley degenerate non-interacting Hofstadter spectrum for
M\neq 0
at
\omega_{0}/\omega_{1}=0.7
(a) obtained using naive minimal coupling compared to the (b) spectrum obtained using the formalism constructed in the paper for
\mathbf{B}\neq 0
THFM. The magnetic subbands within the narrow bands are spread in the energy window of
|2M|
, which also sets the bandwidth of narrow bands at
\mathbf{B}=0
in THFM. The total number of subbands within the narrow bands i.e. in the energy widow [-M,M] at flux
1/q
in (a) is (
2m_{\ast}+1
) while in (b) is correctly
2q
. We use
m_{max}=m_{\ast}=\lfloor\frac{q-3}{2}\rfloor

.

Including both the
c
-
c
and
c
-
f
coupling in Eq.(2) of main-text, the zero
\mathbf{B}
field THFM can be written as a
6\times 6
matrix, say at
\tau=+1

\displaystyle h_{0}^{\tau=1}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cccccc}0&0&v_{\ast}k&0&e^{-\frac{\mathbf{k}^{2}\lambda^{2}}{2}}\gamma&e^{-\frac{\mathbf{k}^{2}\lambda^{2}}{2}}v_{\ast}^{\prime}\bar{k}\\
0&0&0&v_{\ast}\bar{k}&e^{-\frac{\mathbf{k}^{2}\lambda^{2}}{2}}v_{\ast}^{\prime}k&e^{-\frac{\mathbf{k}^{2}\lambda^{2}}{2}}\gamma\\
v_{\ast}\bar{k}&0&0&M&0&0\\
0&v_{\ast}k&M&0&0&0\\
e^{-\frac{\mathbf{k}^{2}\lambda^{2}}{2}}\gamma&e^{-\frac{\mathbf{k}^{2}\lambda^{2}}{2}}v_{\ast}^{\prime}\bar{k}&0&0&0&0\\
e^{-\frac{\mathbf{k}^{2}\lambda^{2}}{2}}v_{\ast}^{\prime}k&e^{-\frac{\mathbf{k}^{2}\lambda^{2}}{2}}\gamma&0&0&0&0\end{array}\right)_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}},

where
k=k_{x}+ik_{y}
and
\bar{k}=k_{x}-ik_{y}
. Following a naive minimal substitution, we promote
k_{x}+ik_{y}\rightarrow-i\sqrt{2}\hat{a}/\ell
, where
\hat{a}
is the Landau level (LL) lowering operator. Although it is completely unclear on how to perform the minimal substitution on
e^{-\frac{1}{2}\mathbf{k}^{2}\lambda^{2}}
, if we were to Taylor expand it as
1-\frac{1}{2}\mathbf{k}^{2}\lambda^{2}+\ldots=1-\frac{1}{2}\frac{k\bar{k}+\bar{k}k}{2}\lambda^{2}+\ldots
and use the finite
\mathbf{B}
substitution for
k
and
\bar{k}
mentioned above we get
1-(a^{\dagger}a+\frac{1}{2})\frac{\lambda^{2}}{\ell^{2}}+\ldots
. If this operator acts on LL state
|m\rangle
, upto
O(\frac{\lambda^{2}}{\ell^{2}})
, we get
1-(m+\frac{1}{2})\frac{\lambda^{2}}{\ell^{2}}
. But recall that this is same as the
\mathbf{B}\rightarrow 0
limit of
\Sigma_{m}
as given in Eq.(
213
). Based on this assumption, we thus promote
e^{-\frac{1}{2}\mathbf{k}^{2}\lambda^{2}}
to
\Sigma_{a^{\dagger}a}=\Sigma(a^{\dagger}a)
at finite
\mathbf{B}
. We thus have

\displaystyle\hat{h}_{B}^{\tau=1}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cccccc}0&0&-i\sqrt{2}\frac{v_{\ast}}{\ell}\hat{a}&0&\gamma\Sigma(\hat{a}^{\dagger}\hat{a})&i\sqrt{2}\frac{v^{\prime}_{\ast}}{\ell}\hat{a}^{\dagger}\Sigma(\hat{a}^{\dagger}\hat{a})\\
0&0&0&i\sqrt{2}\frac{v_{\ast}}{\ell}\hat{a}^{\dagger}&-i\sqrt{2}\frac{v^{\prime}_{\ast}}{\ell}\hat{a}\Sigma(\hat{a}^{\dagger}\hat{a})&\gamma\Sigma(\hat{a}^{\dagger}\hat{a})\\
i\sqrt{2}\frac{v_{\ast}}{\ell}\hat{a}^{\dagger}&0&0&M&0&0\\
0&-i\sqrt{2}\frac{v_{\ast}}{\ell}\hat{a}&M&0&0&0\\
\gamma\Sigma(\hat{a}^{\dagger}\hat{a})&i\sqrt{2}\frac{v^{\prime}_{\ast}}{\ell}\Sigma(\hat{a}^{\dagger}\hat{a})\hat{a}^{\dagger}&0&0&0&0\\
-i\sqrt{2}\frac{v^{\prime}_{\ast}}{\ell}\Sigma(\hat{a}^{\dagger}\hat{a})\hat{a}&\gamma\Sigma(\hat{a}^{\dagger}\hat{a})&0&0&0&0\end{array}\right)_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}},

where we assume that the correct way of ordering
\Sigma(a^{\dagger}a)
with respect to the operators
a
and
a^{\dagger}
(coming from the promotion of
k
and
\bar{k}
respectively) is the one we have in Eq.(
Supplementary note 6.1
). The Hofstadter spectrum based on the naive minimal substitution approach can thus be obtained by solving the eigenstates of
\hat{h}^{\tau=1}_{B_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}}
in the LL basis.
The LL basis
\ket{m}
used for computing the matrix elements for index
\alpha\in\{1,6\}
are:
m\in\{0,m_{\ast}\}
for index
\alpha=1
(
a=1

c
-fermion),
m\in\{0,m_{\ast}-1\}
for index
\alpha=2
(
a=2

c
-fermion),
m\in\{0,m_{\ast}+1\}
for index
\alpha=3
(
a=1

c
-fermion),
m\in\{0,m_{\ast}-2\}
for index
\alpha=4
(
a=1

c
-fermion),
m\in\{0,m_{\ast}\}
for index
\alpha=5
(
b=1

f
-fermion) and
m\in\{0,m_{\ast}-1\}
for index
\alpha=6
(
b=2

f
-fermion). The spectrum is shown in Fig.(
10
a). Although the magnetic subbands within the narrow bands are well separated from the remote bands, the total number of states within the narrow bands is
(2m_{\star}+1)\phi/\phi_{0}
per moié unit cell per spin per valley (same as the number of zero modes for
M=0
, shown in main-text), which of course is incorrect as the total number of states should rather be
2
per moiré unit cell per spin per valley, independent of
\mathbf{B}
.

Supplementary note 9
Parent Kramers inter-valley coherent state

In this section, we discuss the Landau quantization of the one-shot Hartree Fock (HF) bands obtained for a parent Kramers inter-valley coherent (KIVC) state at fillings
\nu=0
(CNP) and
\nu=-2
.

Supplementary note 9.1
CNP

Supplementary Figure 11:
Spin degenerate (b) interacting heavy fermion Hofstadter spectrum for KIVC state at CNP contrasted with (a) corresponding zero-field spectrum at
w_{0}/w_{1}=0.7
.
m_{max}=\lceil\frac{q-3}{2}\rceil
.

The spin degenerate
\mathbf{B}=0
one-shot Hartree-Fock (HF) mean-field (MF) Hamiltonian for parent KIVC state at
\nu=0
is given as
Song and Bernevig 2022

\displaystyle H^{\nu=0,s=\uparrow,\downarrow}_{\mathbf{0},\text{KIVC}}(\mathbf{k})=\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}0&v_{\star}(k_{x}\sigma_{0}\tau_{z}+ik_{y}\sigma_{z}\tau_{0})&e^{-\frac{\mathbf{k}^{2}\lambda^{2}}{2}}\left(\gamma\sigma_{0}\tau_{0}+v_{\star}^{\prime}(k_{x}\sigma_{x}\tau_{z}+k_{y}\sigma_{y}\tau_{0})\right)\\
&-\frac{J}{2}\sigma_{y}\tau_{y}+M\sigma_{x}\tau_{0}&0\\
h.c.&&\frac{U_{1}}{2}\sigma_{y}\tau_{y}\end{array}\right),

where
\mathbf{k}=(k_{x},k_{y})\in
moiré BZ, the Pauli matrices
\sigma
and
\tau
act in the orbital and valley space respectively and h.c. represents hermitian conjugate.

Let us begin the discussion by first analyzing the decoupled
\bar{f}
modes at
\mathbf{B}\neq 0
. Recall that at every
k\in
magnetic BZ,
q-(m_{max}+2)
of
\bar{f}_{11kr^{\prime}s}
and
\bar{f}_{2-1kr^{\prime}s}
modes, i.e. the ones with
r^{\prime}\geq m_{max}+1
decouple from the
c
s. Similarly
q-(m_{max}+1)
of
\bar{f}_{21kr^{\prime}s}
and
\bar{f}_{1-1kr^{\prime}s}
modes, i.e. the ones with
r^{\prime}\geq m_{max}
decouple from the
c
’s at every
k\in
magnetic BZ.
We see that even for the KIVC state, the decoupled
\bar{f}
modes contribute to
2q-(2m_{max}+3)

\mathbf{B}
independent energy levels at
\pm U_{1}/2
.
Physically, these modes are the linear combination
\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\left(\bar{f}_{11kr^{\prime}s}\pm\bar{f}_{2-1kr^{\prime}s}\right)
with
r^{\prime}\geq m_{max}+1
and
\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\left(\bar{f}_{21kr^{\prime}s}\pm\bar{f}_{1-1kr^{\prime}s}\right)
with
r^{\prime}\geq m_{max}
, which contribute
q-(m_{max}+2)
and
q-(m_{max}+1)
modes at
\pm U_{1}/2
, respectively. We thus have
2q-(2m_{max}+3)
modes at
\pm U_{1}/2
as motivated above.

Before discussing the Hofstadter spectrum for the coupled modes, we first perform a
U(4)
rotation (
e^{i\frac{\pi}{2}\hat{\Sigma}_{x0}}
, where
\hat{\Sigma}_{x0}
is defined in Eq.(4) of Ref.
Song and Bernevig 2022
)

\displaystyle\tilde{H}^{\nu=0,s=\uparrow,\downarrow}_{\mathbf{0},\text{KIVC}}(\mathbf{k})=\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}0&v_{\star}(k_{x}\sigma_{0}\tau_{z}+ik_{y}\sigma_{z}\tau_{0})&e^{-\frac{\mathbf{k}^{2}\lambda^{2}}{2}}\left(\gamma\sigma_{0}\tau_{0}+v_{\star}^{\prime}(k_{x}\sigma_{x}\tau_{z}+k_{y}\sigma_{y}\tau_{0})\right)\\
&-\frac{J}{2}\sigma_{0}\tau_{z}-M\sigma_{z}\tau_{x}&0\\
h.c.&&-\frac{U_{1}}{2}\sigma_{0}\tau_{z}\end{array}\right),

If we were to set

the two valleys in the above Hamiltonian decouple (the model becomes identical to that for VP state due to the

\mathbf{B}\neq 0

\displaystyle\hat{h}^{\text{KIVC},\nu=0}_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\hat{h}^{+1,\nu=0}&\hat{h}_{M}\\

Supplementary note 10
Effective Hamiltonians for Landau Quantization of light modes

In order to better understand the Landau quantization of the zero
\mathbf{B}
bands of interacting THFM in vicinity of
\Gamma\in
mBZ, i.e. where the lowest energy single particle excitations (light modes) reside, we derive an effective low energy Hamiltonian for THFM at
\mathbf{B}\neq 0
in this section. Such an analysis offers us with a deeper qualitative understanding of not only the low energy but also the low
\mathbf{B}
physics of THFM. As discussed in the main text, the decoupled
\bar{f}
modes are only responsible for forming the
\mathbf{B}
independent higher energy level (heavy modes)
and it is rather the coupled modes of
c
and
f
fermions which dictate the Hofstadter spectrum of THFM. Thus the results obtained for coupled modes in previous sections serves as the starting point for the following analysis.

Supplementary note 10.1

In the flat band limit

Song and Bernevig 2022

energy
\mp J/2

Supplementary note 10.1.1
Parent Valley Polarized State

Supplementary Figure 13:
The comparison of (b) LL spectrum obtained using the effective Hamiltonian (
\cdot

) contrasted with the corresponding comparison at (a)

Clearly the subspace of interest, for which we want to derive an effective Hamiltonian, is the one spanned by states
|\rho=1,j,\tau,m\rangle
, i.e. the
a=\{3,4\}

c
fermions for each
\tau=\pm 1

Supplementary note 6.1
) and (
Supplementary note 7.1
) (i.e. at
\tau=+1,-1
respectively) which can mix the subspaces using Schrieffer Wolff Transformation (SWT). We moreover omit the contributions to effective Hamiltonian of order greater than
B^{3/2}
, i.e.
1/\ell^{3}
. Below, we discuss the SWT scheme followed to obtain the effective Hamiltonian. We first rewrite the operators in Eqs.(
Supplementary note 6.1
) and (
Supplementary note 7.1
) with respect to the
\mathbf{B}=0

\Gamma
,
|\rho,j,\tau\rangle
, given in Eqs.(

)-(

) as

\displaystyle H=H_{0}+\Delta V,

(475)

where
\Delta
is an artificial parameter that helps us keeping track of the order in perturbation, to which we compute the effective Hamiltonian and is later set to 1. Along with
M
, we treat all the terms in operator in Eqs.(
Supplementary note 6.1
) and (
Supplementary note 7.1
) (i.e. at
\tau=+1,-1
respectively) which can mix the subspaces as perturbation
V
. The unperturbed part
H_{0}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}H_{0}^{\tau=+1}&\\
&H_{0}^{\tau=-1}\end{array}\right)
where

\displaystyle H_{0}^{\tau}=\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}E_{\tau}\sigma_{0}&0&0\\
0&E_{X\tau}\sigma_{0}&0\\
0&0&E_{Y\tau}\sigma_{0}\end{array}\right).

The Pauli matrix
\sigma_{0}
above acts in the
j\in\{1,2\}
space of for each
\rho
, and the energies
E
,
E_{X\tau}
,

are given in Eqs.(
472
)-(
474
). The perturbation is given as
V=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}V^{\tau=+1}&0\\
0.&V^{\tau=-1}\end{array}\right)
, where

\displaystyle V^{\tau=+1}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cccccc}0&M&-i\frac{\sqrt{2}}{\ell}\frac{X_{1}v_{\star}}{\sqrt{N_{X1}}}a&0&-i\frac{\sqrt{2}}{\ell}\frac{Y_{1}v_{\star}}{\sqrt{N_{Y1}}}a&0\\
&0&0&i\frac{\sqrt{2}}{\ell}\frac{X_{1}v_{\star}}{\sqrt{N_{X1}}}a^{\dagger}&0&i\frac{\sqrt{2}}{\ell}\frac{Y_{1}v_{\star}}{\sqrt{N_{Y1}}}a^{\dagger}\\
&&0&-i\frac{2\sqrt{2}}{\ell}\frac{X_{1}v_{\star}^{\prime}}{N_{X1}}a&0&-i\frac{\sqrt{2}}{\ell}\frac{(X_{1}+Y_{1})v_{\star}^{\prime}}{\sqrt{N_{X1}N_{Y1}}}a\\
&&&0&i\frac{\sqrt{2}}{\ell}\frac{(X_{1}+Y_{1})v_{\star}^{\prime}}{\sqrt{N_{X1}N_{Y1}}}a^{\dagger}&0\\
&&&&0&-i\frac{2\sqrt{2}}{\ell}\frac{Y_{1}v_{\star}^{\prime}}{N_{Y1}}a\\
h.c.&&&&&0\end{array}\right),

where
h.c.
represents hermitian conjugate. In above matrix, the terms of

have been opted out as they contribute terms of
O(\frac{1}{\ell^{4}})
to the effective Hamiltonian.
V^{\tau=-1}
can be obtained by replacing
X_{1},Y_{1}\rightarrow X_{-1},Y_{-1}
,
N_{X1},N_{Y1}\rightarrow N_{X-1},N_{Y-1}
and
a\leftrightarrow a^{\dagger}
in Eq.(
Supplementary note 10.1.1
).
Let us now consider the SWT, generated by
S=S^{\dagger}
, such that it decouples the three subspaces in the transformed Hamiltonian. The transformed Hamiltonian is given as

\displaystyle H^{\prime}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle e^{iS}He^{-iS}

(487)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle H+i[S,H]+\frac{i^{2}}{2!}[S,[S,H]]+\frac{i^{3}}{3!}[S,[S,[S,H]]]+\ldots.

(488)

The generator of the transformation can be expanded in orders of
\Delta
as

\displaystyle S=\Delta S_{1}+\Delta^{2}S_{2}+\Delta^{3}S_{3}+\ldots

(489)

Substituting Eq.(
489
) into Eq.(
488
) gives us

\displaystyle H^{\prime}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle H_{0}+\Delta\left(V+i[S_{1},H_{0}]\right)+\Delta^{2}\left(i[S_{2},H_{0}]+i[S_{1},V]+\frac{i^{2}}{2!}[S_{1},[S_{1},H_{0}]]\right)+

(492)

\displaystyle\Delta^{3}\left(i[S_{3},H_{0}]+i[S_{2},V]+\frac{i^{2}}{2!}[S_{2},[S_{1},H_{0}]]+\frac{i^{2}}{2!}[S_{1},[S_{2},H_{0}]]+\frac{i^{2}}{2!}[S_{1},[S_{1},V]]]+\frac{i^{3}}{3!}[S_{1},[S_{1},[S_{1},H_{0}]]\right)

\displaystyle+O(\Delta^{4})+\ldots

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle H_{0}+\Delta H_{1}^{\prime}+\Delta^{2}H_{2}^{\prime}+\Delta^{3}H_{3}^{\prime}+O(\Delta^{4})+\ldots

(493)

Recall that the generator
S
is defined by the condition that the SWT decouples the three subspaces. Since the two valleys are decoupled, for either valley
\tau
, we have

\displaystyle\langle\rho,j,\tau,m|H_{n}^{\prime}|\rho^{\prime},j^{\prime},\tau,m^{\prime}\rangle=\delta_{\rho,\rho^{\prime}}\langle\rho,j,\tau,m|H_{n}^{\prime}|\rho,j^{\prime},\tau,m^{\prime}\rangle.

(494)

We thus have, for
\rho\neq\rho^{\prime}
and
n=1
in above

\displaystyle\langle\rho,j,\tau,m|\left(V+i[S_{1},H_{0}]\right)|\rho^{\prime},j^{\prime},\tau,m^{\prime}\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 0

(495)

\displaystyle\implies\langle\rho,j,\tau,m|S_{1}|\rho^{\prime},j^{\prime},\tau,m^{\prime}\rangle=\begin{cases}i\frac{\langle\rho,j,\tau,m|V|\rho^{\prime},j^{\prime},\tau,m^{\prime}\rangle}{E_{\rho^{\prime}j^{\prime}\tau}-E_{\rho j\tau}}&\text{for}\penalty\ \penalty\ \rho\neq\rho^{\prime},\\
0&\text{for}\penalty\ \penalty\ \rho=\rho^{\prime},\end{cases}

(496)

Similarly, for
\rho\neq\rho^{\prime}
and
n=2
, we have

\displaystyle\langle\rho,j,\tau,m|\left(i[S_{2},H_{0}]+i[S_{1},V]+\frac{i^{2}}{2!}[S_{1},[S_{1},H_{0}]]\right)|\rho^{\prime},j^{\prime},\tau,m^{\prime}\rangle=0

(497)

\displaystyle\implies\langle\rho,j,\tau,m|S_{2}|\rho^{\prime},j^{\prime},\tau,m^{\prime}\rangle=\frac{1}{E_{\rho j\tau}-E_{\rho^{\prime}j^{\prime}\tau}}\left(\sum_{\tilde{\rho}\neq\rho;\tilde{j};\tilde{m}}\langle\rho,j,\tau,m|S_{1}|\tilde{\rho},\tilde{j},\tau,\tilde{m}\rangle\langle\tilde{\rho},\tilde{j},\tau,\tilde{m}|V|\rho^{\prime},j^{\prime},\tau,m^{\prime}\rangle-\right.

\displaystyle\left.\sum_{\tilde{\rho}\neq\rho^{\prime};\tilde{j};\tilde{m}}\langle\rho,j,\tau,m|V|\tilde{\rho},\tilde{j},\tau,\tilde{m}\rangle\langle\tilde{\rho},\tilde{j},\tau,\tilde{m}|S_{1}|\rho^{\prime},j^{\prime},\tau,m^{\prime}\rangle\right.

\displaystyle-\left.\sum_{\tilde{\rho}\neq\rho\rho^{\prime};\tilde{j},\tilde{m}}\left(E_{\tilde{\rho}\tilde{j}\tau}-\frac{E_{\rho j\tau}+E_{\rho^{\prime}j^{\prime}\tau}}{2}\right)\langle\rho,j,\tau,m|S_{1}|\tilde{\rho},\tilde{j},\tau,\tilde{m}\rangle\langle\tilde{\rho},\tilde{j},\tau,\tilde{m}|S_{1}|\rho^{\prime},j^{\prime},\tau,m^{\prime}\rangle\right)\text{for}\penalty\ \penalty\ \rho\neq\rho^{\prime},

\displaystyle\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ =0\penalty\ \penalty\ \text{for}\penalty\ \penalty\ \rho=\rho^{\prime},

(498)

were
\tilde{j}
is summed over
\{1,2\}
, i.e. the two states constituting each subspace. We further assume that

\displaystyle\sum_{\tilde{m}}|\tilde{\rho},\tilde{j},\tau,\tilde{m}\rangle\langle\tilde{\rho},\tilde{j},\tau,\tilde{m}|=|\tilde{\rho},\tilde{j},\tau\rangle\langle\tilde{\rho},\tilde{j},\tau|,

(499)

which is justified in the (
\mathbf{k}\cdot\mathbf{p}
) continuum limit, as the upper cutoff on LLs in such a limit is unbounded. The effective Hamiltonian for either subspace
\rho
, can now be deduced in orders of
\Delta
. The O(
\Delta^{0}
) contribution to the effective Hamiltonian in subspace
\rho
is trivially
E_{\rho j\tau}\delta_{jj^{\prime}}
and O(
\Delta^{1}
) terms are

\displaystyle\langle\rho,j,\tau,m|H_{1}^{\prime}|\rho,j^{\prime},\tau,m^{\prime}\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\langle\rho,j,\tau,m|V|\rho,j^{\prime},\tau,m^{\prime}\rangle.

(500)

The O(
\Delta^{2}
) terms are found out to be

\displaystyle\langle\rho,j,\tau,m|H_{2}^{\prime}|\rho,j^{\prime},\tau,m^{\prime}\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\sum_{\tilde{\rho}\neq\rho;\tilde{j}}\langle\rho,j,\tau,m|V|\tilde{\rho},\tilde{j},\tau\rangle\langle\tilde{\rho},\tilde{j},\tau|V|\rho,j^{\prime},\tau,m^{\prime}\rangle\left(\frac{1}{E_{\rho j\tau}-E_{\tilde{\rho}\tilde{j}\tau}}+\frac{1}{E_{\tilde{\rho}\tilde{j}\tau}-E_{\rho j^{\prime}\tau}}\right).

(501)

The O(
\Delta^{3}
) terms are found out to be

\displaystyle\langle\rho,j,\tau,m|H_{3}^{\prime}|\rho,j^{\prime},\tau,m^{\prime}\rangle=\frac{i}{2}\sum_{\tilde{\rho}\neq\rho;\tilde{j}}\left[\langle\rho,j,\tau,m|S_{2}|\tilde{\rho},\tilde{j},\tau\rangle\langle\tilde{\rho},\tilde{j},\tau|V|\rho,j^{\prime},\tau,m^{\prime}\rangle-\langle\rho,j,\tau,m|V|\tilde{\rho},\tilde{j},\tau\rangle\langle\tilde{\rho},\tilde{j},\tau|S_{2}|\rho,j^{\prime},\tau,m^{\prime}\rangle\right]

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{12}\sum_{\tilde{\rho}\neq\rho;\tilde{j}}\sum_{\bar{\rho}\neq\rho\tilde{\rho};\bar{j}}\left[\langle\rho,j,\tau,m|S_{1}|\bar{\rho},\bar{j},\tau\rangle\langle\bar{\rho},\bar{j},\tau|S_{1}|\tilde{\rho},\tilde{j},\tau\rangle\langle\tilde{\rho},\tilde{j},\tau|V|\rho,j^{\prime},\tau,m^{\prime}\rangle+\right.

\displaystyle\left.\langle\rho,j,\tau,m|V|\bar{\rho},\bar{j},\tau\rangle\langle\bar{\rho},\bar{j},\tau|S_{1}|\tilde{\rho},\tilde{j},\tau\rangle\langle\tilde{\rho},\tilde{j},\tau|S_{1}|\rho,j^{\prime},\tau,m^{\prime}\rangle-2\langle\rho,j,\tau,m|S_{1}|\bar{\rho},\bar{j},\tau\rangle\langle\bar{\rho},\bar{j},\tau|V|\tilde{\rho},\tilde{j},\tau\rangle\langle\tilde{\rho},\tilde{j},\tau|S_{1}|\rho,j^{\prime},\tau,m^{\prime}\rangle\right]

(502)

Substituting Eqs.(
496
) and (
498
) in Eqs.(
501
) and (
502
) and further substituting them and Eq.(
Supplementary note 10.1.1
)
in Eq.(
493
), upto O(
\Delta^{3}
), the effective Hamiltonian for
\rho=1
is given as

\displaystyle H^{eff}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}H^{\tau=+1}_{VP}&0\\
0&H^{\tau=-1}_{VP}\end{array}\right),

where

\displaystyle H^{\tau=+1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}-\frac{J}{2}+\hbar\omega_{c}^{(1)}aa^{\dagger}&i\frac{A^{(1)}}{\ell^{3}}a^{3}\\
-i\frac{A^{(1)}}{\ell^{3}}{a^{\dagger}}^{3}&-\frac{J}{2}+\hbar\omega_{c}^{(1)}a^{\dagger}a\end{array}\right)+M\left(1+\frac{M_{c}^{(1)}}{\ell^{2}}(aa^{\dagger}+a^{\dagger}a)\right)\sigma_{x},

where the Pauli matrix acts in the orbital space of
a\in\{4,3\}

c
-fermions. The cyclotron frequency
\omega_{c}^{\tau}
and the other coefficients above are

\displaystyle\hbar\omega_{c}^{(\tau)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{2v_{\star}^{2}}{\ell^{2}}\left(\frac{X_{\tau}^{2}}{N_{X\tau}(E_{\tau}-E_{X\tau})}+\frac{Y_{\tau}^{2}}{N_{Y\tau}(E_{\tau}-E_{Y\tau})}\right),

(509)

\displaystyle A^{(\tau)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 4\sqrt{2}v_{\star}^{2}v_{\star}^{\prime}\left(\frac{X_{\tau}Y_{\tau}(X_{\tau}+Y_{\tau})}{N_{X\tau}N_{Y\tau}(E_{\tau}-E_{X\tau})(E_{\tau}-E_{Y\tau})}+\frac{X_{\tau}^{3}}{N_{X\tau}^{2}(E_{\tau}-E_{X\tau})^{2}}+\frac{Y_{\tau}^{3}}{N_{Y\tau}^{2}(E_{\tau}-E_{Y\tau})^{2}}\right),

(510)

\displaystyle M_{c}^{(\tau)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-v_{\star}^{2}\left(\frac{X_{\tau}^{2}}{N_{X\tau}(E_{\tau}-E_{X\tau})^{2}}+\frac{Y^{2}_{\tau}}{N_{Y\tau}(E_{\tau}-E_{Y\tau})^{2}}\right).

(511)

H^{\tau=-1}_{VP}
can be obtained by replacing
a\leftrightarrow a^{\dagger}
,
\omega_{c}^{(1)},A^{(1)},M_{c}^{(1)}\rightarrow\omega_{c}^{(-1)},A^{(-1)},M_{c}^{(-1)}
and
-\frac{J}{2}\rightarrow\frac{J}{2}
in Eq.(
Supplementary note 10.1.1
). Substituting Eqs.(
470
)-(
474
) in the coefficients, we have
\ell^{2}\hbar\omega_{c}^{(\tau)}=-\tau(399586.49352)meV\mathring{A}^{2}
,
A^{(\tau)}=-\tau(42668446.86852)

meV\mathring{A}^{3}
and
M_{c}^{(\tau)}=-12847.14767\mathring{A}^{2}
.

The above effective Hamiltonians
H^{\tau}
describe the Landau quantization of the light single particle excitations towards CNP obtained for parent VP state. Clearly, the LLs emanate out of
-\tau J/2\pm M
. For the U(4) symmetric THFM (i.e.
M=0
), in the
\mathbf{B}\rightarrow 0
limit, we can the drop off-diagonal O(
\ell^{-3}
) terms in
H^{\tau}
. Then apart from the mode
\left(0,|0\rangle\right)^{T}
and
\left(|0\rangle,0\right)^{T}
at
\tau=+1
and
-1
, respectively, all other LLs come in degenerate pair of two.
This degeneracy at
\mathbf{B}\rightarrow 0
limit gets split as we tune back
M
. The comparison of the LL spectrum obtained using the above effective Hamiltonian with the one obtained via the exact calculation for valley sector
\mathbf{K}^{\prime}
is shown in Fig.(
13
). The LL basis used to generate the plot is
(0,|m_{1}\rangle,|m_{1}\rangle,0)^{T}
,
(|m_{2}\rangle,|m_{2}+3\rangle,|m_{2}+3\rangle,|m_{2}\rangle)^{T}
and
(|m_{3}\rangle,0,0,|m_{3}\rangle)^{T}
, where
m_{1}\in\{0,1,2\}
,
m_{2}\in\{0,\ldots,m_{max}-4\}
and
m_{3}\in\{m_{max}-3,m_{max}-2,m_{max}-1\}
respectively. We use
(|m_{3}\rangle,0,0,|m_{3}\rangle)^{T}
rather than
(|m_{3}\rangle,|m_{3}+3\rangle,|m_{3}+3\rangle,|m_{3}\rangle)^{T}
in order to avoid three modes emanating out of spurious
\mathbf{B}\rightarrow 0
energy
-\tau J/2
, for each
\tau
. These three spurious modes are present in the exact calculation, as can be seen in Fig.(
13
). However, recall that in practice we use
m_{max}=\lceil\frac{q-3}{2}\rceil
, so these three LLs are lost as
\mathbf{B}(q)
increases(decreases).

Supplementary note 10.1.2
Parent Kramers Intervalley Coherent State

Supplementary Figure 14:
The comparison of (b) LL spectrum obtained using the effective Hamiltonian (
\cdot
) with the exact calculation (
\cdot
) contrasted with the corresponding comparison at (a)
\mathbf{B}=0
for KIVC state above CNP. The spectrum below CNP is related by particle hole symmetry. For the comparison to be tractable we set
m_{max}=11
. Note that the above figure is spin degenerate.

In the case of the parent Kramers intervalley coherent (KIVC) state, the coupled
c
and
f
modes are described by the operator in Eq.(
Supplementary note 9.1
). Unlike in previous section, we now break the
\mathbf{B}=0
Hilbert space at
\Gamma
into 5 subspaces. The subspace of interest is the one spanned by states
|1,j,\tau\rangle
with
j\in\{1,2\}
and
\tau=\pm 1
, i.e. the one spanned by
a=\{3,4\}

c
fermions at each valley. The remaining four subspaces are spanned by
|2,j,+1\rangle
,
|2,j,-1\rangle
,
|3,j,+1\rangle
and
|3,j,-1\rangle
, where
j\in\{1,2\}
. These states are given in Eqs.(
464
)-(
469
). In the following discussion, we refer to these states and their energies by
|\epsilon,l\rangle
and
E_{\epsilon l}
, respectively. Here
\epsilon\in\{1,\ldots,5\}
labels the 5 subspaces. The index
l\in\{1,\ldots,4\}
for
\epsilon=1
and
l\in\{1,2\}
for
\epsilon=\{3,4,5\}
, labels the states spanning the subspace
\epsilon
. We promote these states to
\mathbf{B}\neq 0
as
|\epsilon,l,m\rangle=|\epsilon,l\rangle|m\rangle
, where
|m\rangle
is the
m^{th}
LL, i.e. the finite
\mathbf{B}

\mathbf{k}\cdot\mathbf{p}
basis. Using the SWT procedure introduced in the previous section, we eliminate the terms in the operator given in Eq.(
Supplementary note 9.1
) which can mix between these subspaces to obtain an effective Hamiltonian for the subspace spanned by by states
|\epsilon=1,l,m\rangle
.

To begin with, we re-write the operator in Eq.(
Supplementary note 9.1
) with respect to the above
\mathbf{B}=0
eigenstates at
\Gamma
as

\displaystyle H=H_{0}+\Delta V,

(512)

where
H_{0}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}H_{0}^{\tau=+1}&\\
&H_{0}^{\tau=-1}\end{array}\right)
and
V=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\bar{V}^{\tau=+1}&V_{M}\\
h.c.&\bar{V}^{\tau=-1}\end{array}\right)
.

H_{0}^{\tau}
is given in Eq.(
Supplementary note 10.1.1
). The intra-valley perturbation
\bar{V}^{\tau=+1}
can be obtained by setting
M=0
in Eq.(
Supplementary note 10.1.1
). We can obtain
\bar{V}^{\tau=-1}
by replacing
X_{1},Y_{1}\rightarrow X_{-1},Y_{-1}
,
N_{X1},N_{Y1}\rightarrow N_{X-1},N_{Y-1}
and
a\leftrightarrow a^{\dagger}
in
V^{\tau=+1}
. The inter-valley perturbation
V_{M}
is given as

\displaystyle V_{M}=\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}M\sigma_{z}&0&0\\
0&0&0\\
0&0&0\end{array}\right)

The effective Hamiltonian can then be expanded in orders of
\Delta
, as given in Eq.(
493
). Following the condition that the transformation decouples each subspace, we have

\displaystyle\langle\epsilon,l,m|H_{n}^{\prime}|\epsilon^{\prime},l^{\prime},m^{\prime}\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\delta_{\epsilon,\epsilon^{\prime}}\langle\epsilon,l,m|H_{n}^{\prime}|\epsilon,l^{\prime},m^{\prime}\rangle

(517)

For
\epsilon\neq\epsilon^{\prime}
and substituting
n=1
in
H_{n}^{\prime}
gives

\displaystyle\langle\epsilon,l,m|\left(V+i[S_{1},H_{0}]\right)|\epsilon^{\prime},l^{\prime},m^{\prime}\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 0

(518)

\displaystyle\implies\langle\epsilon,l,m|S_{1}|\epsilon^{\prime},l^{\prime},m^{\prime}\rangle=\begin{cases}i\frac{\langle\epsilon,l,m|V|\epsilon^{\prime},l^{\prime},m^{\prime}\rangle}{E_{\epsilon^{\prime}l^{\prime}}-E_{\epsilon l}}&\text{for}\penalty\ \penalty\ \epsilon\neq\epsilon^{\prime},\\
0&\text{for}\penalty\ \penalty\ \epsilon=\epsilon^{\prime},\end{cases}

(519)

For
\epsilon\neq\epsilon^{\prime}
and substituting
n=2
in
H_{n}^{\prime}
gives

\displaystyle\langle\epsilon,l,m|\left(i[S_{2},H_{0}]+i[S_{1},V]+\frac{i^{2}}{2!}[S_{1},[S_{1},H_{0}]]\right)|\epsilon^{\prime},l^{\prime},m^{\prime}\rangle=0

(520)

\displaystyle\implies\langle\epsilon,l,m|S_{2}|\epsilon^{\prime},l^{\prime},m^{\prime}\rangle=\frac{1}{E_{\epsilon l}-E_{\epsilon^{\prime}l^{\prime}}}\left(\sum_{\tilde{\epsilon}\neq\epsilon;\tilde{l};\tilde{m}}\langle\epsilon,l,m|S_{1}|\tilde{\epsilon},\tilde{l},\tilde{m}\rangle\langle\tilde{\epsilon},\tilde{l},\tilde{m}|V|\epsilon^{\prime},l^{\prime},m^{\prime}\rangle-\right.

\displaystyle\left.\sum_{\tilde{\epsilon}\neq\epsilon^{\prime};\tilde{l};\tilde{m}}\langle\epsilon,l,m|V|\tilde{\epsilon},\tilde{l},\tilde{m}\rangle\langle\tilde{\epsilon},\tilde{l},\tilde{m}|S_{1}|\epsilon^{\prime},l^{\prime},m^{\prime}\rangle\right.

\displaystyle-\left.\sum_{\tilde{\epsilon}\neq\epsilon\epsilon^{\prime};\tilde{l},\tilde{m}}\left(E_{\tilde{\epsilon}\tilde{l}}-\frac{E_{\epsilon l}+E_{\epsilon^{\prime}l^{\prime}}}{2}\right)\langle\epsilon,l,m|S_{1}|\tilde{\epsilon},\tilde{l},,\tilde{m}\rangle\langle\tilde{\epsilon},\tilde{l},\tilde{m}|S_{1}|\epsilon^{\prime},l^{\prime},m^{\prime}\rangle\right)\text{for}\penalty\ \penalty\ \epsilon\neq\epsilon^{\prime},

\displaystyle\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ =0\penalty\ \penalty\ \text{for}\penalty\ \penalty\ \epsilon=\epsilon^{\prime},

(521)

As in the previous section, we further assume that

\displaystyle\sum_{\tilde{m}}|\tilde{\epsilon},\tilde{l},\tilde{m}\rangle\langle\tilde{\epsilon},\tilde{l},\tilde{m}|=|\tilde{\epsilon},\tilde{l}\rangle\langle\tilde{\epsilon},\tilde{l}|,

(522)

which is justified in the (
\mathbf{k}\cdot\mathbf{p}
) continuum limit, as the upper cutoff on LLs in such a limit is unbounded. Substituting Eqs.(
519
) and (
521
) into the expansion in Eq.(
493
), we can now obtain the effective Hamiltonian in orders of
\Delta
. The O(
\Delta^{0}
) contribution to the effective Hamiltonian in subspace
\epsilon
is trivially
E_{\epsilon l}\delta_{ll^{\prime}}
and O(
\Delta^{1}
) terms are

\displaystyle\langle\epsilon,l,m|H_{1}^{\prime}|\epsilon,l^{\prime},m^{\prime}\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\langle\epsilon,l,m|V|\epsilon,l^{\prime},m^{\prime}\rangle.

(523)

The O(
\Delta^{2}
) terms are found out to be

\displaystyle\langle\epsilon,l,m|H_{2}^{\prime}|\epsilon,l^{\prime},m^{\prime}\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\sum_{\tilde{\epsilon}\neq\epsilon;\tilde{l}}\langle\epsilon,l,m|V|\tilde{\epsilon},\tilde{l}\rangle\langle\tilde{\epsilon},\tilde{l}|V|\epsilon,l^{\prime},m^{\prime}\rangle\left(\frac{1}{E_{\epsilon l}-E_{\tilde{\epsilon}\tilde{l}}}+\frac{1}{E_{\tilde{\epsilon}\tilde{l}}-E_{\epsilon l^{\prime}}}\right).

(524)

The O(
\Delta^{3}
) terms are found out to be

\displaystyle\langle\epsilon,l,m|H_{3}^{\prime}|\epsilon,l^{\prime},m^{\prime}\rangle=\frac{i}{2}\sum_{\tilde{\epsilon}\neq\epsilon;\tilde{l}}\left[\langle\epsilon,l,m|S_{2}|\tilde{\epsilon},\tilde{l}\rangle\langle\tilde{\epsilon},\tilde{l}|V|\epsilon,l^{\prime},m^{\prime}\rangle-\langle\epsilon,l,m|V|\tilde{\epsilon},\tilde{l}\rangle\langle\tilde{\epsilon},\tilde{l}|S_{2}|\epsilon,l^{\prime},m^{\prime}\rangle\right]

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{12}\sum_{\tilde{\epsilon}\neq\epsilon;\tilde{l}}\sum_{\bar{\epsilon}\neq\epsilon\tilde{\epsilon};\bar{l}}\left[\langle\epsilon,l,m|S_{1}|\bar{\epsilon},\bar{l}\rangle\langle\bar{\epsilon},\bar{l}|S_{1}|\tilde{\epsilon},\tilde{l}\rangle\langle\tilde{\epsilon},\tilde{l}|V|\epsilon,l^{\prime},m^{\prime}\rangle+\right.

\displaystyle\left.\langle\epsilon,l,m|V|\bar{\epsilon},\bar{l}\rangle\langle\bar{\epsilon},\bar{l}|S_{1}|\tilde{\epsilon},\tilde{l}\rangle\langle\tilde{\epsilon},\tilde{l}|S_{1}|\epsilon,l^{\prime},m^{\prime}\rangle-2\langle\epsilon,l,m|S_{1}|\bar{\epsilon},\bar{l}\rangle\langle\bar{\epsilon},\bar{l}|V|\tilde{\epsilon},\tilde{l}\rangle\langle\tilde{\epsilon},\tilde{l}|S_{1}|\epsilon,l^{\prime},m^{\prime}\rangle\right]

(525)

Up to the O(
\frac{1}{\ell^{3}}
), the effective Hamiltonian for subspace
\epsilon=1
is found to be

\displaystyle H^{eff}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}H^{\tau=+1}_{\text{KIVC}}&H_{M}\\
h.c.&H^{\tau=-1}_{\text{KIVC}}\end{array}\right),

where
H^{\tau}_{\text{KIVC}}
can be obtained by setting
M=0
in the expressions for
H^{\tau}_{VP}
in Eq.(
Supplementary note 10.1.1
). The matrix
H_{M}
couples both the valleys and is given as

\displaystyle H_{M}=M\left(1+\frac{M_{c1}}{\ell^{2}}a^{\dagger}a+\frac{M_{c2}}{\ell^{2}}aa^{\dagger}\right)\sigma_{z}

(529)

where the Pauli matrix acts in the orbital space of
a\in\{4,3\}

c
-fermions and

\displaystyle M_{c1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle v_{\star}^{2}\left(\frac{X_{-1}^{2}}{N_{X{-1}}(E_{1}-E_{X{-1}})(E_{X{-1}}-E_{-1})}+\frac{Y_{-1}^{2}}{N_{Y{-1}}(E_{1}-E_{Y-1})(E_{Y-1}-E_{-1})}\right),

(530)

\displaystyle M_{c2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle v_{\star}^{2}\left(\frac{X_{1}^{2}}{N_{X{1}}(E_{1}-E_{X{1}})(E_{X{1}}-E_{-1})}+\frac{Y_{1}^{2}}{N_{Y{1}}(E_{1}-E_{Y1})(E_{Y1}-E_{-1})}\right).

(531)

Substituting Eqs.(
470
)-(
474
) above we get
M_{c1}=M_{c2}=-20880.79644\mathring{A}^{2}
. Clearly the LLs emanate out of
\mathbf{B}\rightarrow 0
energy
\pm\sqrt{M^{2}+J^{2}/4}
.
In the
\mathbf{B}\rightarrow 0
limit, we can drop the O(
\frac{1}{\ell^{3}}
) terms in the effective Hamiltonian. The LL energies in this limit take the form
\pm E^{\pm}_{n}
, where
E^{\pm}_{n}=\sqrt{(\frac{J}{2}+\hbar\omega^{(-1)}_{c}(n+\frac{1}{2}))^{2}+M^{2}(1+\frac{M_{c1}}{\ell^{2}}(2n+1))^{2}}\pm\frac{\hbar\omega^{(-1)}_{c}}{2}
, and
n\in\{0,1,2,\dots\}
. For small LL index
n
, we further have
E^{-}_{n+1}\approx E^{+}_{n}
.
Thus except the anomalous mode
\pm E^{-}_{0}
, the leading LLs appear in pairs approaching double degeneracy, as can be seen in Fig.(
14
) where we compare the LL spectrum obtained using the above effective Hamiltonian with the one obtained via the exact calculation. Note that such an approximate degeneracy is absent in the spectrum for VP state (see Fig.(
13
)).

The LL basis used to generate the plot is
(0,|m_{1}\rangle,|m_{1}\rangle,0)^{T}
,
(|m_{2}\rangle,|m_{2}+3\rangle,|m_{2}+3\rangle,|m_{2}\rangle)^{T}
and
(|m_{3}\rangle,0,0,|m_{3}\rangle)^{T}
, where
m_{1}\in\{0,1,2\}
,
m_{2}\in\{0,\ldots,m_{max}-4\}
and
m_{3}\in\{m_{max}-3,m_{max}-2,m_{max}-1\}
respectively. We use

. These six spurious modes are present in the exact calculation, as can be seen in Fig.(

, so these three LLs are lost as
\mathbf{B}(q)
increases(decreases).

We break the
\mathbf{B}=0
Hilbert space at
\Gamma

and

for

m^{th}

\mathbf{B}

\mathbf{k}\cdot\mathbf{p}

) which can mix the subspaces, to obtain an effective Hamiltonian for subspace

To begin with, we rewrite the operators in Eqs.(

) with respect to the states given in Eqs.(

)-(

\displaystyle H=H_{0}+\Delta V,

where

is an artificial parameter that helps us keeping track of the orders in perturbation, to which we compute the effective Hamiltonian and is later set to 1. Along with
M
, we treat all the terms in operator in Eq.(
Supplementary note 6.3
) which can mix the subspaces as perturbation
V
. The unperturbed part is given as

\displaystyle H_{0}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cccccc}E_{1}&0&0&0&0&0\\

0&0&0&E_{Y}&0&0\\
0&0&0&0&E_{V}&0\\

The perturbation

is given as

\displaystyle V=\left(\begin{array}[]{cccccc}0&M&i\frac{\sqrt{2}}{\ell}\frac{Xv_{\star}}{\sqrt{N_{X}}}a^{\dagger}&i\frac{\sqrt{2}}{\ell}\frac{Yv_{\star}}{\sqrt{N_{Y}}}a^{\dagger}&0&0\\
&0&0&0&-i\frac{\sqrt{2}}{\ell}\frac{Vv_{\star}}{\sqrt{N_{V}}}a&-i\frac{\sqrt{2}}{\ell}\frac{Zv_{\star}}{\sqrt{N_{Z}}}a\\
&&0&0&i\frac{\sqrt{2}}{\ell}\frac{(X+V)v_{\star}^{\prime}}{\sqrt{N_{X}N_{V}}}a^{\dagger}&i\frac{\sqrt{2}}{\ell}\frac{(X+Z)v_{\star}^{\prime}}{\sqrt{N_{X}N_{Z}}}a^{\dagger}\\
&&&0&i\frac{\sqrt{2}}{\ell}\frac{(Y+V)v_{\star}^{\prime}}{\sqrt{N_{Y}N_{V}}}a^{\dagger}&i\frac{\sqrt{2}}{\ell}\frac{(Y+Z)v_{\star}^{\prime}}{\sqrt{N_{Y}N_{Z}}}a^{\dagger}\\
&&&&0&0\\
h.c.&&&&&0\end{array}\right),

Following the SWT procedure in Sec.(

), i.e. the steps from Eq.(

)-(

(566)

\displaystyle+M\left(1+\frac{\bar{M}_{c}}{\ell^{2}}aa^{\dagger}+\frac{\tilde{M}_{c}}{\ell^{2}}a^{\dagger}a\right)\sigma_{x},

c

\displaystyle 2\sqrt{2}v_{\star}^{2}v_{\star}^{\prime}\left(\frac{XV(X+V)}{12N_{X}N_{V}}\left(\frac{1}{(E_{V}-E_{X})(E_{2}-E_{V})}+\frac{1}{(E_{V}-E_{X})(E_{X}-E_{1})}-\frac{2}{(E_{X}-E_{1})(E_{2}-E_{V})}\right)\right)+

\displaystyle 2\sqrt{2}v_{\star}^{2}v_{\star}^{\prime}\left(\frac{YV(Y+V)}{12N_{Y}N_{V}}\left(\frac{1}{(E_{V}-E_{Y})(E_{2}-E_{V})}+\frac{1}{(E_{V}-E_{Y})(E_{Y}-E_{1})}-\frac{2}{(E_{Y}-E_{1})(E_{2}-E_{V})}\right)\right)+

\displaystyle 2\sqrt{2}v_{\star}^{2}v_{\star}^{\prime}\left(\frac{XZ(X+Z)}{12N_{X}N_{Z}}\left(\frac{1}{(E_{Z}-E_{X})(E_{2}-E_{Z})}+\frac{1}{(E_{Z}-E_{X})(E_{X}-E_{1})}-\frac{2}{(E_{X}-E_{1})(E_{2}-E_{Z})}\right)\right)+

\displaystyle 2\sqrt{2}v_{\star}^{2}v_{\star}^{\prime}\left(\frac{YZ(Y+Z)}{12N_{Y}N_{Z}}\left(\frac{1}{(E_{Z}-E_{Y})(E_{2}-E_{Z})}+\frac{1}{(E_{Z}-E_{Y})(E_{Y}-E_{1})}-\frac{2}{(E_{Y}-E_{1})(E_{2}-E_{Z})}\right)\right)+

\displaystyle-\frac{\sqrt{2}v_{\star}^{2}v_{\star}^{\prime}XV(X+V)}{N_{X}N_{V}(E_{1}-E_{V})}\left(\frac{1}{E_{X}-E_{1}}-\frac{1}{E_{V}-E_{X}}+\frac{E_{X}-\frac{E_{1}+E_{V}}{2}}{(E_{X}-E_{1})(E_{V}-E_{X})}\right)+

\displaystyle-\frac{\sqrt{2}v_{\star}^{2}v_{\star}^{\prime}YZ(Y+Z)}{N_{Y}N_{Z}(E_{1}-E_{Z})}\left(\frac{1}{E_{Y}-E_{1}}-\frac{1}{E_{Z}-E_{Y}}+\frac{E_{Y}-\frac{E_{1}+E_{Z}}{2}}{(E_{Y}-E_{1})(E_{Z}-E_{Y})}\right)+

\displaystyle\frac{\sqrt{2}v_{\star}^{2}v_{\star}^{\prime}YV(Y+V)}{N_{Y}N_{V}(E_{Y}-E_{2})}\left(\frac{1}{E_{V}-E_{Y}}-\frac{1}{E_{2}-E_{V}}+\frac{E_{V}-\frac{E_{2}+E_{Y}}{2}}{(E_{V}-E_{Y})(E_{2}-E_{V})}\right)+

\displaystyle\frac{\sqrt{2}v_{\star}^{2}v_{\star}^{\prime}YZ(Y+Z)}{N_{Y}N_{Z}(E_{Y}-E_{2})}\left(\frac{1}{E_{Z}-E_{Y}}-\frac{1}{E_{2}-E_{Z}}+\frac{E_{Z}-\frac{E_{2}+E_{Y}}{2}}{(E_{Z}-E_{Y})(E_{2}-E_{Z})}\right).

(|m_{3}\rangle,0)^{T}
, where
m_{1}\in\{0,1,2\}
,
m_{2}\in\{0,\ldots,m_{max}-4\}
and
m_{3}\in\{m_{max}-3,m_{max}-2,m_{max}-1\}
respectively. We use
(|m_{3}\rangle,0)^{T}
rather than
(|m_{3}\rangle,|m_{3}+3\rangle)^{T}
in order to avoid three modes emanating out of spurious
\mathbf{B}\rightarrow 0
energy
-(W_{3}+J/2)
. These three spurious modes are present in the exact calculation, as can be seen in Fig.(
16
). However, recall that in practice we use
m_{max}=\lceil\frac{q-3}{2}\rceil
, so these three LLs are lost as
\mathbf{B}(q)
increases(decreases).

Parent Valley Polarized State

In this section we discuss the derivation of the case of
\mathbf{B}\neq 0
effective Hamiltonian for the spin and valley sector
s=\uparrow,\downarrow
,
\mathbf{K}
for the case of parent VP state at
\nu=-2
. As discussed in Sec.(
Supplementary note 6.4
), for the above mean-field sector in flat band limit
M=0
, the magnetic subbands within the narrow bands emanate out of the
\mathbf{B}\rightarrow 0
energy
-2W_{3}-\zeta_{s}\frac{J}{2}
, where
\zeta_{s}=1,-1
for

respectively. The remote subbands on the other hand emanate out of the

energy

\frac{1}{2}\left(-2W_{1}-(12U_{2}+\frac{4+\zeta_{s}}{2}U_{1})\pm\sqrt{4\gamma^{2}+(2W_{1}-(12U_{2}+\frac{4+\zeta_{s}}{2}U_{1}))^{2}}\right)

\mathbf{B}=0
THFM at
\Gamma
at
\nu=-2
for the same mean-field sector. Recall that at
\Gamma
, it has the form
Song and Bernevig 2022

\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}-2W_{1}\sigma_{0}&0&\gamma\sigma_{0}\\
&-(2W_{3}+\zeta_{s}J/2)\sigma_{0}&0\\
h.c.&&-(12U_{2}+\frac{4+\zeta_{s}}{2}U_{1})\sigma_{0}\end{array}\right)
, where Pauli matrix
\sigma_{0}
acts in orbital space. The corresponding eigenvectors for given spin

can be listed as

\displaystyle|1,1,s\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(0,0,0,1,0,0\right)^{T},

(594)

\displaystyle|1,2,s\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(0,0,1,0,0,0\right)^{T},

(595)

\displaystyle|2,1,s\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{N_{Xs}}}\left(0,X_{s},0,0,0,1\right)^{T},

(596)

\displaystyle|2,2,s\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{N_{Xs}}}\left(X_{s},0,0,0,1,0\right)^{T},

(597)

\displaystyle|3,1,s\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{N_{Ys}}}\left(0,Y_{s},0,0,0,1\right)^{T},

(598)

\displaystyle|3,2,s\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{N_{Ys}}}\left(Y_{s},0,0,0,1,0\right)^{T},

(599)

where

\displaystyle X_{s}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2\gamma}\left(2W_{1}-(12U_{2}+\frac{4+\zeta_{s}}{2}U_{1})+\sqrt{4\gamma^{2}+(2W_{1}-(12U_{2}+\frac{4+\zeta_{s}}{2}U_{1}))^{2}}\right),

(600)

\displaystyle Y_{s}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2\gamma}\left(2W_{1}-(12U_{2}+\frac{4+\zeta_{s}}{2}U_{1})-\sqrt{4\gamma^{2}+(2W_{1}-(12U_{2}+\frac{4+\zeta_{s}}{2}U_{1}))^{2}}\right),

(601)

and normalizations

,

. The energy of state

and

,

\displaystyle E_{1,j,s}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-(2W_{3}+\zeta_{s}\frac{J}{2})\equiv E_{s},

(602)

\displaystyle E_{2,j,s}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\left(-2W_{1}-(12U_{2}+\frac{4+\zeta_{s}}{2}U_{1})-\sqrt{4\gamma^{2}+(2W_{1}-(12U_{2}+\frac{4+\zeta_{s}}{2}U_{1}))^{2}}\right)\equiv E_{Xs},

(603)

\displaystyle E_{3,j,s}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\left(-2W_{1}-(12U_{2}+\frac{4+\zeta_{s}}{2}U_{1})+\sqrt{4\gamma^{2}+(2W_{1}-(12U_{2}+\frac{4+\zeta_{s}}{2}U_{1}))^{2}}\right)\equiv E_{Ys}.

(604)

We break the
\mathbf{B}=0
Hilbert space at
\Gamma
into 3 subspaces for each
s
. These three subspaces are spanned by the states
|1,j,s\rangle
,
|2,j,s\rangle
and
|3,j,s\rangle

\mathbf{B}\neq 0
as
|\rho,j,s,m\rangle=|\rho,j,s\rangle|m\rangle
, where
|m\rangle
is the
m^{th}
LL, i.e. the finite
\mathbf{B}

\mathbf{k}\cdot\mathbf{p}
basis. Using the SWT procedure introduced in Sec.(
Supplementary note 10.1.1
), we eliminate all the terms in the operator in Eq.(
Supplementary note 6.4
) that can mix the subspaces to obtain an effective Hamiltonian for the subspace spanned by states
|1,j,s,m\rangle
, i.e. the one spanned by
a=\{3,4\}

c
fermions.

We first re-write the operator in Eq.(
Supplementary note 6.4
) with respect to the eigenstates in Eqs.(

)-(
599
) as

\displaystyle H=H_{0}+\Delta V,

(605)

where

\displaystyle H_{0}=\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}E_{s}\sigma_{0}&0&0\\
0&E_{Xs}\sigma_{0}&0\\
0&0&E_{Ys}\sigma_{0}\end{array}\right),

where Pauli matrix
\sigma_{0}
acts in the
j
space for each
\rho
. Along with
M
, we treat all the terms in operator in Eq.(
Supplementary note 6.4
) which can mix the subspaces as perturbation
V
.
\Delta
is an artificial parameter that helps us keeping track of the orders in perturbation, to which we compute the effective Hamiltonian and is later set to 1. The matrix
V
can be obtained by replacing
X_{1}
,
Y_{1}
,
N_{X1}
and
N_{Y1}
in Eq.(
Supplementary note 10.1.1

,

,

and

), i.e. from Eq.(

)-(

) and replacing

by
|\rho,j,s,m\rangle
in these steps, the effective Hamiltonian for
\rho=1
and spin

is found to be

\displaystyle H^{\tau=1,s=\uparrow,\downarrow,\nu=-2}_{VP}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}-(2W_{3}+\zeta_{s}\frac{J}{2})+\hbar\omega_{c}^{(s)}aa^{\dagger}&i\frac{A^{(s)}}{\ell^{3}}a^{3}\\
-i\frac{A^{(s)}}{\ell^{3}}{a^{\dagger}}^{3}&-(2W_{3}+\zeta_{s}\frac{J}{2})+\hbar\omega_{c}^{(s)}a^{\dagger}a\end{array}\right)+M\left(1+\frac{M_{c}^{(s)}}{\ell^{2}}(aa^{\dagger}+a^{\dagger}a)\right)\sigma_{x},

where the Pauli matrix acts in the orbital space of
a\in\{4,3\}

c
-fermions. The cyclotron frequency
\omega_{c}^{(s)}
and the other coefficients above are

\displaystyle\hbar\omega_{c}^{(s)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{2v_{\star}^{2}}{\ell^{2}}\left(\frac{X_{s}^{2}}{N_{Xs}(E_{s}-E_{Xs})}+\frac{Y_{s}^{2}}{N_{Ys}(E_{s}-E_{Ys})}\right),

(613)

\displaystyle A^{(s)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 4\sqrt{2}v_{\star}^{2}v_{\star}^{\prime}\left(\frac{X_{s}Y_{s}(X_{s}+Y_{s})}{N_{Xs}N_{Ys}(E_{s}-E_{Xs})(E_{s}-E_{Ys})}+\frac{X_{s}^{3}}{N_{Xs}^{2}(E_{s}-E_{Xs})^{2}}+\frac{Y_{s}^{3}}{N_{Ys}^{2}(E_{s}-E_{Ys})^{2}}\right),

(614)

\displaystyle M_{c}^{(s)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-v_{\star}^{2}\left(\frac{X_{s}^{2}}{N_{Xs}(E_{s}-E_{Xs})^{2}}+\frac{Y^{2}_{s}}{N_{Ys}(E_{s}-E_{Ys})^{2}}\right).

(615)

Substituting Eqs.(

)-(

) in the coefficients, we have

,
\ell^{2}\hbar\omega_{c}^{(\downarrow)}=-514097.23133

meV\mathring{A}^{2}
,

,
A^{(\downarrow)}=-59278877.34125

meV\mathring{A}^{3}
,
M_{c}^{(\uparrow)}=-13211.61944\mathring{A}^{2}
and
M_{c}^{(\downarrow)}=-15952.11343\mathring{A}^{2}
. For the U(4) symmetric THFM (i.e.
M=0
), in the
\mathbf{B}\rightarrow 0
limit, we can the drop off-diagonal O(
\ell^{-3}
) terms in in the above effective Hamiltonian. Then apart from the mode
\left(0,|0\rangle\right)^{T}

This degeneracy at
\mathbf{B}\rightarrow 0
limit gets split as we tune back
