Supplementary note 11
Review of Magnetic Bloch’s Theorem at
2\pi
Flux

In the following two appendices we detail the gauge-invariant technique of calculating the strong-coupling spectrum of TBG in flux.
We first review our technique of calculating the band structures and topology of twisted bilayer graphene with
2\pi
flux per unit cell, as derived in
Herzog-Arbeitman et al. 2022a
;
Herzog-Arbeitman et al. 2022b
, with the eventual goal of extending the
2\pi
formalism to rational flux
\phi=2\pi p/q
. Compared to other techniques, our method explicitly defines gauge-invariant
momentum eigenstates

\ket{\kk,m}
that are simultaneous eigenkets of magnetic translation operators with the same formal properties as Bloch states. By keeping the full momentum quantum numbers and manifestly preserving the lattice symmetries, we are able to leverage the machinery of band topology, such as Wilson loops and the quantum metric tensor as will be explored in future work, in Hofstadter systems at rational flux.

To add nonzero flux to the BM model, we perform canonical substitution:
-i{\partial}\rightarrow{{\bm{\pi}}}
, with
{\bm{\pi}}
the canonical momentum yielding the operators

\displaystyle\pi_{\mu}

\displaystyle=-i\partial_{\mu}-eA_{\mu},\penalty\ \penalty\ Q_{\mu}=\pi_{\mu}-eB\epsilon_{\mu\nu}x_{\nu}.

(639)

where
{\bf A}
is the vector potential
\boldsymbol{\nabla}\times{\bf A}=B{\hat{z}}
, and
Q_{\mu}
is the guiding center operator obeying
[\pi_{\mu},Q_{\nu}]=0
. We define the Landau level operators
a,b
as

\displaystyle a

\displaystyle=\dfrac{\pi_{x}+i\pi_{y}}{\sqrt{2B}},\penalty\ \penalty\ a^{\dagger}=\dfrac{\pi_{x}-i\pi_{y}}{\sqrt{2B}},\penalty\ \penalty\ [a,a^{\dagger}]=1,

(640)

\displaystyle b

\displaystyle=\dfrac{(\mathbf{L}_{1}-i\mathbf{L}_{2})\cdot{\bf Q}}{\sqrt{2\phi}},\penalty\ \penalty\ b^{\dagger}=\dfrac{(\mathbf{L}_{1}+i\mathbf{L}_{2})\cdot{\bf Q}}{\sqrt{2\phi}},\penalty\ \penalty\ [b,b^{\dagger}]=1\ .

(641)

In magnetic flux, the translation operators
T_{\bf{R}}
which commute with the Hamiltonian are

\displaystyle{\hat{t}}_{{\bf{R}}}=e^{i{\bf{R}}\cdot{\bf Q}}

(642)

where
\mathbf{R}
is a lattice vector. Because at
2\pi
flux the magnetic translation operators
T_{\mathbf{L}_{1}},T_{\mathbf{L}_{2}}
commute with each other and the Hamiltonian
H_{\text{BM}}
, one can label the eigenstates of
H_{\text{BM}}
by the momentum carried by the operators
T_{\mathbf{L}_{1}},T_{\mathbf{L}_{2}}
. A gauge-invariant definition of the momentum eigenstates was given in
Herzog-Arbeitman et al. 2022a
as

\displaystyle\ket{\kk, m}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{{\cal N}(k_{1},k_{2})}}\sum_{{\bf{R}}}e^{-2\pi i{\bf{k}}\cdot{\bf{R}}}T_{\mathbf{L}_{1}}^{R_{1}}T_{\mathbf{L}_{2}}^{R_{2}}\ket{m},\qquad\langle{\mathbf{k},m|\mathbf{k}^{\prime},n}\rangle=\delta(\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{k}^{\prime})\delta_{mn}\ .

(643)

Here momentum is
{\bf{k}}=k_{1}\mathbf{g}_{1}+k_{2}\mathbf{g}_{2}
,
k_{i}={\bf{k}}\cdot\mathbf{L}_{i}\in[0,1)
, and
{\bf{R}}=R_{1}\mathbf{L}_{1}+R_{2}\mathbf{L}_{2}
is summed over all integers
R_{1},R_{2}
. The ket
\ket{m}
is the
m
th Landau level

\displaystyle\ket{m}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{m!}}a^{{\dagger}m}\ket{0}

(644)

and
{\cal N}(k_{1},k_{2})
is the normalization factor, worked out to be
Herzog-Arbeitman et al. 2022a

\displaystyle{\cal N}(k_{1},k_{2})={\vartheta}\left(k_{1},k_{2}\bigg|\Phi\right)=\sqrt{2}e^{-\pi(k_{1}^{2}+k_{2}^{2}-2ik_{1}k_{2})}\theta\left(ik_{1}+k_{2}\bigg|i\right)\theta\left(k_{1}+ik_{2}\bigg|i\right).

(645)

Here
\vartheta
denotes the Siegel theta function and
\Phi
the Riemann matrix given by

\displaystyle\vartheta(\mathbf{k}|M)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\mathbf{R}}e^{2\pi^{2}\mathbf{k}\cdot M\cdot\mathbf{k}+2\pi i\mathbf{k}\cdot\mathbf{R}},\qquad\Phi=\frac{i}{2}\begin{bmatrix}1&i\\
i&1\end{bmatrix}.

(646)

In Eq.(
645
), we have simplified the Siegel theta into a product of Jacobi theta functions
\theta
. There is a zero in the normalization at
{\bf{k}}=(\pi,\pi)
which is protected by the Chern number of the Landau level states
\ket{\mbf{k},m}
.

Within this basis, one may calculate the matrix elements at momentum
{\bf{k}}
. The matrix elements read
Herzog-Arbeitman et al. 2022a

\displaystyle\langle{{\bf{k}},m|a^{\dagger}a|{\bf{k}}^{\prime},n}\rangle

\displaystyle=\delta({\bf{k}}-{\bf{k}}^{\prime})n\delta_{mn}

(647)

\displaystyle\langle{{\bf{k}},m|e^{-i{\bf{q}}\cdot{\bf{r}}}|{\bf{k}}^{\prime},n}\rangle

\displaystyle=\delta({\bf{k}}-{\bf{k}}^{\prime}-{\bf{q}})e^{i\xi_{\bf{q}}({\bf{k}})}{\cal H}_{mn}^{\bf{q}},

(648)

where the form factor matrix and phases are defined as

\displaystyle e^{i\xi_{\bf{q}}({\bf{k}})}

\displaystyle=\dfrac{e^{-\frac{q{\bar{q}}}{4\phi}}\vartheta\left(\dfrac{(k_{1}-q/2,k_{2}+iq/2)}{2\pi}\bigg|\Phi\right)}{\sqrt{\vartheta\left(\dfrac{(k_{1},k_{2})}{2\pi}\bigg|\Phi\right)\vartheta\left(\dfrac{(k_{1}-q_{1},k_{2}-q_{2})}{2\pi}\bigg|\Phi\right)}},\penalty\ \penalty\ q=q_{1}+iq_{2},\penalty\ \penalty\ q_{j}={\bf{q}}\cdot\mathbf{L}_{j}

(649)

\displaystyle{\cal H}_{mn}^{\bf{q}}

\displaystyle=\langle{m|e^{i\frac{\gamma_{q}a+{\bar{\gamma}}_{q}a^{\dagger}}{\sqrt{2\phi}}}|n}\rangle,\penalty\ \penalty\ \gamma_{q}=\epsilon_{ij}q_{i}{\bar{z}}_{j},\penalty\ \penalty\ {\bar{z}}_{j}=\dfrac{({\hat{x}}-i{\hat{y}})\cdot\mathbf{L}_{j}}{\sqrt{\Omega}},

(650)

with
\Omega=|\mathbf{a}_{1}\times\mathbf{a}_{2}|
the area of the unit cell. These matrix elements have been worked out in detail in
Herzog-Arbeitman et al. 2022a
, and the momentum Landau levels form a complete orthonormal basis. To evaluate the band structure of the BM model in flux, one simply evaluates the matrix elements of the Hamiltonian with respect to this basis at any momentum. Being able to block diagonalize the Hamiltonian by momentum allows for efficient calculations as well as access to the Wilson loop and quantum geometric tensor.

Supplementary note 12
Rational Flux

To solve the Bistritzer MacDonald model (or other continuum models) at rational flux
\phi=2\pi p/q
, we will create new momentum eigenstates that reflect the projective representation of the translation group in flux. At flux
\phi=2\pi p/q
, we generalize Eq.(
641
) to the following raising and lowering operators

\displaystyle a

\displaystyle=\dfrac{\pi_{x}+i\pi_{y}}{\sqrt{2B}},\penalty\ \penalty\ a^{\dagger}=\dfrac{\pi_{x}-i\pi_{y}}{\sqrt{2B}}

(651)

\displaystyle b

\displaystyle=\dfrac{(\mathbf{L}_{1}/p-iq\mathbf{L}_{2})\cdot\mathbf{Q}}{\sqrt{4\pi}},\penalty\ \penalty\ b^{\dagger}=\dfrac{(\mathbf{L}_{1}/p+iq\mathbf{L}_{2})\cdot\mathbf{Q}}{\sqrt{4\pi}}

(652)

The
a,a^{\dagger}
operators are unchanged and obey
[a,a^{\dagger}]=1
, and we verify that
[b,b^{\dagger}]=1
holds:

\displaystyle[b,b^{\dagger}]

\displaystyle=2i\frac{1}{4\pi}\frac{q}{p}[\mathbf{L}_{1}\cdot{\bf{Q}},\mathbf{L}_{2}\cdot{\bf{Q}}]=eB\Omega\frac{1}{2\pi}\frac{q}{p}=1

(653)

since
eB\Omega=\phi
. The translation operators
T_{\bf{R}}=e^{i{\bf{R}}\cdot{\bf{Q}}}
now obey

\displaystyle{\hat{t}}_{\mathbf{L}_{1}}{\hat{t}}_{\mathbf{L}_{2}}=e^{-[\mathbf{L}_{1}\cdot{\bf{Q}},\mathbf{L}_{2}\cdot{\bf{Q}}]}{\hat{t}}_{\mathbf{L}_{2}}{\hat{t}}_{\mathbf{L}_{1}}=e^{i\phi}{\hat{t}}_{\mathbf{L}_{2}}{\hat{t}}_{\mathbf{L}_{1}}.

(654)

These magnetic translation operators commute with the Hamiltonian, but do not commute with each other. However, the operators
{\hat{t}}_{\mathbf{L}_{1}},{\hat{t}}_{q\mathbf{L}_{2}}
form a commuting subgroup,
[{\hat{t}}_{\mathbf{L}_{1}},{\hat{t}}_{q\mathbf{L}_{2}}]=0
and so label the magnetic Brillouin zone (MBZ):

\displaystyle{\bf{k}}\in\text{MBZ},\penalty\ \penalty\ \text{MBZ}=\{{\bf{k}}|{\bf{k}}=2\pi k_{1}\mathbf{g}_{1}+2\pi k_{2}\mathbf{g}_{2},\penalty\ \penalty\ k_{1}\in[0,1),\penalty\ \penalty\ k_{2}\in[0,1/q)\}.

(655)

We will now provide a complete basis of states which carry the momentum quantum number
\mathbf{k}\in
MBZ. This is accomplished by adapting the basis states in
2\pi
flux in Eq.(
643
). The completeness and orthogonality of the basis in Eq.(
643
) is an algebraic property that relies on the fact that
\hat{t}_{\mathbf{L}_{1}}
and
\hat{t}_{\mathbf{L}_{2}}
enclose
2\pi
flux and therefore commute. So in rational flux
\phi=\frac{2\pi p}{q}
, we consider the operators
\hat{t}_{\mathbf{a}_{1}/p},\hat{t}_{q\mathbf{L}_{2}}
which also enclose
2\pi
flux and commute. Notably,
\hat{t}_{\mathbf{L}_{1}/p}
is not a symmetry of the Hamiltonian because it is a translation by a
partial
lattice vector (although it is a well-defined operator). Thus we are led to consider the orthonormal states

\displaystyle\ket{\mbf{\tilde{k}},m}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{{\cal N}(\tilde{k}_{1},\tilde{k}_{2})}}\sum_{R_{1},R_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}}e^{-2\pi i(\tilde{k}_{1}R_{1}+\tilde{k}_{2}R_{2})}{\hat{t}}_{\mathbf{L}_{1}/p}^{R_{1}}{\hat{t}}_{q\mathbf{L}_{2}}^{R_{2}}\ket{m},\quad\ket{m}=\dfrac{1}{\sqrt{m!}}a^{\dagger m}\ket{0}

(656)

which are parameterized by
\mathbf{\tilde{k}}\in[0,1)\times[0,1)
. To understand the physical meaning of
\mathbf{\tilde{k}}
in terms of the momentum quantum number
\mathbf{k}\in\text{MBZ}
, we compute

\displaystyle{\hat{t}}_{\mathbf{a}_{1}}\ket{\mbf{\tilde{k}},m}

\displaystyle={\hat{t}}_{\mathbf{a}_{1}/p}^{p}\ket{\mbf{\tilde{k}},m}=e^{2\pi ip\tilde{k}_{1}}\ket{\mbf{\tilde{k}},m},\qquad\implies p\tilde{k}_{1}=k_{1}\mod 1

(657)

\displaystyle{\hat{t}}^{q}_{\mathbf{a}_{2}}\ket{\mbf{\tilde{k}},m}

\displaystyle=e^{2\pi i\tilde{k}_{2}}\ket{\mbf{\tilde{k}},m},\qquad\implies\tilde{k}_{2}=qk_{2}\mod 1

(658)

and thus we identify
\tilde{k}_{1}=\frac{k_{1}+\kappa}{p},\ \kappa=0,\dots,p-1
and
\tilde{k}_{2}=qk_{2}
. Plugging into Eq.(
656
), we arrive at the following basis of magnetic translation group eigenstates

\displaystyle\ket{\mbf{k},\kappa,m}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{{\cal N}((k_{1}+\kappa)/p,qk_{2})}}\sum_{R_{1},R_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}}e^{-2\pi i\big(\frac{k_{1}+\kappa}{p}R_{1}+qk_{2}R_{2}\big)}{\hat{t}}_{\mathbf{L}_{1}/p}^{R_{1}}{\hat{t}}_{q\mathbf{L}_{2}}^{R_{2}}\ket{m},

(660)

\displaystyle\quad\mathbf{k}\in\text{MBZ},\ \kappa=0,\dots,p-1\mod p\ .

We see that the basis of states are labeled by
\mathbf{k}
in the MBZ and an additional flavor index
\kappa=0,\dots,p-1
. This index has a simple physical interpretation: consider the Landau level limit of zero crystalline potential so that each momentum in the MBZ is
p
-fold degenerate due to
\kappa
. This means there are
p
states per magnetic unit cell
\mathbf{a}_{1}\times q\mathbf{a}_{2}
, leading to an electron density of
p/q=\phi/2\pi
per unit cell. This is expected because Landau levels have a density of
\nu=p/q=\frac{\phi}{2\pi}
. For later reference, we remark that the eigenstates Eq.(
660
) obey the embedding property

\displaystyle\ket{\kk+ \frac{1}{2\pi} \bg_1, \kappa, m}=\ket{\kk,\kappa+1, m},\penalty\ \penalty\ \ket{\kk+ \frac{1}{2\pi q} \bg_2, \kappa, m}=\ket{\kk,\kappa, m}

(661)

over the MBZ. These eigenstates are similar to the light fermions used to describe the conduction electrons in flux, Eq.(
32
). The orthonormality
\langle{\mathbf{\tilde{k}},m|\mathbf{\tilde{k}}^{\prime},n}\rangle=\delta(\mathbf{\tilde{k}}-\mathbf{\tilde{k}}^{\prime})\delta_{mn}
of the states Eq.(
656
) is used to calculate the orthonormality of the new states
\ket{\kk,\kappa, m}
by employing the previously derived identification

\displaystyle\tilde{k}_{1}

\displaystyle=\frac{k_{1}+\kappa}{p},\ \kappa=0,\dots,p-1,\penalty\ \penalty\ \tilde{k}_{2}=qk_{2},

(662)

\displaystyle\delta(\tilde{k}_{1}-\tilde{k}_{1}^{\prime})\delta(\tilde{k}_{2}-\tilde{k}_{2}^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\delta(\frac{k_{1}+\kappa}{p}-\frac{k_{1}^{\prime}+\kappa^{\prime}}{p})\delta(qk_{2}-qk_{2}^{\prime})=\frac{p}{q}\delta(k_{1}-k^{\prime}_{1})\delta(k_{2}-k_{2}^{\prime})\delta_{\kappa\kappa^{\prime}},

(663)

giving overlap

\displaystyle\langle{\mathbf{k},\kappa,m|\mathbf{k}^{\prime},\kappa^{\prime},n}\rangle

\displaystyle=\delta((k_{1}-k^{\prime}_{1})/p)\delta_{\kappa\kappa^{\prime}}\delta(q(k_{2}-k_{2}^{\prime}))\delta_{mn}=\frac{p}{q}\delta(k_{1}-k^{\prime}_{1})\delta(k_{2}-k_{2}^{\prime})\delta_{\kappa\kappa^{\prime}}\delta_{mn}

(664)

where the factor of
p/q=\phi/2\pi
shows that the density of the states changes with flux. This dependence of density of states on flux is equivalent to the Streda formula
\nu=C\frac{\phi}{2\pi}
with
C=1
for the Landau level state
\ket{\mbf{k},\kappa,m}
.

Given a periodic Hamiltonian in magnetic flux,

\displaystyle H=h({\bm{\pi}})+V(\mathbf{r}),\qquad V(\mathbf{r}+\mathbf{a}_{i})=V(\mathbf{r})

(665)

where
h
is the kinetic energy, a function of canonical momentum, and
V
is the lattice-periodic potential energy, one can compute the matrix elements of this Hamiltonian
H^{\phi}_{m\kappa,n\kappa^{\prime}}(\mathbf{k})
in the basis Eq.(
660
):

\displaystyle\langle{{\bf{k}},\kappa,m|H({\bf{r}})|{\bf{k}}^{\prime},\kappa^{\prime},n}\rangle

\displaystyle=\dfrac{p}{q}\delta(k_{1}-k_{1}^{\prime})\delta(k_{2}-k_{2}^{\prime})H^{\phi}_{m\kappa,n\kappa^{\prime}}(\mathbf{k}).

(666)

The kinetic term
h(\boldsymbol{\pi})
has a simple action on the
\ket{\mbf{k},\kappa,n}
states because it is
\mathbf{k}
-independent:

\displaystyle h({\bm{\pi}})

\displaystyle=\hbar v_{F}\sqrt{2B}\begin{bmatrix}0&a^{\dagger}\\
a&0\end{bmatrix}

(667)

\displaystyle\langle{{\bf{k}}^{\prime},\kappa^{\prime},m|a^{\dagger}|{\bf{k}},\kappa,n}\rangle

\displaystyle=\frac{p}{q}\delta(k_{1}-k^{\prime}_{1})\delta(k_{2}-k_{2}^{\prime})\ \sqrt{n+1}\delta_{\kappa\kappa^{\prime}}\delta_{mn},

(668)

calculated by observing that
a^{\dagger}
commutes with the magnetic translation operators:

\displaystyle a^{\dagger}\ket{\mbf{k},\kappa,m}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{{\cal N}((k_{1}+2\pi\kappa)/p,qk_{2})}}\sum_{R_{1},R_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}}e^{-i\big(\frac{k_{1}+2\pi\kappa}{p}R_{1}+qk_{2}R_{2}\big)}T_{\mathbf{L}_{1}/p}^{R_{1}}T_{q\mathbf{L}_{2}}^{R_{2}}a^{\dagger}\ket{m},

(669)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{{\cal N}((k_{1}+2\pi\kappa)/p,qk_{2})}}\sum_{R_{1},R_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}}e^{-i\big(\frac{k_{1}+2\pi\kappa}{p}R_{1}+qk_{2}R_{2}\big)}T_{\mathbf{L}_{1}/p}^{R_{1}}T_{q\mathbf{L}_{2}}^{R_{2}}\sqrt{m+1}\ket{m+1}

(670)

\displaystyle=\sqrt{m+1}\ket{\mbf{k},\kappa,m+1}.

(671)

We now move on to the an explicit expression for the potential term
V(\mathbf{r})
containing
e^{2\pi i\mathbf{b}\cdot\mathbf{r}}
operators. Let us first focus on the case where
p=1
, where many of the formulae simplify greatly. We recall the formula at
\phi=2\pi

Herzog-Arbeitman et al. 2022a
:

\displaystyle\langle{\mathbf{k},m|e^{-2\pi i\mathbf{b}\cdot\mathbf{r}}|\mathbf{k}^{\prime},n}\rangle

\displaystyle=(2\pi)^{2}\delta(\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{k}^{\prime})\exp{\lp- i \pi b_1 b_2 - i \epsilon_{ij} b_i k_j \rp}[\exp( i \epsilon_{ij} 2\pi b_i \tilde{Z_j})]_{mn},

(672)

where the matrix
\tilde{Z}
on the Landau level indices (derived for all
\phi
in
Herzog-Arbeitman et al. 2022a
) is

\displaystyle\hbox{}Z_{j}=\frac{\bar{z}_{j}a+z_{j}a^{\dagger}}{\sqrt{2\phi}},\penalty\ \penalty\ [\tilde{Z}_{j}]_{mn}=\langle{m|Z_{j}|n}\rangle,z_{j}=\frac{(\hat{x}+i\hat{y})\cdot\mathbf{a}_{j}}{|\mathbf{a}_{1}\times\mathbf{a}_{2}|}\ .

(673)

Note that
\tilde{Z}_{j}
is a sparse matrix since
a,a^{\dagger}
are sparse (each row has only one nonzero element). At
\phi=\frac{2\pi}{q}
, we take
\mathbf{a}_{2}\to q\mathbf{a}_{2}
in Eq.(
672
), again employing our identification
k_{1}\rightarrow k_{1},k_{2}\rightarrow qk_{2},G_{1}\rightarrow G_{1},G_{2}\rightarrow qG_{2}
to obtain

\displaystyle\langle{\mathbf{k},m|e^{-i\mathbf{G}\cdot\mathbf{r}}|\mathbf{k}^{\prime},n}\rangle

\displaystyle=\frac{(2\pi)^{2}}{q}\delta(k_{1}-k_{1}^{\prime})\delta(k_{2}-k_{2}^{\prime})\exp{\lp- i \frac{q}{2\pi} ( \frac{1}{2} G_1 G_2 +\epsilon_{ij} G_i k_j) \rp}[e^{i\epsilon_{ij}G_{i}\tilde{Z}_{j}}]_{mn},

(674)

\displaystyle k\in\text{MBZ},\ \mathbf{G}\cdot\mathbf{a}_{i}=0\mod 2\pi

\displaystyle=(2\pi)^{2}\frac{\phi}{2\pi}\delta(k_{1}-k_{1}^{\prime})\delta(k_{2}-k_{2}^{\prime})\exp\left(-\frac{i}{\phi}(\frac{1}{2}G_{1}G_{2}+\epsilon_{ij}G_{i}k_{j})\right)[e^{i\epsilon_{ij}G_{i}\tilde{Z_{j}}}]_{mn}

(675)

Note that from Eq.(
674
),
H^{\phi}(k_{1},k_{2})=H^{\phi}(k_{1}+1,k_{2})=H^{\phi}(k_{1},k_{2}+1/q)
is explicitly periodic across the magnetic BZ, and additionally
H^{\phi}(k_{1},k_{2})=H^{\phi}(k_{1}+1/q,k_{2})
which proves immediately that
\epsilon_{n}(\mathbf{k})=\epsilon_{n}(\mathbf{k}+\frac{1}{q}\mathbf{b}_{i})
as follows from the properties of the magnetic translation group
Zak 1964a
;
Zak 1964b
.

The simple form for the potential for
p=1
becomes significantly more complex when
p\neq 1
. One replaces the formula Eq.(
648
) as
\mathbf{L}_{1}\rightarrow\mathbf{L}_{1}/p,\mathbf{L}_{2}\rightarrow q\mathbf{L}_{2}
, to yield the overlap

\displaystyle\langle{{\bf{k}}^{\prime},\kappa^{\prime},m|e^{-i{\bf{q}}\cdot{\bf{r}}}|{\bf{k}},\kappa,n}\rangle

\displaystyle=\frac{p}{q}\delta(k_{1}-k_{1}^{\prime}-q_{1}/2\pi+\kappa-\kappa^{\prime})\delta(k_{2}-k_{2}^{\prime}-q_{2}/2\pi)

\displaystyle\times e^{i\xi_{q_{1}/p+iqq_{2}}((k_{1}+\kappa)/p,qk_{2})}{\exp(i \epsilon_{ij}q_i {\tilde Z}_j)}_{mn}.

(676)

where the phase factor is

\displaystyle e^{i\xi_{q_{1}/p+iqq_{2}}((k_{1}+\kappa)/p,qk_{2})}=\dfrac{e^{-\frac{\tilde{q}\bar{{\bf q}}}{8\pi}}\vartheta\left((k_{1}+\kappa)/p-\tilde{q}/4\pi,qk_{2}+i\tilde{q}/4\pi\bigg|\Phi\right)}{\sqrt{\vartheta\left((k_{1}+\kappa)/p,qk_{2}\bigg|\Phi\right)\vartheta\left((k_{1}+\kappa-q_{1}/2\pi)/p,q(k_{2}-q_{2}/2\pi)\bigg|\Phi\right)}},\penalty\ {\tilde{q}}=q_{1}/p+iqq_{2}.

(677)

In practice, we will not need to full expression for the phase in Eq.(
676
). To evaluate the Hamiltonian, we only need an expression for the overlap at
\mathbf{q}=\mathbf{G}
, a reciprocal lattice vector. In this case, the expression simplifies to

\displaystyle\langle{{\bf{k}}^{\prime},\kappa^{\prime},m|e^{-i\mathbf{G}\cdot{\bf{r}}}|{\bf{k}},\kappa,n}\rangle

\displaystyle=\frac{p}{q}\delta(k_{1}-k_{1}^{\prime})\delta_{\kappa,\kappa^{\prime}+G_{1}}\delta(k_{2}-k_{2}^{\prime})

(678)

\displaystyle\times e^{i\xi_{G_{1}/p+iqG_{2}}((k_{1}+\kappa)/p,qk_{2})}{\exp(i \epsilon_{ij}G_i {\tilde Z}_j)}_{mn}

Supplementary Figure 19:
Band structures of the BM model at flux
\phi=2\pi/3
, calculated with our momentum eigenstates.
Left
. The Hofstadter bands show significant flattening over the MBZ, leading to pronounced peaks in the density of states (
Center
).
Right
. Zoom-in of the Hofstadter splitting of the flat bands.

To calculate the matrix elements of the BM model, we first perform a gauge transformation to recast the Hamiltonian into an explicitly translation-symmetric form. Because
{\bf{q}}_{1},{\bf{q}}_{2},{\bf{q}}_{3}
are not reciprocal lattice vectors, the BM model is only periodic up to a gauge transformation. Conjugating
H_{\text{BM}}
by
e^{-i\pi{\bf{q}}_{3}\cdot{\bf{r}}}
restores translation symmetry, giving Hamiltonian

\displaystyle{\tilde{H}}_{\text{BM}}

\displaystyle=e^{\pi i{\bf{q}}_{3}\cdot{\bf{r}}}H_{\text{BM}}e^{-\pi i{\bf{q}}_{3}\cdot{\bf{r}}}=\begin{bmatrix}-i\hbar v_{F}\boldsymbol{\sigma}\cdot\boldsymbol{\nabla}-\pi\hbar v_{F}{\bf{q}}_{3}\cdot\boldsymbol{\sigma}&{\tilde{T}}^{\dagger}({\bf{r}})\\
{\tilde{T}}({\bf{r}})&-i\hbar v_{F}\boldsymbol{\sigma}\cdot\boldsymbol{\nabla}+\pi\hbar v_{F}{\bf{q}}_{3}\cdot\boldsymbol{\sigma}\end{bmatrix},\penalty\ \penalty\

(679)

\displaystyle{\tilde{T}}({\bf{r}})

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{3}e^{2\pi i\mathbf{g}_{1}\cdot{\bf{r}}}T_{1}+e^{2\pi i\mathbf{g}_{2}\cdot{\bf{r}}}T_{2}+T_{3},\penalty\ \penalty\ T_{j+1}=w_{0}\sigma_{0}+w_{1}\left(\sigma_{1}\cos\dfrac{2\pi j}{3}+\sigma_{2}\sin\dfrac{2\pi j}{3}\right).

(680)

. Upon canonical substitution, the Hamiltonian in flux reads

\displaystyle{\tilde{H}}_{\text{BM}}(\phi)

\displaystyle=\begin{bmatrix}\hbar v_{F}\boldsymbol{\sigma}\cdot{\bm{\pi}}-\pi\hbar v_{F}{\bf{q}}_{3}\cdot\boldsymbol{\sigma}&{\tilde{T}}^{\dagger}({\bf{r}})\\
{\tilde{T}}({\bf{r}})&\hbar v_{F}\boldsymbol{\sigma}\cdot{\bm{\pi}}+\pi\hbar v_{F}{\bf{q}}_{3}\cdot\boldsymbol{\sigma}\end{bmatrix},

(681)

An example band structure at flux
\phi=2\pi/3
calculated with our technique is depicted in Supplementary Fig.(
19
).

Supplementary note 12.1
Groundstate Charge Density

A key feature of the HF model of TBG is the concentration of charge at the moiré unit cell center in the non-interacting model. This is one indication that the Wannierization must contain orbitals at the
1a
position. To check the validity of the HF approximation in small flux, we will compute the charge density of the groundstate in flux
\phi=\frac{2\pi}{q}
, for this section restricting to
p=1
for convenience. We will compute the quantity

\displaystyle\langle{GS|n(\mathbf{r})|GS}\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{l,\alpha,\eta,s}\langle{GS|c^{\dagger}_{l,\alpha,\eta,s}(\mathbf{r})c_{l,\alpha,\eta,s}(\mathbf{r})|GS}\rangle

(682)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{q\Omega}\sum_{\mathbf{G}_{q}}\int_{q_{1}=0}^{2\pi}\int_{q_{2}=0}^{2\pi/q}\frac{d^{2}q}{(2\pi)^{2}/q}e^{i(\mathbf{q}+\mathbf{G}_{q})\cdot\mathbf{r}}\langle{GS|\rho_{\mathbf{q}+\mathbf{G}_{q}}|GS}\rangle

(683)

where
\ket{GS}=\prod_{j}\prod_{\mathbf{k},n,\eta_{j},s_{j}}\gamma^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{k},n,\eta_{j},s_{j}}\ket{0}
is the Slater determinant state filling all the
2q
Hofstadter flat bands and
n(\mathbf{r})=\sum_{l,\alpha,\eta,s}c^{\dagger}_{l,\alpha,\eta,s}(\mathbf{r})c_{l,\alpha,\eta,s}(\mathbf{r})
is the local charge operator summed over layer
l
, sublattice
\alpha
, valley
\eta
, and spin
s
. The vectors
{\bf{G}}_{q}=2\pi\mathbb{Z}\mathbf{g}_{1}+2\pi\mathbb{Z}\mathbf{g}_{2}/q
are the reciprocal lattice vectors of the MBZ (note that
\mathbf{G}_{q}\cdot\mathbf{a}_{2}
can be fractional). The momentum space density operator
\rho
is defined as

\displaystyle\rho_{\mathbf{q}+{\bf{G}}_{q}}=\int d^{2}re^{-i({\bf{q}}+{\bf{G}}_{q})\cdot{\bf{r}}}\sum_{l,\alpha,\eta,s}c^{\dagger}_{l,\alpha,\eta,s}({\bf{r}})c_{l,\alpha,\eta,s}({\bf{r}}).

(684)

We first find the matrix elements of this operator in terms of Landau levels. Define the creation operator
\psi^{\dagger}_{{\bf{k}},m,\alpha,l,\eta,s}
as the operator that creates the state
\ket{\kk,m,\alpha,l,\eta,s}
. Following the calculations from Ref.
Herzog-Arbeitman et al. 2022a
gives the following relations:

\displaystyle\langle{{\bf{k}}^{\prime},\alpha,l,m,\eta,s|c^{\dagger}_{l,\alpha,\eta,s}({\bf{r}})c_{l,\alpha,\eta,s}({\bf{r}})|{\bf{k}},\alpha,l,n,\eta,s}\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\langle{0|\psi_{{\bf{k}}^{\prime},\alpha,l,m,\eta,s}c^{\dagger}_{l,\alpha,\eta,s}({\bf{r}})c_{l,\alpha,\eta,s}({\bf{r}})\psi^{\dagger}_{{\bf{k}},\alpha,l,n,\eta,s}|0}\rangle

(685)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\langle{0|\psi_{{\bf{k}}^{\prime},\alpha,l,m,\eta,s}c^{\dagger}_{l,\alpha,\eta,s}({\bf{r}})|0}\rangle\langle{0|c_{l,\alpha,\eta,s}({\bf{r}})\psi^{\dagger}_{{\bf{k}},\alpha,l,n,\eta,s}|0}\rangle

(686)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\psi_{{\bf{k}}^{\prime},\alpha,l,m,\eta,s}({\bf{r}})\psi^{*}_{{\bf{k}},\alpha,l,n,\eta,s}({\bf{r}}),

(687)

where
\psi_{\mathbf{k}^{\prime},\alpha,l,m,\eta,s}(\mathbf{r})
is the real-space wavefunction corresponding to the momentum ket
\ket{\bk',\alpha,l,m,\eta,s}
. These wavefunctions are orthonormal, and integrating over
\mathbf{r}
gives

\displaystyle\int d^{2}re^{-i{\bf{q}}\cdot{\bf{r}}}\psi_{{\bf{k}}^{\prime},\alpha,l,m,\eta,s}({\bf{r}})\psi^{*}_{{\bf{k}},\alpha,l,n,\eta,s}({\bf{r}})=\langle{{\bf{k}}^{\prime},\alpha,l,m,\eta,s|e^{-i{\bf{q}}\cdot{\bf{r}}}|{\bf{k}},\alpha,l,n,\eta,s}\rangle

(688)

The density operator becomes

\displaystyle\rho_{{\bf{q}}+{\bf{G}}_{q}}

\displaystyle=\int d^{2}r\sum_{\alpha,l,\eta,s}\sum_{mn}\int\dfrac{d^{2}kd^{2}k^{\prime}}{1/q^{2}}e^{-i({\bf{q}}+{\bf{G}}_{q})\cdot{\bf{r}}}

\displaystyle\times\langle{{\bf{k}}^{\prime},\alpha,l,m,\eta,s|c^{\dagger}_{l,\alpha,\eta,s}({\bf{r}})c_{l,\alpha,\eta,s}({\bf{r}})|{\bf{k}},\alpha,l,n,\eta,s}\rangle\psi^{\dagger}_{{\bf{k}}^{\prime},\alpha,l,m,\eta,s}\psi_{{\bf{k}},\alpha,l,n,\eta,s}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\alpha,l,\eta,s}\sum_{mn}\int\dfrac{d^{2}kd^{2}k^{\prime}}{1/q^{2}}\langle{{\bf{k}}^{\prime},m,\alpha,l,\eta,s|e^{-i({\bf{q}}+{\bf{G}}_{q})\cdot{\bf{r}}}|{\bf{k}},n,\alpha,l,\eta,s}\rangle\psi^{\dagger}_{{\bf{k}}^{\prime},\alpha,l,m,\eta,s}\psi_{{\bf{k}},\alpha,l,n,\eta,s},

(689)

where the form factor
\langle{{\bf{k}}^{\prime},m|e^{-i({\bf{q}}+{\bf{G}}_{q})\cdot{\bf{r}}}|{\bf{k}},n}\rangle
has been defined in Eq.(
676
). The factor of
1/q^{2}
in the integrand arises from the fact that working at
\phi=2\pi/q
, the Brillouin zone size has reduced by a factor of
q
.

We project into the flat bands, defining the eigenoperators
\gamma^{\dagger}_{{\bf{k}},M,\eta,s}
as the operators that create the
M
th energy eigenstate at momentum
{\bf{k}}
, with
M
ranging from
1
to
2q
. To do this, expand the eigenoperators as

\displaystyle\gamma_{{\bf{k}},N,\eta,s}=\sum_{\alpha,l,m}[U^{(\eta,s)}]^{N}_{\alpha,l,m}({\bf{k}})\psi_{{\bf{k}},\alpha,l,m,\eta,s},

(690)

so upon projection

\displaystyle\psi^{\dagger}_{{\bf{k}}^{\prime},\alpha,l,m,\eta,s}\psi_{{\bf{k}},\alpha,l,n,\eta,s}\rightarrow\sum_{MN}[U^{(\eta,s)*}]^{M}_{\alpha,l,m}({\bf{k}}^{\prime})[U^{(\eta,s)}]^{N}_{\alpha,l,n}({\bf{k}})\gamma^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{k}^{\prime},M,\eta,s}\gamma_{\mathbf{k},N,\eta,s}

(691)

The projected form factors read

\displaystyle\rho_{\mathbf{q}}\rightarrow\rho_{\mathbf{q}+\mathbf{G}_{q}}=\int_{[0,1)\times[0,1/q)}\frac{d^{2}k}{1/q}\sum_{MN}M_{mn}^{(\eta,s)}(\mathbf{k},\mathbf{q}+\mathbf{G}_{q})\gamma^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{q}/2\pi,M,\eta_{j},s_{j}}\gamma_{\mathbf{k},N,\eta_{j},s_{j}},

(692)

where
M^{(\eta,s)}(\mathbf{k},\mathbf{q})
are the form factor matrices given by

\displaystyle M^{(\eta,s)}_{MN}({\bf{k}},{\bf{q}}+{\bf{G}}_{q})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\alpha,l,\eta,s,mn}e^{i\xi_{{\bf{q}}+{\bf{G}}_{\bf{q}}}({\bf{k}})}[{U^{(\eta,s)}}^{\dagger}_{\alpha,l,n}(\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{q}){\cal H}^{{\bf{q}}+{\bf{G}}_{q}}_{mn}U^{(\eta,s)}_{\alpha,l,n}({\bf{k}})]_{MN},

(693)

\displaystyle\dfrac{1}{q}\delta(k_{1}-k_{1}^{\prime})\delta(k_{2}-k_{2}^{\prime})e^{i\xi_{{\bf{q}}+{\bf{G}}_{\bf{q}}}({\bf{k}})}{\cal H}^{{\bf{q}}+{\bf{G}}_{q}}_{mn}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\langle{{\bf{k}}^{\prime},m|e^{-i({\bf{q}}+{\bf{G}}_{q})\cdot{\bf{r}}}|{\bf{k}},n}\rangle

(694)

with
[U^{(\eta,s)}]^{N}_{\alpha,l,m}(\mathbf{k})
the wavefunction of the
N
th energy band in the
m
th Landau level, with repeated indices summed. We have suppressed the indices
\alpha,l,\eta,s
in the ket
\ket{\kk,m}
as they are unchanged by the operator
e^{-i{\bf{q}}\cdot{\bf{r}}}
.

When computing many-body overlaps, it is more convenient to discretize the magnetic BZ and work with Kronecker delta normalized fermion operators instead of Dirac delta normalized operators. To do so, we discretize according to the normalization

\displaystyle\{\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\gamma_{\mathbf{k},m,\eta_{j},s_{j}},\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\gamma^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{k}^{\prime},n,\eta_{j^{\prime}},s_{j^{\prime}}}\}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N}\frac{1}{q}\delta(\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{k}^{\prime})\delta_{mn}\delta_{jj^{\prime}}\to\delta_{\mathbf{k}\mathbf{k}^{\prime}}\delta_{mn}\delta_{jj^{\prime}}

(695)

where
N\to\delta^{2}(0)
is the number of unit cells. Equivalently,
N
is the number of magnetic unit cells which is equal to the number of
k
-points in the magnetic BZ. Working in momentum space, we discretize the momentum

\displaystyle\rho_{\mathbf{q}+\mathbf{G}_{q}}

\displaystyle=\int_{[0,1)\times[0,1/q)}\frac{d^{2}k}{1/q}\sum_{MN}M_{MN}^{(\eta,s)}(\mathbf{k},\mathbf{q}/2\pi+\mathbf{G}_{q})\gamma^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{q}/2\pi,M,\eta_{j},s_{j}}\gamma_{\mathbf{k},N,\eta_{j},s_{j}}

(696)

\displaystyle\to\sum_{\mathbf{k}MN}M_{MN}^{(\eta,s)}(\mathbf{k},\mathbf{q}/2\pi+\mathbf{G}_{q})\gamma^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{q}/2\pi,M,\eta_{j},s_{j}}\gamma_{\mathbf{k},N,\eta_{j},s_{j}}

where
M,N
sum over all the Hofstadter bands and the arrow indicates a discretization of the the magnetic BZ.

Supplementary Figure 20:
Real space cuts along the
\mathbf{L}_{1}
lattice vector. All fluxes have many-body charge densities that are peaked at the
1a
location, but the
0
flux is the most peaked while the
2\pi
is the most spread. As flux is increased, the wavefunction weight is increasingly spread, but remains strongly localized at
1a
. This validates the use of the HF Hamiltonian in flux.

Acting on the groundstate, we have

\displaystyle\bra{GS}\rho_{\mathbf{q}+\mathbf{G}_{q}}\ket{GS}

\displaystyle=\delta_{\mathbf{q},0}\bra{GS}\rho_{\mathbf{G}_{q}}\ket{GS}=\delta_{\mathbf{q},0}\sum_{\mathbf{k}}\Tr M(\mathbf{k},\mathbf{G}_{q})

(697)

\displaystyle\bra{GS}\rho_{\mathbf{q}+\mathbf{G}_{q}}\ket{GS}

\displaystyle=\frac{(2\pi)^{2}}{q}\delta(q_{1})\delta(q_{2})\int_{[0,1)\times[0,1/q)}\frac{d^{2}k}{1/q}\Tr M(\mathbf{k},\mathbf{G}_{q})

(698)

switching back to the continuum normalization in the second line. As
{\bf{q}}
ranges over the MBZ, the continuum delta function
\delta(q_{1})\delta(q_{2})
appears with a prefactor of
(2\pi)^{2}/q
, so that the following integral over the MBZ is normalized to
1
:

\displaystyle\int_{[0,2\pi)\times[0,2\pi/q)}\dfrac{d^{2}q}{(2\pi)^{2}/q}\frac{(2\pi)^{2}}{q}\delta(q_{1})\delta(q_{2})=1.

(699)

We now prove that only
\bra{GS}\rho_{\mathbf{G}_{q}}\ket{GS}
is nonzero, for
\mathbf{G}_{q}
a reciprocal lattice vector of the zero-flux (non-magnetic) BZ: that is,
\bra{GS}\rho_{\mathbf{G}_{q}}\ket{GS}
is nonzero only if
{\bf{G}}_{q}=2\pi{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbf{g}_{1}+2\pi\mathbb{Z}\mathbf{g}_{2}
. This follows from the periodicity of the form factors:

\displaystyle M_{mn}(\mathbf{k}+\mathbf{b}_{1}/q,\mathbf{G}_{q})

\displaystyle=e^{i\mathbf{G}_{q}\cdot\mathbf{a}_{2}}M_{mn}(\mathbf{k},\mathbf{G}_{q})

(700)

which is derived from the form factor definition:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{q}\delta(k_{1}-k_{1}^{\prime})\delta(k_{2}-k_{2}^{\prime})e^{i\xi_{{\bf{G}}_{q}}({\bf{k}}+\mathbf{g}_{1}/2\pi q)}{\cal H}^{{\bf{G}}_{q}}_{mn}=\langle{\mathbf{k}+\mathbf{g}_{1}/2\pi q,m|e^{-2\pi i\mathbf{G}_{q}\cdot\mathbf{r}}|\mathbf{k}^{\prime}+\mathbf{g}_{1}/2\pi q,n}\rangle

(701)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{q}\delta(k_{1}-k_{1}^{\prime})\delta(k_{2}-k_{2}^{\prime})e^{-iq(\pi(G_{q})_{1}(G_{q})_{2}+\epsilon_{ij}(G_{q})_{i}k_{j}-2\pi(G_{q})_{2}/q)}[e^{i\epsilon_{ij}2\pi(G_{q})_{i}{\tilde{Z}}_{j}}]_{mn}

(702)

\displaystyle=e^{2\pi i{\bf{G}}_{q}\cdot\mathbf{L}_{2}}\langle{\mathbf{k},m|e^{-2\pi i\mathbf{G}_{q}\cdot\mathbf{r}}|\mathbf{k}^{\prime},n}\rangle,

(703)

or

\displaystyle\langle{\mathbf{k}+\mathbf{g}_{1}/2\pi q,m|e^{-2\pi i\mathbf{G}_{q}\cdot\mathbf{r}}|\mathbf{k}^{\prime}+\mathbf{g}_{1}/2\pi q,n}\rangle=e^{2\pi i{\bf{G}}_{q}\cdot\mathbf{L}_{2}}\langle{\mathbf{k},m|e^{-2\pi i\mathbf{G}_{q}\cdot\mathbf{r}}|\mathbf{k}^{\prime},n}\rangle

(704)

so that

\displaystyle\int\frac{d^{2}k}{1/q}\Tr M(\mathbf{k},\mathbf{G}_{q})

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{1}\frac{dk_{1}}{2\pi}\int_{0}^{1/q}\frac{dk_{2}}{1/q}\Tr M(\mathbf{k},\mathbf{G}_{q})

(705)

\displaystyle=\left(\sum_{j=0}^{q-1}e^{ij\mathbf{G}\cdot\mathbf{a}_{2}}\right)\int_{0}^{1/q}dk_{1}\int_{0}^{1/q}\frac{dk_{2}}{1/q}\Tr M(\mathbf{k},\mathbf{G})

(706)

\displaystyle=\delta_{{\mathbf{G}}_{\bf{q}},\mathbf{G}}\int_{0}^{1/q}\frac{dk_{1}}{1/q}\int_{0}^{1/q}\frac{dk_{2}}{1/q}\Tr M(\mathbf{k},\mathbf{G}_{q})

(707)

because
\sum_{j=0}^{q-1}e^{ij\mathbf{G}_{q}\cdot\mathbf{a}_{2}}=q
if
\mathbf{G}_{q}\cdot\mathbf{L}_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}
, and otherwise vanishes: that is, only if
{\bf{G}}_{q}
is a reciprocal lattice vector. This observation allows significant speedup in numerical calculations, as
\mathbf{G}_{q}
vectors that are not reciprocal lattice vectors do not need to be calculated in the expression for the many-body charge density. The integrals in Eq.(
697
) will appear throughout the many-body calculations, so we find it convenient to define

\displaystyle n_{\mathbf{G}}

\displaystyle=\int_{[0,1)\times[0,1/q)}\frac{d^{2}k}{1/q}\frac{1}{q}\Tr M(\mathbf{k},\mathbf{G}),\qquad\mathbf{G}\in 2\pi\mathbf{b}_{1}\mathbb{Z}+2\pi\mathbf{b}_{2}\mathbb{Z}

(708)

which is the average of the form factor over the magnetic BZ and (the
2q
) occupied Hofstadter bands. Physically,
n_{\tilde{\mathbf{G}}}
are the Fourier modes of the charge distribution, which we see from plugging into Eq.(
682
):

\displaystyle\langle{GS|n(\mathbf{r})|GS}\rangle

\displaystyle=\sum_{\tilde{\mathbf{G}}}e^{i{{\mathbf{G}}}\cdot\mathbf{r}}\ \int\frac{d^{2}k}{(2\pi)^{2}\Omega/q}\frac{1}{q}\Tr M(\mathbf{k},{{\mathbf{G}}})=\sum_{{\mathbf{G}}}e^{i{{\mathbf{G}}}\cdot\mathbf{r}}\ \int\frac{d^{2}k}{\Omega/q}\frac{1}{q}\Tr M(\mathbf{k},{{\mathbf{G}}})=\frac{1}{\Omega}\sum_{{\mathbf{G}}}e^{i{{\mathbf{G}}}\cdot\mathbf{r}}n_{{\mathbf{G}}}\ .

(709)

By proving that
n_{\mathbf{G}}
vanishes if
\mathbf{G}
is not a reciprocal lattice vector, we have shown that the charge distribution at rational flux is periodic over the physical unit cell, not just over the (extended) magnetic unit cell. A figure illustrating the expectation value over a slice in real space along the
\mathbf{L}_{1}
direction is depicted in Supplementary Fig.(
20
). We see that even in the presence of flux, the charge density remains localized at the
1a
position, though the spread does increase slightly with flux.

Supplementary note 12.2
Many-body Charge Excitations

It was realized in
Wang and Vafek 2022
that the double commutator calculation could be extended to TBG in flux. In this appendix, we detail a variation of this calculation using our gauge invariant formalism
Herzog-Arbeitman et al. 2022a
;
Herzog-Arbeitman et al. 2022b
, which makes the derivation of the charge excitations very simple. First we recall some basic results from the strong coupling theory of TBG: the double-commutator technique of
Vafek and Kang 2020
;
Bernevig et al. 2021c
will be used extensively in this section, along with results from
Bernevig et al. 2021b
;
Herzog-Arbeitman et al. 2022a
. Starting with a filled-band groundstate ansatz
Kang and Vafek 2019
;
Bultinck et al. 2020b
;
Lian et al. 2021
at filling
\nu
(
\nu=0
is the charge neutrality point) given by

\displaystyle\ket{\Psi_\nu}=\prod_{\mathbf{k},n}\prod_{j=1}^{\nu+4}\gamma^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{k},n,\eta_{j},s_{j}}\ket{0}

(710)

in spin-valley flavors
\{s_{j}\},\{\eta_{j}\}
, we compute the excitations of the Coulomb Hamiltonian projected into
2q
flat bands (per spin-valley) defined by

\displaystyle H_{int}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\int\frac{d^{2}q}{(2\pi)^{2}}V(\mathbf{q})\bar{\rho}_{\mathbf{q}}\bar{\rho}_{-\mathbf{q}}=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{\mathbf{G},j=0}^{q-1}\int_{(0,2\pi)\times(0,2\pi/q)}\frac{d^{2}q}{(2\pi)^{2}}\frac{V(\mathbf{q}+\frac{2\pi j}{q}\mathbf{b}_{2}+\mathbf{G})}{\Omega}\bar{\rho}_{\mathbf{q}+\frac{2\pi j}{q}\mathbf{b}_{2}+\mathbf{G}}\bar{\rho}_{-(\mathbf{q}+\frac{2\pi j}{q}\mathbf{b}_{2}+\mathbf{G})}

(711)

\displaystyle\qquad\bar{\rho}_{\mathbf{q}}=\sum_{\mathbf{k},MN,\eta s}M^{(\eta,s)}_{MN}(\mathbf{k},\mathbf{q})(\gamma^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{q},M,\eta,s}\gamma_{\mathbf{k},N,\eta,s}-\frac{1}{2}\delta_{\mathbf{q},0}\delta_{MN}).

(712)

Working at
p=1
, the equation breaks up the momentum
{\bf{q}}
by restricting
{\bf{q}}
into a vector in the MBZ, then adding a multiple of
\mathbf{g}_{2}/q
, and a reciprocal lattice vector
{\bf{G}}
, so that the total momentum
{\bf{q}}+\frac{2\pi j}{q}\mathbf{g}_{2}+{\bf{G}}
.

At charge neutrality,
\nu=0
,
\ket{\Psi_\nu}
is a groundstate of
H_{int}
because
H_{int}
is positive semi-definite and

\displaystyle\bar{\rho}_{\mathbf{q}+\frac{2\pi}{q}j\mathbf{b}_{2}+\mathbf{G}}\ket{\Psi_\nu}=\frac{\nu}{2}\frac{(2\pi)^{2}}{q}\delta(q_{1})\delta(q_{2})\delta_{j,0}qn_{\mathbf{G}}\ket{\Psi_\nu}

(713)

and thus
H_{int}\ket{\Psi_{\nu=0}}=0
. This convention justifies the choice of the projected interaction Hamiltonian. More generally, we observe that

\displaystyle H_{int}\ket{\Psi_\nu}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{\mathbf{G},j=0}^{q-1}\int_{(0,2\pi)\times(0,2\pi/q)}\frac{d^{2}q}{(2\pi)^{2}}\frac{V(\mathbf{q}+\frac{2\pi j}{q}\mathbf{b}_{2}+\mathbf{G})}{\Omega}(\frac{\nu}{2})^{2}((2\pi)^{2}\delta(q_{1})\delta(q_{2}))^{2}\delta_{j,0}|n_{\mathbf{G}}|^{2}\ket{\Psi_\nu}

(714)

\displaystyle=(2\pi)^{2}\delta^{2}(0)\left(\frac{\nu^{2}}{8}\sum_{\mathbf{G}}\frac{V(\mathbf{G})}{\Omega}|n_{\mathbf{G}}|^{2}\right)\ket{\Psi_\nu}

(715)

where
(2\pi)^{2}\delta^{2}(0)
is the total number of unit cells. Thus the many-body energy density of
\ket{\Psi_\nu}
is given by the quantity in parentheses in Eq.(
714
).

Supplementary Figure 21:
Charge
+1
excitations above the
\nu=0
VP state in flux, calculated via the strong coupling technique.

A double-commutator calculation
Vafek and Kang 2020
;
Bernevig et al. 2021c
gives the charge
\pm 1
excitations in terms of the following
2q\times 2q
effective Hamiltonians (the
\phi=2\pi/q
dependence is suppressed in
M(\mathbf{k},\mathbf{q})
and
n_{\mathbf{G}}
):

\displaystyle R^{+}_{MN}(\mathbf{k})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{\mathbf{q},\mathbf{G}}\frac{1}{2}\frac{V(\mathbf{q}+\mathbf{G})}{\Omega}[M^{\dagger}(\mathbf{k},\mathbf{q}+\mathbf{G})M(\mathbf{k},\mathbf{q}+\mathbf{G})]_{MN}+\frac{\nu}{2}\sum_{\mathbf{G}}\frac{V(\mathbf{G})}{\Omega}[M(\mathbf{k},\mathbf{G})]_{MN}n^{*}_{\mathbf{G}}-\mu\delta_{MN}

(717)

\displaystyle R^{-}_{MN}(\mathbf{k})^{*}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{\mathbf{q},\mathbf{G}}\frac{1}{2}\frac{V(\mathbf{q}+\mathbf{G})}{\Omega}[M^{\dagger}(\mathbf{k},\mathbf{q}+\mathbf{G})M(\mathbf{k},\mathbf{q}+\mathbf{G})]_{MN}-\frac{\nu}{2}\sum_{\mathbf{G}}\frac{V(\mathbf{G})}{\Omega}[M(\mathbf{k},\mathbf{G})]_{MN}n^{*}_{\mathbf{G}}+\mu\delta_{MN}

(718)

where
\mathbf{q}
sums over the
N
states in the full Brillouin zone, and the chemical potential
\mu=(E_{min,+1}-E_{min,-1})/2
is chosen so that the minima of the charge
\pm 1
spectra at
\mu=0
, denoted
E_{min,+\pm}
, are the same. Physically, this choice of
\mu
corresponds to setting zero energy in the middle of the excitation gap. We refer to the first term in
R^{\pm}(\mathbf{k})
as the Fock term. Since
V(\mathbf{q})>0
and
M^{\dagger}M
is a positive-semi-definite matrix, the Fock term is positive semi-definite. It also does not depend on the filling
\nu
. Note that the phase
e^{i\xi_{\mathbf{q}}(\mathbf{k})}
in the form factors
M(\mathbf{k},\mathbf{q})
cancels in the
M^{\dagger}M
product, and so does not need to be evaluated. To further speed up the numerical calculations,
n_{\mathbf{G}}
can be precomputed since it does not depend on
\mathbf{k}
. Most importantly, the computation of
M(\mathbf{k},\mathbf{q})
can be carried out with sparse matrix multiplication of
e^{i\epsilon_{ij}q_{i}\tilde{Z}_{j}}
on
U(\mathbf{k})
. We find that even for very large
q
, the computations of
R^{\pm}(\mathbf{k})
are feasible taking the Landau level cutoff to be
30q
and truncating the
\mathbf{G}
sum after three shells. A plot of the many-body charge excitations calculated with strong coupling is shown in Supplementary Fig.(
21
); this is compared to the spectrum obtained via heavy fermions to excellent agreement in the main text.

Supplementary note 13
Notational Changes

•

The notation
I^{0}_{[m,r^{\prime}]}
is used in the supplementary to represent the

c
-
f
hybridization matrix (see Eq.(
152
)). In the main text, we use the notation
\Upsilon_{[m,r^{\prime}]}
for the same. It is defined in Eq.(13) of main-text.

•

The notation
m_{max}
is used in the supplementary to represent the upper cut-off on c-LL index. In the main text, we use the notation
m_{\star}
for the same.
